

## Processed decision inbox batch — 2026-08-02T10:05:00+0000 (Scribe fused LinearAttention merge)

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-inline-scan-default.md -->
### 2026-08-02: Inc-1b decode-inline is now the ONLY path (graph-property-gated); ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN removed
**By:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime performance)
**PR:** #592 (branch `squad/inline-scan-default`, base `origin/main` @ 05695ca6)

**What:** Deleted the `ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN` env flag. The Inc-1b decode-inline
sibling is now built + routed automatically, gated on the GRAPH PROPERTY (an inlineable
single-trip / extent==1 recurrent `Scan` that `inline_single_trip_scan_bodies` can lower),
not a user knob. Models with no such `Scan` build no sibling and stay byte-identical.

**Why:** Justin's directive — "maybe there doesn't need to be a flag right? just default to
the fastest path for users and make it the only way (if there is no tradeoffs)." Verified no
tradeoff (below), so the conditional is honored and the flag is removed.

**Mutation map (4 files):**
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/native_decode/mod.rs`
  - Deleted `fn decode_inline_scan_enabled()` (env reader).
  - `maybe_enable_decode_inline`: removed the `if !decode_inline_scan_enabled()` short-circuit;
    it now probes at the first single-token decode step and delegates to
    `session.enable_decode_inline()`. No-Scan -> `enable_decode_inline` returns `false` -> latches
    `Disabled` (no sibling). Doc + `DecodeInlineState` enum comments rewritten to graph-property wording.
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/native_decode/cuda.rs`
  - Three doc comments retargeted from "flag on/off" to "route_inline true iff inlineable single-trip Scan".
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/native_decode/tests.rs`
  - Deleted `decode_inline_flag_defaults_off_and_parses_truthy` (tested the removed fn).
  - Retitled the section header: gate is graph-property, covered by session executor tests.
  - Kept the 3 pure routing-decision tests (`route_decode_inline_decision`) unchanged.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/lib.rs`
  - `enable_decode_inline` doc: removed the "callers gate behind ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN"
    line; the session reads no env and the graph property is the only gate.

The gate itself (`Executor::build_decode_inline_sibling`) was ALREADY graph-property-based
(returns `Ok(None)` when `default_domain_scan_count` doesn't drop after the transform). The flag
was a redundant second gate in front of it; removing it changes nothing for no-Scan models.

**No-Scan -> no-sibling proof (unit):**
- `onnx-runtime-session` `executor::tests::decode_inline_sibling_none_for_dense_graph` — a dense
  (Relu-only) decoder yields `build_decode_inline_sibling() == None` (no sibling). PASS.
- `executor::tests::decode_inline_sibling_is_byte_exact_with_scan_and_preserves_state` and the
  fold-into-captured-graph byte-exact test — with-Scan -> sibling built + byte-exact. PASS.
- `onnx-genai-engine` `native_decode::tests::decode_inline_{routes_only_single_token_when_enabled,
  never_routes_when_decoder_has_eager_step_inputs, never_routes_when_disabled_or_unbuilt}` —
  `Untried`/`Disabled`/no-sibling never route. PASS (3/3).

**Byte-exact greedy on stock 27b, path AUTOMATIC (no env set), CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3:**
first 16 generated ids =
`[11751, 13, 271, 248068, 271, 248069, 271, 4639, 369, 4252, 13, 11751, 369, 279, 6511, 321]`
— exactly the CPU fp32 oracle sequence in the task. (profile_native release, greedy fast path.)

**27b decode ms/tok, automatic-on (CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3, release):**
- run1 71.754 / run2 70.770 / run3 71.017 ms/tok; steady median **71.017 ms/tok** (14.08 tok/s),
  prefill ~741 ms, decode_skip=8, runs=3, warmups=1.
- vs old pre-inline baseline 143.8 ms/tok -> **2.03x** retained (target ~70). Win preserved with the
  flag gone.

**Byte-exact vs CPU fp32 oracle E2E (`native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e::
stock_export_auto_derives_io_and_matches_cpu_oracle`, `--ignored --test-threads=1 --nocapture`,
CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=2, path automatic / no env):** **PASSED (1 passed, 0 failed; finished in
5537.72s).** Both sides byte-identical:
- `cuda tokens=[11751,13,271,248068,271,248069,271,4639,369,4252,13,11751,369,279,6511,321]` (20.38s, automatic inline path)
- `cpu  tokens=[11751,13,271,248068,271,248069,271,4639,369,4252,13,11751,369,279,6511,321]` (5516.62s, fp32 oracle)
Confirms the decode-inline plan is byte-exact against the CPU fp32 oracle on the real 27B with the
path AUTOMATIC (no env set).

**Tradeoff found:** NONE. Inline path is byte-exact and ~2x faster; dense/no-Scan models build no
sibling and are untouched. Removal is safe.

**Composition note (Cohaagen-13 / LinearAttention fused kernel, PR pending):** once the EP keeps
`LinearAttention` as an op, the 27B decode graph has no `Scan`, so `build_decode_inline_sibling`
returns `None` and this path goes dormant for the 27B (fused kernel handles it). Intended. This
change keeps inline-scan perf for GENUINE Scan/Loop control-flow models with no fused kernel. The
graph-property gate makes the two changes compose with no conflict.

**Verification:** `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy -p onnx-genai-engine
-p onnx-runtime-session --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; unit tests above PASS.

**Do NOT self-merge** — Harry reviews, then coordinator merges.

# Cohaagen — LinearAttention fused-dispatch lever — BUILD evidence

**PR:** #594 — `squad/linattn-fused-kernel` (base `main` @ 05695ca6)
**Scope note:** `.squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-linattn-fused-scope.md` (verdict BUILD, size L, bucket-(A)-only)
**Date:** 2026-08-02

## What shipped
Make the fused `LinearAttention` kernel dispatch on real models, and support both
the `com.microsoft` and the standard ONNX domain (onnx/onnx#7689).

Root cause: the stock qwen3.6-27b declares `com.microsoft::LinearAttention` as an
ONNX *function* whose body is a `Scan`; the loader inlines every model-local
function unconditionally, so all 48 calls expanded into Scans before dispatch and
the (registered, ORT-parity, capture-eligible) fused kernel was dead.

## Mutation map (bucket-(A)-only — no shared capture-team code touched)
Loader (Part 1 — general, claim-driven keep-as-op):
- `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/function_inline.rs`
  - `pub type KeepAsOp = dyn Fn(&Node, u64, &[DataType]) -> bool`
  - `inline_functions_filtered(model, keep_as_op)` + `inline_functions_impl`
  - `InlineFilter{ keep_as_op, value_types, opset_of }` + `should_keep`
  - helpers: `claim_view_node`, `scalar_attribute`, `tensor_elem_type`, `collect_value_types`
  - `filter` threaded through `inline_graph` / `expand_node` / `instantiate` /
    `inline_subgraph_attrs`; keep-as-op branch in `expand_node`.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/graph_builder.rs` — `build_graph(model, keep)`.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/lib.rs` — `load_model_bytes_with_weights_filtered`,
  `build_from_bytes_with_weights(.., keep)`, `build_graph(.., keep)`. Existing
  entries pass `None`.

Session (wire the predicate from the selected EP):
- `crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/lib.rs` — hoist EP selection above the load;
  build `keep_as_op = |node, opset, dtypes| ep.supports_op(node, opset, &[], dtypes, &[]).is_supported()`;
  call the filtered loader at both load sites; pass the hoisted `ep` to `from_parts`.

Part 2 — dual domain (reuse the same kernel):
- `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/mod.rs` — register `OpKey("LinearAttention","",1)`.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/provider.rs` — widen claim gate to
  `matches!(op.domain, "" | "ai.onnx" | "com.microsoft")`.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/mod.rs` — register `OpKey("LinearAttention","",1)`
  (cpu claims by registry only).
- `crates/onnx-runtime-shape-inference/src/handlers/custom_ops.rs` — register the
  `linear_attention` handler for the default domain.

Tests:
- `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/tests/function_inline.rs` — keep-as-op when claimed;
  inline when the predicate declines (dtype); still inline functions no kernel claims.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/mod.rs` — `registry_has_all_phase1_ops`
  count bumped 100 -> 101 for the added standard-domain LinearAttention entry.
- `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/tests/linear_attention_gpu.rs` — standard-domain
  parity + cross-domain byte-identity (gated/delta/gated_delta/gated/linear, GQA +
  inverse-GQA, f16/f32/bf16).
- `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/tests/qwen35_ort_parity.rs` — standard-domain == com.microsoft + ORT golden.
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e.rs` — golden-ids
  assertion + new 0-Scan structural lock (48 fused ops, 0 Scans).

## Default-path preservation (non-LinearAttention models unchanged)
- `inline_functions` (no predicate) is byte-identical to before; only the new
  `_filtered` path evaluates a predicate, and it is evaluated **only** on nodes
  that match a declared function. Every function a kernel does not claim inlines
  exactly as today. Proven by `filtered_still_inlines_functions_no_kernel_claims`
  and `filtered_inlines_when_predicate_declines_dtype`.
- Unknown input dtype -> `DataType::Undefined` -> the EP claim gate declines ->
  safe fallback to inlining.

## Structural gate (Part 1) — PASS
`stock_export_keeps_linear_attention_as_fused_op_with_zero_scans`:
```
loaded 27B plan: LinearAttention fused ops=48, Scan ops=0
test ... ok
```

## Parity tests — PASS
ep-cuda (GPU 7, --test-threads=1):
```
linear_attention_both_domains_identical ... ok
linear_attention_standard_domain_parity ... ok
linear_attention_f32_parity / f16 / bf16 ... ok
linear_attention_state_carry_matches_chained ... ok
result: ok. 6 passed
```
ep-cpu:
```
linear_attention_standard_domain_matches_com_microsoft ... ok
linear_attention_matches_onnxruntime_1_26 ... ok
result: ok. 5 passed
```
loader:
```
filtered_keeps_function_as_op_when_predicate_claims_it ... ok
filtered_inlines_when_predicate_declines_dtype ... ok
filtered_still_inlines_functions_no_kernel_claims ... ok
result: ok. 31 passed
```

## Perf (fused path) — 27B decode ms/tok
Two-length subtraction (prefill cancels), GPU 5, warm:
```
short=1.658s(16 tok)  long=4.299s(80 tok)  => decode 41.27 ms/tok
```
- Fused path: ~41 ms/tok
- Inc-1b decode-inline (flag ON): 70.1 ms/tok
- Baseline (Scan-inlined decode): 143.8 ms/tok
- ORT: N/A — ORT-CUDA crashes on this model (internal stl_vector assertion), so
  there is no ORT reference for the 27B. Fused path also removes the Scan unroll
  from **prefill** (single fused launch per layer).

## Byte-exact oracle (correctness gate) — PASS
`native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e::stock_export_auto_derives_io_and_matches_cpu_oracle`
with the fused path (48 LinearAttention ops, 0 Scans):
```
cuda tokens=[11751,13,271,248068,271,248069,271,4639,369,4252,13,11751,369,279,6511,321] (20.383s)
cpu  tokens=[11751,13,271,248068,271,248069,271,4639,369,4252,13,11751,369,279,6511,321] (5516.625s)
test ... ok   (result: ok. 1 passed)
```
Native CUDA == native CPU fp32 oracle == known-good golden ids. The CPU oracle
leg is ~92 min for the 27B (expected; `#[ignore]`).

## Capture-eligibility
The fused `LinearAttention` kernel is a single launch per layer with no host-read
seam, so it is Supported under capture (unlike the inlined Scan body, which forced
eager seams). Confirmed by the kernel's existing capture registration; no
quarantine needed.

## Does this make Inc-1b redundant?
For LinearAttention models: yes — no Scan at all, fixes prefill, faster decode.
Inc-1b stays useful for generic single-trip Scans with no fused kernel.

## Follow-up (not this PR)
mobius could emit the standard-domain spelling directly (`functions/linear_attention.py`
cites onnx/onnx#7689). Noted only — no mobius changes here.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-linattn-fused-scope.md -->
### 2026-08-02: SCOPE — Fused LinearAttention dispatch (keep-as-op + dual-domain). Scope-first, no build yet in this section.

**By:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf)
**Status:** Traced against latest `origin/main` = `05695ca6` in a throwaway worktree (`wt-cohaagen-linattn`), with a proto-level probe of the real stock 27b `model.onnx`. Precedes the build.
**Verdict up front:** **Size L, bounded, LOW default-path risk → BUILD.** The keep-as-op skip is a general, claim-driven loader hook (default = inline everything, byte-identical to today); dual-domain is additive registration. Supersedes Inc-1b for LinearAttention models. Details + citations below.

---

## 0. Problem confirmed against the real model (proto probe)
Stock `qwen3.6-27b-int4-cuda/model.onnx`:
- Declares `com.microsoft::LinearAttention` as a **model-local FUNCTION** (inputs `[query,key,value,past_state,decay,beta]`, outputs `[output,present_state]`); its body has **1 `Scan`** plus Transpose(11)/Reshape(7)/Concat(4)/Shape(5)/Tile(2)/Unsqueeze(2)/Constant(7)/Mul/CastLike.
- **48** top-level `LinearAttention` call-nodes. Each node: `q/k/v` dtype = **f16**, `past_state` = f16, attrs `scale=0`, `q_num_heads=16`, `kv_num_heads=48` (**inverse GQA**, kv=3×q), **no `update_rule`** attr (defaults to `gated_delta`).
- The graph's `value_info` (**4164 entries**) carries dtypes/shapes for the LinearAttention I/O boundary tensors.

The loader inlines EVERY model-local function unconditionally (`function_inline.rs:258` `expand_node` -> `instantiate`, driven from `graph_builder.rs:52`), so all 48 calls expand into their Scan-bodies before dispatch. The fused `LinearAttention` kernel — registered + ORT-parity + capture-eligible in ep-cpu (`kernels/mod.rs:516`) and ep-cuda (`kernels/mod.rs:733`, claim gate `provider.rs:332`) — is therefore **dead on this model**. It IS exercised on the Qwen3.5-0.8b split model, which exports LinearAttention as a raw op (no function), confirmed by `qwen35_0_8b_placement_lock.rs`.

## 1. Central design question — where does the keep-as-op decision live?
Constraint: the loader (`onnx-runtime-loader`) inlines at **proto level, before IR build and before EP selection**, and must not depend on ep-cpu/ep-cuda. Two facts unlock a clean, DRY hook:
- (a) The claim `unsupported_reason` for LinearAttention needs only **node attributes + q/k/v dtypes** (`ep-cuda/kernels/linear_attention.rs:235`; no shapes) — and those dtypes are in `value_info` at proto level.
- (b) The EP already exposes a node-level claim gate: `ExecutionProvider::supports_op(op:&Node, opset, shapes, input_dtypes, layouts) -> KernelMatch` (`ep-api/provider.rs:395`). LinearAttention's gate ignores `shapes`/`layouts`, so it answers correctly with `&[]` placeholders.

**No shape-inference risk.** `onnx-runtime-ir` has NO per-op shape-inference pass (`shape.rs` is just `Shape`/`Dim` types); Values are seeded from the export's `value_info`. Keeping LinearAttention as an op leaves its I/O shapes intact from `value_info` (the 0.8b op-path is the standing precedent). So downstream resolution, KV binding, and capture are unaffected.

### Chosen design (general, claim-driven, default-preserving)
**Loader (Part 1 hook):** add a predicate-carrying variant
`load_model_bytes_with_weights_filtered(bytes, dir, keep_as_op: Option<&dyn Fn(&ir::Node, u64 /*opset*/, &[DataType]) -> bool>)`
threaded into `inline_functions` -> `expand_node`. When a node matches a declared function AND `keep_as_op(node, opset, input_dtypes)` returns true, **emit the node unchanged instead of instantiating**; else inline exactly as today. The loader converts the proto call-node to a lightweight `ir::Node` (reusing existing node conversion) and resolves `input_dtypes` from `value_info` -> graph inputs -> initializers; **if a dtype is unknown, treat as NOT claimed -> inline** (safe fallback, preserves behavior). Default `load_model*` passes `None` -> inline everything -> **byte-identical to current main**. This is the general "keep-as-op iff a kernel claims it, else inline" policy — not hardcoded to any op name.

**Session wiring:** select the EP **before** load (EP selection is device-only, `lib.rs:739`; today it runs at `lib.rs:693` after load — the move is safe: `fp16_decode`/`optimize_graph` don't need the EP). Build the closure `|node, opset, dtypes| matches!(ep.supports_op(node, opset, &[], dtypes, &[]), KernelMatch::Supported{..})` and pass it into the new loader variant. Non-`from_dir` load paths keep passing `None`.

**Why not a post-build re-fusion / IR-level inliner:** re-fusing an already-inlined Scan is fragile and there is no IR-level function inliner. Deciding at proto time (before inlining) with the EP claim is strictly simpler and reuses the existing `supports_op` gate (DRY).

## 2. Part 2 — dual-domain (com.microsoft + standard ONNX, onnx/onnx#7689)
The registry normalizes domains (`ep-api/registry.rs` `norm_domain`), collapsing `""`/`ai.onnx`, but `com.microsoft` is distinct. To serve BOTH with the SAME kernel:
- **ep-cuda** `kernels/mod.rs:733`: add `reg.register(OpKey::new("LinearAttention", "", 1), LinearAttentionFactory{..})` alongside the `com.microsoft` entry; **claim gate** `provider.rs:332`: widen `op.domain == "com.microsoft"` to also match the default domain (`op.domain.is_empty() || op.domain == "ai.onnx"`) so `unsupported_reason` runs for both.
- **ep-cpu** `kernels/mod.rs:516`: add the `OpKey::new("LinearAttention","",1)` registration. CPU claims purely by registry (`provider.rs:128`, no per-config gate), so registration alone suffices.
- **Schema reconciliation:** the mobius header (`functions/linear_attention.py`) cites onnx/onnx#7689 as "same naming convention" — attrs `q_num_heads`, `kv_num_heads`, `scale`, `update_rule` and the `[q,k,v,past_state,decay,beta]`/`[output,present_state]` I/O are identical across domains; `scale=0` resolves to `1/sqrt(d_k)` in both. **One kernel serves both**; no attribute/opset divergence found. (If a future standard-schema revision renames an attr, reconcile in the claim/parse; none needed today.)

## 3. Blast radius / files
- **A (this PR, additive, flag-free but default-preserving):**
  - `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/function_inline.rs` — predicate-aware skip in `expand_node`; dtype resolution from `value_info`.
  - `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/lib.rs` — new `*_filtered` public entry (old entries delegate with `None`).
  - `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/graph_builder.rs` — thread predicate into `build_graph`/`inline_functions`.
  - `crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/lib.rs` — reorder EP selection above load; build + pass the claim closure.
  - `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/mod.rs` + `provider.rs` — standard-domain registration + claim-gate domain widening.
  - `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/mod.rs` — standard-domain registration.
  - Tests (see §5).
- **NOT touched:** the fused kernels themselves (already ORT-parity), the capture machinery (#443/#543), mobius (separate PR).

## 4. Does this make Inc-1b redundant?
**For LinearAttention models: YES.** The fused kernel eliminates the Scan entirely — one launch per layer, fixing **prefill too** — so Inc-1b's single-trip decode-Scan inline (PR-1) + eager sibling (PR-2) + capture-fold (PR-3) are all redundant on these models (no Scan remains to inline/capture). Inc-1b stays valuable for **generic single-trip Scans that are NOT a fused op** (models with hand-rolled recurrences). Recommendation: keep Inc-1b (default-OFF flag) as-is; do not remove. On the 27b, once this lands and is defaulted, the `ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN` path becomes a no-op (0 Scans to route).

## 5. Correctness + perf plan (mandatory bar)
- **Byte-exact oracle:** `native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e` on stock 27b — native CUDA (fused path, 0 Scans) vs native CPU fp32 oracle (also fused CPU kernel), both must equal the known-good greedy ids `[11751,13,271,248068,271,248069,271,4639,369,4252,13,11751,369,279,6511,321]`. GPU `--test-threads=1`, free GPU.
- **0-Scan assertion:** instrument the built plan / log op-type counts to assert ZERO `Scan` nodes remain for the LinearAttention layers after load (and that 48 `LinearAttention` ops are present + placed on the fused kernel).
- **Dual-domain parity tests:** parameterize the existing `linear_attention_gpu.rs` (+ ep-cpu parity) over `DOMAIN in {"com.microsoft", ""}` proving identical `output`/`present_state` for gated/delta/gated_delta/linear rules, GQA + inverse-GQA, fp16/fp32/bf16. Add a loader unit test: a model declaring a `LinearAttention` function is kept-as-op when the predicate claims it and inlined when it declines (dtype), and a non-fused function still inlines.
- **Perf:** 27b decode ms/tok on the fused path vs Inc-1b's 70.1 (flag ON) vs 143.8 baseline; PREFILL time before/after. Expect fused <= 70.1 and a prefill cut (Scan removed). Report honestly; ORT-CUDA crashes on this hybrid export (documented) so no live ORT number.
- **Capture-eligibility:** confirm the fused `LinearAttention` kernel is `CaptureSupport::Supported` (no host-read seam forcing an eager segment). If it forces a seam, say so and quantify.

## 6. Hazards
- **update_rule default:** node omits `update_rule`; `UpdateRule::parse` maps `None -> GatedDelta` (`ep-cuda/kernels/linear_attention.rs`). Matches the mobius 27b intent; the parity/oracle test guards it.
- **CPU oracle now fused too:** the e2e CPU leg switches from inlined-Scan to the fused CPU kernel. It must stay byte-exact to the known-good ids — this is a real behavior change on the default CPU path for LinearAttention models (intended; validated by the oracle).
- **beta/decay optional inputs:** node passes 5 inputs (…,decay) with beta absent for some layers; confirm the fused kernel's optional-input handling matches the inlined body for the gated_delta rule (covered by oracle byte-exactness).
- **dtype unknown at proto level:** handled by the inline-fallback (safe), but means a model lacking `value_info` for q/k/v would silently inline; acceptable and behavior-preserving.
- **EP-before-load reorder:** verify EPContext / explicit-EP-override load paths still work (regression-run the session test suite).

## 7. Follow-up (not this PR)
- **mobius:** should emit the standard-domain `LinearAttention` (onnx/onnx#7689) so future exports need no `com.microsoft` alias. Separate mobius PR (never self-merged) — noted here for the coordinator.

---

## Verdict
**BUILD.** Size L, bounded. Default path (no fused-kernel function op, or unresolved dtype) is byte-identical to today; the change is a general claim-driven keep-as-op hook + additive dual-domain registration. Correctness gated by the 27b byte-exact oracle with 0 Scans, dual-domain parity, and capture-eligibility confirmation. Supersedes Inc-1b for LinearAttention models (keep Inc-1b for generic Scans).

---

## Addendum — 2026-08-02: policy clarification from Justin (generalize Part 1) + Inc-1b composition

**By:** Cohaagen. **Status:** Confirmed the shipped design (PR #594) already satisfies the clarified policy; no code change required. Verification below.

### The rule, restated (Justin): skip inlining a model-local function call whenever the ACTIVE EP has a registered kernel that CLAIMS that node (factory registered for op+domain AND claim gate returns None for this node). Else fall back to inlining. General, DRY, registry-driven — LinearAttention is just the first beneficiary.

### The shipped hook is already exactly this — no op-name list in the loader
- The loader's keep-as-op decision is made by a caller-supplied predicate `KeepAsOp = dyn Fn(&ir::Node, u64 /*opset*/, &[DataType]) -> bool`, applied in `expand_node` to **every** node that matches a declared function (`function_inline.rs`), with no reference to any specific op name/domain. Verified: `git grep -n "LinearAttention|\"Scan\"|com.microsoft"` over `onnx-runtime-loader/src/` and `onnx-runtime-session/src/lib.rs` returns only pre-existing, unrelated code (EPContext handling; the pre-existing `If|Loop|Scan` control-flow helper at loader `lib.rs:592`). The keep-as-op path adds zero op-name gating.
- The session builds the predicate from the **active EP's own claim path**: `|node, opset, dtypes| ep.supports_op(node, opset, &[], dtypes, &[]).is_supported()` (`onnx-runtime-session/src/lib.rs` `build()`). `ExecutionProvider::supports_op` first checks the kernel **registry** (`registry.supports(op_type, domain, opset)` — is a factory registered?) and then runs that op's **claim gate** (`unsupported_reason`/equivalent). So the predicate returns true **iff a backend kernel is registered for the op+domain AND claims this node** — precisely the rule. Any fused op we register later automatically gets kept-as-op with no loader edit.
- **Fallback preserved:** predicate false (no kernel, unsupported config, or unresolved dtype -> `DataType::Undefined` -> gate declines) -> inline the function body, byte-identical to today. Default `load_model*` passes no predicate -> inline everything.

### Why this is the least-invasive general design (justification requested)
The loader inlines at **proto level, before EP selection**, and must not depend on ep-cpu/ep-cuda. Rather than move inlining after EP selection (large refactor, and shape/KV derivation assume post-inline IR) or build a separate op registry inside the loader (duplicates the EP's claim logic — not DRY), we **inject the EP's existing claim decision as a callback**. The loader stays EP-agnostic; the session (which already selects the EP) supplies the closure. This reuses the single source of truth (`supports_op` = registry + claim gate) and touches only the inline seam + one session wiring point.

### Inc-1b composition — CONFIRMED NO CONFLICT (Inc-1b inline-scan going DEFAULT-ON separately)
The two levers are orthogonal and compose cleanly:
- **On 27b (and any LinearAttention model):** after this change the 48 LinearAttention calls are kept-as-op and dispatch to the fused kernel -> **0 Scans** for those layers (verified: `stock_export_keeps_linear_attention_as_fused_op_with_zero_scans` prints `LinearAttention fused ops=48, Scan ops=0`). With no Scan to route, Inc-1b's `inline_single_trip_scan_bodies` / decode-inline sibling is a **structural no-op there** regardless of whether its flag is default-ON — nothing for it to transform. So flipping Inc-1b default-ON does not change 27b behavior post-this-change.
- **On genuine Scan/Loop models we have NO fused kernel for:** the keep-as-op predicate declines (no registered claiming kernel) -> the function/Scan inlines as today -> Inc-1b (now default-ON) still optimizes those single-trip decode Scans. Its benefit is retained exactly where it matters.
- **Ordering:** keep-as-op happens at load/inline time (before the executor); Inc-1b's inline-scan transform runs later in the session/executor over whatever Scans remain. Since keep-as-op removes only Scans that became fused ops (never leaves a partially-inlined Scan), Inc-1b sees a clean graph. No double-transform, no contention.

**Net:** keep-as-op (fused dispatch) and Inc-1b (generic single-trip Scan inline) are complementary — one removes Scans we can fuse, the other optimizes Scans we can't. Both can be default-ON without interference.

---

## Addendum 2 — 2026-08-02: architecture steer "inlining must be the EP's job" (defer inlining to EP/partition time). SCOPE-FIRST; verdict at end.

**By:** Cohaagen. **Steer (Justin):** the keep-vs-inline decision must be OWNED by the EP, ideally by DEFERRING function inlining to where the EP/assignment is known (session partitioning), not decided at loader proto-time. General mechanism for ANY fused op. No env flag. Scope note is the gate; STOP if L/XL or if it risks the shared partition/shape/capture layers.

### Where the pipeline actually stands (cited against main `1ba215ee`)
- Function inlining is **proto-level and EP-agnostic**, run inside the loader before IR build: `graph_builder.rs:52` `inline_functions(model)` (only inliner in the tree; grep confirms no IR-level inliner — `function_inline.rs` operates on `ModelProto`/`NodeProto`).
- **Shape inference is a LOADER stage**, run AFTER inlining on the primitive graph: `loader/lib.rs:219` `infer_shapes`. It is **permissive**: a node with no handler yields `NodeIo::default()` and inference continues (`shape-inference/registry.rs:104`). So function nodes don't crash it, but their outputs go **shape-unknown** unless a custom handler exists.
- **Load-time validation** (`validate_ir`, `validate_loaded_model`) also runs in the loader on the inlined graph.
- **Per-node EP capability** (`ep.supports_op`) is queried MUCH later, at **executor build / kernel-cache** time: `executor/build.rs:164,292`, `executor/kernel_cache.rs:130` — after load, after EP is known.
- **Default session binds ONE EP** (`lib.rs:739` `select_execution_provider` → single `Arc<dyn ExecutionProvider>`; comment: "CUDA is an explicit opt-in until heterogeneous placement and fallback exist"). Multi-EP `hetero.rs` exists but is opt-in/planning-only, not the default execution path.
- **Layering constraint:** `onnx-runtime-loader` does NOT (and should not) depend on `ep-cpu`/`ep-cuda`. So "the EP owns the decision" can only reach the loader via a callback/trait-object the session injects, or by moving the decision out of the loader entirely.

### The two designs
**(b) — SHIPPED in PR #594 (EP-decision via injected predicate at the existing inline seam).** The loader stays EP-agnostic; the session selects the EP first and passes `keep_as_op = |node,opset,dtypes| ep.supports_op(node,opset,&[],dtypes,&[]).is_supported()`. Claimed function-calls are kept as ops; everything else inlines exactly as today. Reuses the EP's existing claim machinery (registry + `unsupported_reason`), no duplicated op list, general to any fused op, default byte-identical, 27b oracle PASS. The *decision* is EP-driven; the *mechanism* remains one proto-level pass. For the single-EP session this IS "the EP owns the decision."

**(a) — Justin's steer (defer inlining to session/partition time, EP owns it structurally).** The loader stops inlining; function nodes survive into the session; at capability-query/partition time each EP claims the function-ops it implements and UNCLAIMED functions are inlined THEN, with shape/validation re-run on the exposed bodies.

### Layering issues for (a) — enumerated honestly
1. **No IR-level inliner exists.** (a) needs inlining to happen post-IR-build (in the session), so it requires a **new IR-level function inliner** reimplementing the proto inliner's subtle logic against `ir::Graph`: fresh `ValueId` minting, attribute/ref-attr binding, boundary `Identity` aliases, name-collision freshening (the "BUG 3/BUG 4" cases), nested-subgraph recursion, opset-import merging. This is itself M–L and correctness-critical.
2. **Shape inference is a loader stage, run pre-session.** Deferring inlining forces either (i) run shape-infer in the loader on the un-inlined graph (function outputs symbolic) and **re-run** shape-infer in the session after the fallback inline, or (ii) **move shape inference into the session** after inlining. Either restructures the load/session boundary — the session currently receives a fully-shaped IR graph and many downstream stages assume that.
3. **Validation** (`validate_loaded_model`/`validate_ir`) similarly assumes a post-inline graph; must be deferred or re-run on injected bodies.
4. **Kernel assignment assumes every node is a dispatchable op.** A surviving function node has no kernel; the session must inline unclaimed functions BEFORE kernel-cache build, then re-shape/re-validate the injected subgraph — a new session-side pass with ordering constraints.
5. **Multi-EP hetero (Justin flagged this).** A function claimed by EP-A but placed on an EP-B partition that does NOT claim it must be inlined for that instance and the body **re-partitioned** — potentially iterative (inline → re-place → maybe inline again). This interleaves inlining with the `hetero.rs` union-find placement machinery — i.e. it **touches the shared partition layer**.
6. **Capture (#443/#543).** Capture segment planning runs on the final assigned plan; it's only safe if inlining reaches a fixpoint BEFORE capture. Deferred/iterative inlining adds a new ordering invariant to protect the capture layer.
7. **Weights/name_map.** The weight `name_map` is minted at loader graph-build keyed to node outputs; deferring when function-body internal names are created shifts weight binding timing — must be re-verified.

### Size + risk
**Design (a) = L/XL.** It requires a new IR-level inliner **plus** restructuring the load→session boundary (shape-infer/validation), **plus** interleaving inlining with capability query and (for correctness in the general multi-EP case) with `hetero.rs` partitioning, **plus** a new capture-ordering invariant. It directly touches the shared **shape**, **partition**, and **capture** layers Justin named as stop-conditions. Blast radius spans loader, session build, executor build, hetero, and capture — well beyond the LinearAttention goal.

### Optional bounded middle — (b′), if we want the decision to be *explicitly* EP-owned without the deferral
Promote the shipped predicate to a first-class `ExecutionProvider` trait method, e.g. `fn keeps_function_op(&self, node:&Node, opset:u64, dtypes:&[DataType]) -> bool` with a default impl of `self.supports_op(...).is_supported()`. The session calls `ep.keeps_function_op(..)` instead of an ad-hoc closure. This makes "the EP owns the decision" explicit and overridable per-EP, while keeping the mechanism at the existing single-pass inline seam — **S/M, zero shape/partition/capture risk**, default byte-identical. It does NOT defer inlining (so it does not address the forward-looking multi-EP "different EPs claim differently" case), but it is the least-invasive way to genuinely put the choice in EP hands today.

### VERDICT — STOP at scope for the full (a) redesign; surface to Justin
Per the decision rule ("if the EP-driven redesign turns out L/XL or risks the shared partition/shape/capture layers, STOP and report"): **the full deferred/EP-owned-inlining redesign (a) is L/XL and directly risks the shared shape, partition, and capture layers, and requires a new IR-level inliner.** I am NOT building (a) autonomously.

Recommendation to Justin (pick one):
- **KEEP (b)** (already merged-candidate PR #594): EP-driven decision via the existing claim machinery, bounded, oracle-passing, general to any fused op. Satisfies the intent for the single-EP session that ships today.
- **ADD (b′)** on top of (b): a one-method `ExecutionProvider::keeps_function_op` so the decision is a first-class, per-EP-overridable API (least-invasive way to make it "genuinely the EP's job"). I can build this now — it's S/M and low-risk.
- **SCHEDULE (a)** as its own L/XL initiative with capture-team + partition-layer sign-off (defer-inlining-to-partition, new IR inliner, shape/validation restructure). Not autonomous-safe; needs a greenlight and probably its own design doc.

Part 2 (dual-domain, same kernel) is unaffected by this choice and already shipped/validated. No env flag anywhere (confirmed).

### 2026-08-02: Review of PR #592 — remove ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN, make Inc-1b decode-inline graph-property-gated
**By:** Harry (independent reviewer)
**PR:** #592 (branch `squad/inline-scan-default`, head `06a27e09460a1f5b2ee792f392be4c76e7f7cdbf`)
**Author:** Cohaagen (locked out on REJECT — n/a, this is an APPROVE)

## VERDICT: APPROVE

Reviewed at a detached worktree on the PR head. Diff is 4 files, +34/-69, exactly as the
author's note claims. Every checklist item verified by trace + mutation, not on trust.

### 1. Flag fully gone, no dead reads — PASS
`grep -rn ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN --include=*.rs` over the whole tree: **zero code
hits**. `fn decode_inline_scan_enabled()` deleted. Only surviving mentions are in the
historical PR-2 decision note (`.squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-inc1b-pr2.md`), which is an
immutable record, not code. No other code branches on the removed flag. The nearby
`ONNX_GENAI_SCAN_INLINE_SINGLE_TRIP` env var is a **different, pre-existing** flag (main-path
Scan inlining in `executor/state.rs`) — the sibling gate does NOT read it (see #2).

### 2. THE KEY RISK — graph-property gate is sound + conservative — PASS (mutation-proven)
`Executor::build_decode_inline_sibling` (executor/build.rs:686) is a two-stage,
defense-in-depth graph gate, independent of any env:
  - `scans_before == 0 → Ok(None)` (no default-domain Scan → no sibling).
  - runs `inline_single_trip_scan_bodies(&self.graph)` **directly** (not gated by the
    `ONNX_GENAI_SCAN_INLINE_SINGLE_TRIP` env flag) and, if the Scan count did not drop
    (`>= scans_before`), `Ok(None)` — a Scan the transform can't lower is a no-op → no sibling.
  - Only a genuine single-trip-eligible Scan reaches the `Self::build(...)` sibling.
Mutation check on `decode_inline_sibling_none_for_dense_graph` (a real Relu-only
`Executor::build`ed decoder): flipping `>= scans_before` → `> scans_before` alone did NOT
break it (the `== 0` guard catches dense first — defense in depth). Breaking BOTH guards
made the test **FAIL** with "a dense decoder must not build a decode-inline sibling" — so the
test is non-vacuous and genuinely guards the no-Scan→no-sibling property. A false positive on a
model that previously ran the default path is structurally impossible.

### 3. No wasted work when ineligible — PASS
No-Scan path returns at `scans_before == 0` **before** any transform / shape-inference /
compile / memory alloc — only a cheap node-count scan. `maybe_enable_decode_inline`
(native_decode/mod.rs:525) latches on `Untried`, so the probe runs once (idempotent) at the
**first single-token decode step** on both entry points (backend.rs:22 greedy `decode_argmax`,
mod.rs:498 `decode_with_step_inputs`); prefill (len≠1) is skipped. `enable_decode_inline`
(lib.rs:1099) only builds when `decode_inline_exec.is_none()`. Ineligible → latches `Disabled`,
never retries.

### 4. Byte-exact — PASS (test wiring correct; inline-engaged corroborated)
`native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e::stock_export_auto_derives_io_and_matches_cpu_oracle` on this
head reads **no** `ONNX_GENAI_DECODE_INLINE_SCAN` (only `QWEN3_6_27B_CUDA_E2E_DIR`); it asserts
`cuda.token_ids == cpu.token_ids` and len==16 — the gate engages on its own. I did not re-run
the 90-min oracle. Byte-exactness in isolation cannot distinguish inline from the (also
byte-exact) main path, but the separate **2.03× perf (71 vs 144 ms/tok baseline)** is only
achievable if inline actually engaged — a silent dense fallback would sit at ~144 — so
"byte-exact" is not vacuous. The with-Scan positive path is independently covered by the
passing `decode_inline_sibling_is_byte_exact_with_scan_and_preserves_state` unit test.

### 5. Perf sanity — PASS
71.017 ms/tok steady median (runs=3, warmups=1, decode_skip=8) = 2.03×, consistent with
#588/#589 (~71). Methodology sound; not re-run per scope.

### 6. Tests updated correctly — PASS
Removed `decode_inline_flag_defaults_off_and_parses_truthy` (it tested the now-deleted fn;
nothing to replace since the flag is gone). Section header rewritten to point at the session
crate's graph-property tests (`decode_inline_sibling_none_for_dense_graph`,
`..._folds_body_into_captured_graph_byte_exact`). The 3 `route_decode_inline_decision` tests
retained and **PASS** (`--features mlas`, 3/3). Doc comments in mod.rs / cuda.rs / lib.rs
rewritten to graph-property wording; no stale "Default OFF" referencing the removed flag
remains (remaining "default off" hits are unrelated flags — capture-step-inputs, precision
rewrite).

### 7. fmt + clippy — PASS
`cargo fmt --all --check` clean. `cargo clippy -p onnx-genai-engine -p onnx-runtime-session
--all-targets -D warnings` clean at default features **and** re-run with `--features mlas`
(so the `native-backend`-gated touched code is actually linted) — clean.

### Non-blocking note (for future, not a condition of approval)
The byte-exact E2E does not itself assert which executor path ran; it relies on the perf delta
+ unit tests to establish inline engaged. A cheap future assertion on `decode_inline_ready()` /
a routed-step counter inside the E2E would make "inline engaged" self-evident without the
out-of-band perf argument. Not required here.

**Justin's condition ("only way IF no tradeoffs") is honored:** dense/no-Scan models build no
sibling and are byte-identical; genuine single-trip-Scan models get the byte-exact 2.03× path
automatically. No tradeoff found. Approve.

### 2026-08-02: REVIEW — PR #594 (EP-driven function inlining + fused LinearAttention dual-domain dispatch)

**By:** Harry (independent reviewer)
**Verdict:** **REJECT** — one hard, objective blocker (rustfmt fails on code this PR added). The design and logic are otherwise sound and well-tested; this is a clean-up-and-resubmit, not a redesign.
**Head reviewed:** `8cfdfca92592f839badd984c8b4ae2050b69e30b`, base `main` @ `05695ca6`, in detached worktree `wt-harry-594`.
**Author locked out:** Cohaagen. **Revision must be done by a DIFFERENT agent → Deckard** (Systems Dev, CUDA & Perf pod).

---

## BLOCKING FINDING

### B1 — `cargo fmt --all --check` FAILS at the PR head (hard blocker)
The review bar is explicit: "actually RUN `cargo fmt --all --check` … If fmt/clippy is dirty → REJECT." I ran it:

```
Diff in .../crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e.rs:161:
-    let keep = |node: &onnx_runtime_ir::Node,
-                opset: u64,
-                dtypes: &[onnx_runtime_ir::DataType]| {
+    let keep = |node: &onnx_runtime_ir::Node, opset: u64, dtypes: &[onnx_runtime_ir::DataType]| {
         ep.supports_op(node, opset, &[], dtypes, &[]).is_supported()

Diff in .../crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e.rs:178:
-    eprintln!(
-        "loaded 27B plan: LinearAttention fused ops={linear_attention}, Scan ops={scans}"
-    );
+    eprintln!("loaded 27B plan: LinearAttention fused ops={linear_attention}, Scan ops={scans}");
FMT_EXIT=1
```

Both offending blocks are **new code added by this PR** (the `keep` closure and the `eprintln!` in the new `stock_export_keeps_linear_attention_as_fused_op_with_zero_scans` test). This is exactly the gap flagged: Cohaagen's build note claims all checks pass but pasted **no fmt output**. The claim was inaccurate — fmt is dirty.

**Fix (trivial, Deckard):** run `cargo fmt --all`, re-verify `cargo fmt --all --check` is clean, re-run the loader/ep-cpu unit tests (unaffected), and (if a GPU is free) re-confirm the `#[ignore]` 27B oracle + 0-Scan structural test still pass. No logic change required.

---

## EVERYTHING ELSE — VERIFIED GOOD (independent trace/mutation, not trusting the note)

**1. EP is the sole decider (loader has zero op-name knowledge) — PASS.**
`git grep` of `onnx-runtime-loader/src` for `LinearAttention` / `com.microsoft` / `Scan` returns ONLY pre-existing, unrelated code (EPContext handling in `epcontext.rs`/`writer.rs`; the pre-existing `If|Loop|Scan` control-flow helper at `lib.rs:592`; error text). The keep-as-op path adds no op-name gating. The verdict is the caller-supplied `KeepAsOp = dyn Fn(&Node,u64,&[DataType])->bool` applied in `expand_node` to every node matching a declared function. Session builds it from the active EP: `|node,opset,dtypes| ep.supports_op(node,opset,&[],dtypes,&[]).is_supported()` (session `lib.rs`). `supports_op` = registry membership (`registry.supports`) AND per-op claim gate (`unsupported_reason`) — confirmed by reading both cpu (`provider.rs:128`) and cuda (`provider.rs:226`) impls. Predicate true iff a registered kernel claims the node. Exactly the intended rule.

**2. Default path byte-identical for unclaimed functions (the regression risk) — PASS (construction + non-vacuous mutation tests + full-suite regression).**
- By construction: default entries pass `None`; `inline_functions` → `inline_functions_impl(model, None)`; in `expand_node` the new branch is `if filter.is_some_and(|f| f.should_keep(node))` — with `filter == None` it is NEVER taken, so the exact pre-PR `instantiate` path runs. The `filter.map(...)` allocates nothing when `None`.
- Mutation proof (non-vacuous fallback): `filtered_inlines_when_predicate_declines_dtype` forces the predicate to decline (f32 vs f16) and asserts the body `Scan` is exposed (full inline) — i.e. predicate-false == pre-PR unconditional inline. `filtered_still_inlines_functions_no_kernel_claims` proves an unclaimed function inlines identically. Both PASS locally.
- Full regression: I ran the **entire** loader inline suite — `31 passed; 0 failed`. No pre-existing inline behavior changed. This closes the "no dense oracle re-run" gap for the default path as far as is possible without the model.

**3. 27B byte-exact oracle + 0-Scan structural lock are REAL and non-artificial — PASS (as constructed; GPU legs `#[ignore]`).**
`stock_export_keeps_linear_attention_as_fused_op_with_zero_scans` builds the keep-predicate from the **real** `CudaExecutionProvider::supports_op` (nothing forced) and asserts `LinearAttention == 48, Scan == 0`. `stock_export_auto_derives_io_and_matches_cpu_oracle` asserts the exact golden ids `[11751,13,271,248068,271,248069,271,4639,369,4252,13,11751,369,279,6511,321]`. Both require the real 27B export + CUDA and are `#[ignore]` (CPU oracle leg ~92 min); I did not re-run them, but confirmed they are genuine and use the production predicate. Cohaagen's build note shows both passing.

**4. Dual-domain identical, same kernel — PASS.**
Claim gate widened to `matches!(op.domain.as_str(), "" | "ai.onnx" | "com.microsoft")` (cuda `provider.rs:333`). Standard-domain kernel registered in both ep-cpu (`kernels/mod.rs:520`) and ep-cuda (`kernels/mod.rs:739`) reusing the same `LinearAttentionFactory`. Shape handler added for the default domain (`shape-inference/handlers/custom_ops.rs:436` `reg.register("", "LinearAttention", 1, linear_attention)`) so no un-shaped function node survives. I **ran** the CPU parity test `linear_attention_standard_domain_matches_com_microsoft` → PASS (bit-identical to com.microsoft + matches ORT golden). GPU tests `linear_attention_standard_domain_parity` / `linear_attention_both_domains_identical` are real, non-vacuous (byte-equality assertions across domains over f32/f16/bf16 × configs) and skip cleanly without a GPU.

**5. Registry count 100→101 — PASS.** Legit: exactly one added standard-domain `LinearAttention` registration. Not masking a mistake.

**6. Capture-eligibility — PASS.** Fused `LinearAttention` is a single launch/layer with no host-read seam, Supported under capture via the kernel's existing capture registration; the change only prevents Scan expansion, so nothing is newly quarantined/eager.

**7. Written-evidence gaps —** (a) fmt → **FAILS (B1, blocker)**. (b) clippy: `cargo clippy` on `onnx-runtime-loader`, `onnx-runtime-session`, `onnx-runtime-ep-cpu`, `onnx-runtime-shape-inference --all-targets` → clean, zero warnings. `cargo clippy -p onnx-runtime-ep-cuda --features cuda --all-targets` emits warnings, but I confirmed **none** originate from PR-touched files (`linear_attention_gpu.rs`, `provider.rs`, `kernels/mod.rs`) — they are all pre-existing in unrelated GPU test files (pooling/conv/matmul/etc.), so not a regression from this PR. (c) dense default-path confirmation: closed via item 2.

**8. Multi-EP scope — PASS (safe for the shipping single-EP default).** The predicate is bound to the one selected EP (session selects EP before load; device-only selection, correctly hoisted above the load with the `ep` binding reused by `from_parts`). The hetero (EP-A claims / EP-B assigned) case is deferred as L/XL with a documented, honest rationale in the scope note (new IR-level inliner + shape/validation/partition/capture restructuring). Not a latent correctness bug for the default path.

**Perf methodology (41.3 ms/tok):** two-length subtraction (prefill cancels), warm, single GPU — sound as a decode-ms/tok estimate; not re-run. The correctness gate (item 3) is what matters and is real.

---

## Summary
The lever is correctly designed — EP-owned keep-vs-inline via the existing claim machinery, no op-name list in the loader, default byte-identical, dual-domain served by one kernel, capture-eligible, single-EP-sound. The **only** blocker is a rustfmt violation in this PR's own new test code, which the author's note incorrectly implied was clean. **REJECT** on B1. Deckard (not Cohaagen) to run `cargo fmt --all` and resubmit; no logic changes expected.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-thread3-hetero-inline-design.md -->
### 2026-08-02: Move kept function-op fallback to EP assignment time
**By:** Cohaagen

## Scope / note on checkout
This is a design/scoping note only; no product code is implemented here. The working checkout is behind `origin/main` and its local files still show unconditional function inlining, so the concrete #594 citations below are from `origin/main` (commit `50f9ed1a`, PR #594) without switching branches.

## 1. Current pipeline map

### Load-time keep-vs-inline (#594)
- `SessionBuilder::build` now selects the single requested EP before decoding the model: `select_execution_provider` runs at `crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/lib.rs:626-641`.
- Immediately after that, it constructs a `keep_as_op` closure whose only policy is `ep.supports_op(node, opset, &[], dtypes, &[]).is_supported()` (`lib.rs:643-647`). This is intentionally loader-agnostic: the loader sees only a predicate.
- Both file and byte load paths call `onnx_runtime_loader::load_model_bytes_with_weights_filtered(..., &keep_as_op)` (`lib.rs:670-674`, `690-694`).
- The filtered loader API documents the policy: matched function-call nodes are kept iff `keep_as_op` returns true; the unfiltered entry is byte-identical to inlining all functions (`crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/lib.rs:192-210`).
- `graph_builder::build_graph` receives the optional predicate, runs function expansion before IR construction, and chooses `inline_functions_filtered` vs `inline_functions` (`crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/graph_builder.rs:47-59`). This confirms function inline/keep happens before the IR graph is built.
- `function_inline.rs` defines `KeepAsOp` as a predicate over a lightweight `Node`, effective opset, and input dtypes (`crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/function_inline.rs:70-80`). It builds a filter from merged opsets and top-level value types (`function_inline.rs:231-246`).
- When a proto node matches a model-local function, `expand_node` keeps it if `filter.should_keep(node)` is true; otherwise it calls `instantiate` to inline (`function_inline.rs:395-447`). Kept nodes still have graph-valued attributes recursively processed (`function_inline.rs:412-424`, `452-470`).
- Full inlining remains the byte-identical fallback: `inline_functions` delegates to `inline_functions_impl(model, None)` (`function_inline.rs:196-216`), which clears `model.functions` after expansion and merges opset imports (`function_inline.rs:269-276`).

### After load
- After loading, session build may apply fp16 decode rewrite (`lib.rs:701-725`) and general optimizer passes (`lib.rs:727-728`).
- `InferenceSession::from_parts` computes IO metadata and then builds the executor (`lib.rs:967-1006` and downstream executor build).
- `Executor::build` calls `build_with_cuda_requirement`, which immediately calls `place_graph` before materializing initializers or compiling kernels (`crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/executor/build.rs:419-442`).
- `place_graph` validates control-flow signatures, topological order, and single-EP support, then runs EP-scoped passes. For CUDA declines it can revert and replace the entire EP with CPU fallback (`executor/build.rs:720-756`). This is not true heterogeneous assignment; it is whole-session selected-EP-or-CPU fallback.

### Heterogeneous planner / assignment (currently separate)
- `crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/hetero.rs` is a correctness-first heterogeneous planner. It assigns every node to the highest-priority provider whose `supports_op` claims it, partitions assigned nodes into convex claims, and plans transfers (`hetero.rs:1-18`).
- `ProviderPlacement` stores per-provider identity and provider handles (`hetero.rs:59-70`); `HeterogeneousPlan` stores partitions, transfers, and `node_placement` (`hetero.rs:103-112`).
- `assign_nodes` is the actual assignment step: for each node, compute opset + shapes/dtypes/layouts and pick the first provider whose `supports_op` is supported (`hetero.rs:223-258`).
- `provider_partitions` then wraps each provider in `AssignedOracle` so `query_capabilities` only claims nodes assigned to that provider (`hetero.rs:260-296`).
- `plan` calls `assign_nodes`, builds provider partitions, orders them, and plans transfers (`hetero.rs:410-455`).
- `execute` extracts each partition into a runnable subgraph and builds a normal `Executor` for the assigned provider (`hetero.rs:462-620`).

### Control-flow / subgraph bodies
- Loader stores graph-valued attributes in `Graph.subgraphs` so validation/traversal can reach them (`graph_builder.rs:216-245`), and `convert_attribute` recursively builds subgraphs (`graph_builder.rs:389-397`, `442-445`).
- Function inlining already recurses into control-flow subgraphs before IR construction (`function_inline.rs:39-44`, `452-470`) and applies scope-aware capture renaming (`function_inline.rs:717-780`).
- At execution time, control-flow bodies are compiled lazily as child executors using the same `Arc<dyn ExecutionProvider>` as the parent (`crates/onnx-runtime-session/src/executor/control_flow.rs:9-15`, `80-126`, `520-538`). Thus today subgraph bodies are not independently hetero-partitioned; they inherit the parent executor EP and call `Executor::build` recursively.

### Confirmed order / gap
The current #594 order is:

`select one EP -> load proto -> keep/inline function calls using that one EP predicate -> build IR -> optimize -> Executor::place_graph / hetero::plan assignment (if used)`.

So the gap is real: a kept function op can survive because EP-A claimed it at load time, but a later heterogeneous assignment is allowed to choose EP-B by its own priority/support result. If EP-B does not support that fused function op, either planning rejects a graph that could have run via the function body, or a stale/mismatched placement can mis-dispatch. The loader has no assignment context and cannot make the final decision.

## 2. Chosen relocation design

### Recommendation: fusion of inline + hetero partition as a bounded fixpoint
Do not put op knowledge in the loader and do not hardcode `LinearAttention`. Add a partition-time legalization loop owned by the heterogeneous planner:

1. Preserve #594's load-time filtered keep for the common single-EP path. That keeps the existing single-EP perf win and avoids extra work for default sessions.
2. For heterogeneous planning, carry enough function metadata past load to legalize kept calls later. Concretely, introduce a small `FunctionLibrary` artifact from the loader alongside `Graph`/`WeightStore`, containing parsed `FunctionProto`s, merged function opset imports, and an IR/proto-level expansion service shared with `function_inline`.
3. Change hetero planning from one-shot `assign_nodes(graph, providers)` to:
   - assign current graph nodes by provider priority;
   - scan assigned nodes that are model-local function calls kept as ops;
   - for each such node, ask the **assigned** provider's `supports_op(node, effective_opset, shapes, dtypes, layouts)`;
   - if supported, keep it;
   - if unsupported, inline exactly that call into the graph using the shared inliner, preserving ONNX function semantics;
   - re-run shape inference for the edited region/graph;
   - repeat assignment until no assigned kept function op needs expansion.
4. Then run convex partitioning and transfer planning on the legalized graph.

This is a fixpoint rather than a blind second pass because inlining a function can expose nested function calls. Some nested calls may be claimed by an EP and kept; others must inline depending on the provider they are actually assigned to. A fixpoint makes the invariant simple: after planning, every node assigned to an EP is either not a model-local function call or is supported by its assigned EP.

### Why not a pure second pass?
A single post-assignment pass that inlines mismatches and then re-runs partition is the right phase-1 implementation. Architecturally, it should be expressed as the first iteration of a fixpoint (`assign -> legalize -> changed?`) so nested functions and newly exposed ops do not become phase-2 rework.

### Where the code should live
- Keep proto-level expansion mechanics DRY in `onnx-runtime-loader::function_inline`; extract reusable expansion primitives from `instantiate` / `inline_subgraph_attrs` rather than duplicating rename/attribute-binding logic.
- Put assignment-time policy in `onnx-runtime-session::hetero`, because only the planner knows the assigned provider.
- Add a small IR rewrite helper (likely in `onnx-runtime-ir` or loader-owned `FunctionLibrary`) that can splice one function call into an existing IR `Graph`. If initial implementation is faster at proto-level, load hetero sessions through a retained proto + function library and rebuild the graph after legalization; but the long-term API should not force every planner iteration through protobuf decode.

### Function-call identity in IR
A kept node must be recognizable as a model-local function call. Options:
- Preferred: store function library keyed by `(domain, op_type/name, overload)` and add `Node.overload` or a `model_function_key` metadata attribute at graph build. Current IR `Node` has domain/op_type/name/version/attributes; overload is not visible in the viewed lines, so this is likely a small IR metadata addition.
- Acceptable phase-1: only treat a node as legalizable if `(domain, op_type)` uniquely maps to one model-local function and no overload ambiguity exists; error with a clear diagnostic otherwise. This avoids semantic ambiguity while keeping the first slice small.

### Nested functions
The legalization loop must use the same recursion and recursion-cycle guard already in `function_inline` (`function_inline.rs:475-641`). After inlining one call, newly inserted body nodes are ordinary nodes. If any body node is itself a kept function call, the next assignment/legalization iteration handles it. True recursive functions remain rejected exactly as today.

### Subgraphs / control flow
- Top-level hetero planning must recurse through `Graph.subgraphs` and legalize each body. Today subgraphs are compiled as child `Executor`s with the parent's single EP (`control_flow.rs:520-538`), so a complete hetero design needs `ChildExecutor` to carry either (a) the provider list and legalized hetero plan for that body, or (b) the single assigned provider when the control-flow op itself executes on one provider.
- Phase-1 conservative behavior: legalize subgraphs with the same provider set and then compile child bodies with the assigned provider path that can run every body node. If no single assigned provider can run a body after legalization, mark that control-flow node/region ineligible for hetero execution and fall back to all-inlined CPU/reference for the body. This preserves correctness while deferring nested hetero execution.
- Long-term: make child execution mirror top-level hetero planning (`plan_subgraph` + `execute_subgraph`) so If/Loop/Scan bodies can split across providers too.

### Capture eligibility / device capture
Inlining a kept fused op can expand one capture-eligible leaf into multiple primitive ops, or vice versa. Therefore capture analysis must run after legalization/partitioning, not before it. If a partition is rewritten, invalidate `GraphViewCache`, kernel cache entries, capture segmentation, and any device-graph schedule keyed by old node ids. This is consistent with current executor ordering where placement precedes materialization/compile (`executor/build.rs:419-442`).

### IO/value wiring correctness
Use the existing inliner semantics as the source of truth:
- formal inputs/outputs are remapped positionally;
- internal values get globally fresh names;
- pass-through formal outputs synthesize boundary `Identity` nodes;
- `ref_attr_name` attributes bind at all depths;
- subgraph captures are renamed scope-aware and subgraph locals shadow outer names (`function_inline.rs:536-641`, `643-714`, `717-780`).
For IR-level splicing, preserve the same invariants with `ValueId` remaps rather than string remaps, and immediately run graph validation + shape inference on the rewritten graph before re-assignment.

### Generality / DRY requirements
- No per-op names, no model-specific paths, no CUDA/CPU special cases.
- All decisions are `(assigned_ep, node, opset, shapes, dtypes, layouts, function_library)`.
- A non-function op unsupported by every provider remains a normal unsupported-op error.
- A function op unsupported by its assigned provider is not an error until its inlined body also cannot be assigned anywhere.

## 3. Correctness invariant and proof plan

### Invariant
After heterogeneous legalization reaches a fixpoint:
1. Every executable node in every graph/subgraph is assigned to an EP whose `supports_op` accepts it at the effective opset and inferred shape/type/layout metadata.
2. Any model-local function call not accepted by its assigned EP has been replaced by the exact ONNX function body expansion.
3. Therefore hetero execution is semantically equivalent, and for deterministic kernels byte-identical, to the all-inlined fallback for any provider assignment.

### Proof: hetero byte-identical fallback
- Differential test each legalizing rewrite against `inline_functions(model)` / unfiltered load: run the original all-inlined graph on CPU and the hetero-legalized graph with forced provider split; compare output bytes.
- Mutation: make the fake accelerator claim the fused function at load but decline it at assigned-provider legalization; without legalization the test must fail (unsupported/misdispatch), with legalization it passes and the final graph contains primitive body ops.
- Validate graph structure after each rewrite: SSA, no producer-less outputs, topological order, inferred shapes, and no unresolved function calls except those supported by their assigned EP.

### Proof: single-EP perf preserved
- The existing single-EP load path remains #594: select EP before load, keep function call if that EP claims it (`lib.rs:626-647`). No hetero fixpoint runs for single-EP sessions.
- Add/keep a test that a `LinearAttention` model-local function call is retained as `com.microsoft::LinearAttention` when the selected EP claims it, and that the fused kernel dispatches. This guards against accidentally reverting to unconditional inlining.

## 4. Phased implementation plan + tests

### Phase 0 — test harness + graph observability (S)
- Rebase/update branch to include #594 in the local checkout.
- Add inspection helpers for function-op count / op types in loaded graphs and hetero plans.
- Extend the existing CPU-backed hetero test infra (`hetero/tests.rs:1-11`, `26-79`) with a fake provider that can independently control: load-time claim, assignment-time claim, and `get_kernel` support. This avoids requiring CUDA in CI.
- Mutation target: fake provider claims `FusedFn` at load but declines it in assignment; test must expose the current hole.

### Phase 1 — bounded correctness fix (M)
- Implement conservative post-assignment legalization in `hetero::plan`: assign, inline any assigned kept function whose assigned EP declines it, re-run shape inference, reassign once or to a bounded fixpoint.
- Initially support top-level graphs and subgraphs by recursively legalizing each `Graph.subgraphs` body with the same provider set. If child hetero execution is not ready, require each legalized body to be runnable by the inherited child EP or fall back the body to fully inlined CPU/reference.
- Keep all existing single-EP behavior untouched.
- Tests:
  - synthetic model-local function `FusedAddRelu` or `LinearAttention`-shaped function; load-time fake EP keeps it, hetero assignment forces it to CPU/fallback EP that lacks fused kernel; assert final legalized graph contains body ops and execution matches all-inlined CPU bytes.
  - nested function: outer kept call inlines to inner function call; fixpoint handles inner.
  - If/Loop/Scan body contains kept function; assert body legalizes and child executor runs.

### Phase 2 — first-class `FunctionLibrary` / IR splicer (M/L)
- Refactor `function_inline` so the expansion algorithm is available for both whole-model proto expansion and single-node/IR expansion.
- Carry function metadata through `load_model_bytes_with_weights_filtered` without requiring product kernels to know about functions.
- Add overload-safe function identity in IR.
- Tests cover overload ambiguity, recursive rejection, pass-through Identity, attr binding, and scope-aware subgraph captures using existing `function_inline` fixtures as regression seeds.

### Phase 3 — integrate hetero sessions / child hetero plans (L)
- Expose a public/session path for multiple providers. Today `SessionBuilder` selects one EP (`lib.rs:626-641`) and `place_graph` only does whole-session CUDA fallback (`executor/build.rs:720-756`); `hetero.rs` is present but not wired as the normal session executor.
- Teach control-flow `ChildExecutor` to carry provider lists and compile/execute a hetero plan for bodies, not just `self.ep.clone()` (`control_flow.rs:520-538`).
- Add CUDA+CPU integration test behind `#[cfg(feature="cuda")]` and env/availability gates.

### Phase 4 — perf and capture cleanup (M)
- Ensure capture segmentation and kernel caches are keyed after legalization.
- Add perf counters/tracing: number of kept functions, assignment-time inlined functions, legalization iterations, and final partition counts.
- Benchmark single-EP Qwen3.5/LinearAttention path to prove no regression and hetero fallback overhead is bounded.

## 5. Test strategy details

### CI synthetic hetero test (no CUDA required)
Use the existing host-backed `AcceleratorEp` pattern (`hetero/tests.rs:26-79`):
- Provider A: fake accelerator with `DeviceType::Mlx`, CPU-backed kernels, and allow-list behavior.
- Provider B: CPU EP or fake CPU that intentionally lacks the fused function but supports the primitive body ops.
- Construct a small ONNX `ModelProto` with a model-local function (prefer `FusedAddRelu` for simple bytes; add a `LinearAttention`-named variant only if its body/kernels are convenient).
- At load-time, use a predicate that keeps the function call. At hetero assignment, force that node to Provider B or make Provider A unavailable for that node so the assigned EP lacks the fused op.
- Assert:
  - before legalization, planning would reject/decline the fused op;
  - after legalization, the fused node count is zero for the Provider-B-assigned case;
  - primitive ops are assigned and execute;
  - outputs are byte-identical to unfiltered all-inlined CPU load.

### Single-EP regression test
- Use actual CPU `LinearAttention` if available: CPU registers `com.microsoft::LinearAttention` (`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/mod.rs` in #594 area) and `supports_op` should claim it.
- Load through `InferenceSession::builder().execution_provider(cpu)` and assert graph/op audit still sees one `LinearAttention` node, not the decomposed body.
- Execute a minimal deterministic case and assert the fused kernel path is reached (counter/dispatch audit if available), preserving #594's perf win.

### GPU-gated integration
- If CUDA feature + device available, run CUDA+CPU provider list with a real `LinearAttention` model where CUDA claims fused op but assignment is forced/priority-swapped to CPU or a fallback provider that lacks it. If current public session API cannot register multiple providers, this is Phase 3 infra scope.

## 6. Risk register

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Local checkout drift from #594 | Design/code mismatch | Base implementation branch on `origin/main` or later; I verified #594 on `origin/main` without switching. |
| IR lacks function overload identity | Wrong function expansion | Add `Node.overload`/function metadata or fail closed on ambiguous functions in Phase 1. |
| Rewriting after assignment invalidates node ids/caches | Stale placement/capture/kernel cache | Legalization must happen before convex partitioning, extraction, materialization, kernel compile, and capture planning; rebuild `GraphViewCache` each iteration. |
| Subgraph bodies still inherit single EP | Hidden runtime failure inside If/Loop/Scan | Phase 1 recursively legalizes bodies; Phase 3 gives child executors hetero plans. |
| Shape inference after partial inline changes metadata | Bad supports_op decisions | Re-run permissive shape inference after each rewrite and before re-assignment, matching existing optimizer/child-compile patterns. |
| Inlining all mismatches regresses perf | Loss of fused kernels | Only inline when the **assigned** EP declines; single-EP path unchanged; tests assert fused op kept. |
| Function body ops unsupported by any provider | Late error | After inlining, normal `assign_nodes` reports the first unsupported primitive with provider list. |
| Dtype/layout mismatch between load-time claim and assignment-time claim | Incorrect keep | Assignment-time support uses real graph shapes/dtypes/layouts (`hetero.rs:227-235`), not #594's lightweight load predicate. |

## 7. Bounded first step
Smallest safe increment that closes the correctness hole:

- In hetero planning only, after initial assignment, detect kept model-local function nodes whose assigned provider declines `supports_op` with real graph metadata.
- Inline those nodes using the existing function expansion semantics.
- Re-run whole-graph permissive shape inference.
- Re-run `hetero::plan` from scratch on the rewritten graph.
- Limit to a small max iteration count (e.g. number of kept function calls + nested function count); if exceeded, report recursive/unstable function legalization.

This is reviewable and mutation-testable without wiring full public multi-EP sessions. It may be less elegant (whole-graph replan), but it is conservative and keeps the single-EP fast path untouched.

## Recommendation
Build this now if heterogeneous CUDA+CPU/fallback sessions are in or near scope; otherwise land Phase 0 now and Phase 1 before exposing multi-EP sessions publicly. Phase 1 should be the bounded post-assignment legalization loop in `hetero::plan`, backed by CPU-only fake-provider mutation tests. I would ask Justin/coordinator for one decision before coding: should Phase 1 add first-class `FunctionLibrary`/IR overload metadata immediately, or is fail-closed unique `(domain, op_type)` support acceptable for the first correctness patch?

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/coordinator-thread3-phase1-scope.md -->
### 2026-08-02: Thread-3 (hetero inlining relocation) — build bounded Phase 0+1 now, defer Phases 2-4
**By:** Squad (Coordinator), for justinchuby (full autonomous authority)
**What:** After Cohaagen's design pass (`cohaagen-thread3-hetero-inline-design.md`), decided to build only the bounded slice now:
- **Phase 0** — CPU fake-provider test harness (load-time claim vs assignment-time decline).
- **Phase 1** — post-assignment legalization loop in `hetero::plan`: after `assign_nodes`, any kept model-local function-call node whose ASSIGNED provider declines `supports_op` (real graph shapes/dtypes/layouts) is inlined via the existing `function_inline` semantics (DRY reuse), re-shape-inferred, and reassigned to a bounded fixpoint; subgraph bodies legalized conservatively.
- **Function-identity policy:** fail-closed — only legalize when `(domain, op_type)` uniquely maps to one model-local function; ambiguity → actionable Err. NO IR `Node.overload`/`FunctionLibrary` metadata now.
**Why:**
- **The correctness hole is LATENT.** `hetero.rs` (the multi-EP planner that can assign a kept fused op to a non-supporting EP) is present but NOT wired to the default session path — `SessionBuilder` selects ONE EP and `place_graph` only does whole-session CUDA→CPU fallback. So no user currently hits the bug; it only bites once public multi-EP hetero sessions are exposed.
- Phase 0+1 is bounded, reviewable, mutation-testable on CPU (no CUDA), changes no public API, and leaves the #594 single-EP perf fast path untouched. It closes the hole + adds regression locks now, without committing to the L/XL build.
**Deferred to future-work (surface to Justin):** Phase 2 (first-class `FunctionLibrary` + IR overload-safe function identity), Phase 3 (public multi-EP session wiring + child-executor hetero plans for If/Loop/Scan bodies), Phase 4 (capture/kernel-cache keying after legalization + perf counters). Fund when public CUDA+CPU/fallback hetero sessions are actually in scope.
**Reference:** design note `cohaagen-thread3-hetero-inline-design.md`; #594 (EP-driven inlining) `origin/main` 50f9ed1a; hetero.rs assign_nodes:223-258 / plan:410-455; lib.rs:626-647 (single-EP keep predicate).
