## Processed decision inbox batch — 2026-08-03T03:10:00+0000 (PackedMHA bias-slot admission fix)

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-35b-a3b-admission-rootcause.md -->
# Root cause: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B admission fails — PackedMultiHeadAttention "6 inputs vs 5 declared"

**Author:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime)
**Date:** 2026-08-03
**Status:** Diagnosis complete (READ-ONLY, no PR yet)
**Verdict:** **(A) mobius export bug.** onnx-genai's loader is spec-correct.

---

## TL;DR

The `vision_encoder` `com.microsoft::PackedMultiHeadAttention` node has **6 inputs**
(`query, key, value, ""(bias absent), token_offset, cumulative_sequence_length`),
which is **valid** per ORT's contract (op takes 6–7 inputs). But mobius exports a
**model-local ONNX function** fallback for `PackedMultiHeadAttention` whose formal
signature declares only **5 inputs** — it **omits the `bias` slot**. onnx-genai's
function-inliner correctly refuses to bind a call that passes more actuals (6) than
the function declares formals (5), so admission fails before load. Fix is one line of
signature in mobius; do NOT change onnx-genai.

---

## 1. The real op contract

`com.microsoft::PackedMultiHeadAttention` — ORT `docs/ContribOperators.md`:

> **Inputs (6 - 7)**
> 1. `query` (required)
> 2. `key` (optional)
> 3. `value` (optional)
> 4. `bias` (optional) — `(hidden + hidden + v_hidden)` projection bias
> 5. `token_offset` (required)
> 6. `cumulative_sequence_length` (required)
> 7. `attention_bias` (optional)

**Minimum valid arity is 6, max is 7.** Because `token_offset`/`cumulative_sequence_length`
are positional slots 5 and 6, the optional `bias` slot 4 must be *present* (as an
absent/empty input `""`) — you cannot drop it. So a **6-input** node
`(query, key, value, "", token_offset, cu_seqlens)` is the correct minimal call, and
that is exactly what mobius emits. **6 inputs is VALID.**

## 2. Where "5 declared" comes from (mobius, not onnx-genai)

onnx-genai has **no** native `PackedMultiHeadAttention` kernel, schema, or shape-inference
handler (grep of `crates/` finds zero references outside docs). Admission therefore relies
on inlining the **model-local function** that mobius ships in `model.functions`.

That function is defined in mobius and declares only **5** formal inputs (verified by
building it):

```
com.microsoft::PackedMultiHeadAttention
FORMAL inputs (5): ['query', 'key', 'value', 'token_offset', 'cumulative_sequence_length']
                                          ^^^ 'bias' is MISSING here
```

- `mobius/src/mobius/functions/packed_multi_head_attention.py:156-172` — `build_function(...)`
  inputs list `[query, key, value, token_offset, cumulative_sequence_length]` (no `bias`).
- `.../packed_multi_head_attention.py:67-74` — `body(op, query, key, value, token_offset, cumulative_sequence_length)` (no `bias` param).

Both mobius **call sites** emit **6** inputs (with `None`/absent bias in slot 4):

- `mobius/src/mobius/components/_qwen3_vl_vision.py:252-263` — `_emit_packed_mha`:
  `op.PackedMultiHeadAttention(query, key, value, None, token_offset, cu_seqlens_int32, ...)`
- `mobius/src/mobius/rewrite_rules/_packed_attention.py:169-180` — `BlockDiagonalToPackedMHA.rewrite`:
  `op.op("PackedMultiHeadAttention", q, k, v, None, token_offset, cu_seqlens_i32, ...)`

So the **call node (6 inputs) ≠ the function it resolves to (5 formals)**.

## 3. The exact onnx-genai site enforcing 5 (and why it is CORRECT)

`crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/function_inline.rs:329-339`:

```rust
// Arity: passing *more* actuals than the function declares is illegal;
// passing fewer is allowed (trailing optionals omitted, mapped to absent).
if call.input.len() > func.input.len() {        // 6 > 5  -> reject
    return Err(LoaderError::FunctionArityMismatch {
        function: fmt_key(&key), node: node_label(call),
        kind: "input", formal: func.input.len(), actual: call.input.len(),
    });
}
```

Error text (`crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/error.rs:170-183`, `FunctionArityMismatch`):

> "...supplies **6** input(s) but the function declares only **5**. RULES #1: a function
> call may omit trailing optional inputs but must not supply more than are declared."

This is **spec-correct ONNX semantics**: an ONNX function call may omit *trailing* optional
inputs, but must never pass *more* actuals than the callee declares formals. The "5 declared"
originates entirely from the mobius-exported `FunctionProto`, not from any onnx-genai
registry/kernel. **onnx-genai is right to reject; do not loosen this check.**

## 4. Verdict + exact fix

**(A) mobius export bug.** The portable-fallback function signature is missing the `bias`
input that the op's contract defines and that every mobius call site actually passes.

### Fix (mobius — Justin merges, do NOT self-merge)

File: `mobius/src/mobius/functions/packed_multi_head_attention.py`

1. Add `bias` as the **4th** formal input in `build_function(...)` (between `value` and
   `token_offset`):

   ```python
   [
       ir.Value(name="query"),
       ir.Value(name="key"),
       ir.Value(name="value"),
       ir.Value(name="bias"),                       # <-- ADD (optional, unused in fallback)
       ir.Value(name="token_offset"),
       ir.Value(name="cumulative_sequence_length"),
   ]
   ```

2. Add the matching `bias_input` param to the `body(...)` signature and ignore it (exactly
   as `token_offset_input` is already ignored by the fallback):

   ```python
   def body(op, query_input, key_input, value_input,
            bias_input, token_offset_input, cumulative_sequence_length_input):
   ```

3. Update the module/function docstring input list (currently 5) to include `bias`.

That is the minimal correct change: the function now declares **6** formal inputs matching
ORT's contract and both call sites; `6 <= 6` so the inliner binds it and admission passes.
(Ignoring `bias` in the body is functionally correct today because both call sites always
pass `bias = None`/absent; if a future call site supplies a real bias, the body should add
it to Q/K/V projections — out of scope here.)

### onnx-genai: no change

The loader arity rule is correct. **Do not** add a native PackedMHA kernel or relax the
function-arity check to "fix" this — that would mask a genuinely malformed function proto.

## 5. How verified (synthetic, no full model download needed)

Root-caused from the op contract + direct inspection of the emitted function — faster and
sufficient to prove (A) vs (B); did not need the 35B weights.

1. **ORT contract**: fetched `microsoft/onnxruntime docs/ContribOperators.md` → PackedMHA
   `Inputs (6 - 7)` with `bias` as slot 4. → 6-input call is valid.
2. **mobius function arity** (`.venv/bin/python`):
   ```
   FORMAL inputs (5): ['query','key','value','token_offset','cumulative_sequence_length']
   call 6 > formal 5  => mismatch = True
   ```
   Confirms `bias` is dropped from the signature while call sites pass 6.
3. **onnx-genai reject path**: `function_inline.rs:331` `call.input.len() > func.input.len()`
   → `FunctionArityMismatch{formal:5, actual:6}` → the reported "6 inputs vs 5 declared".
4. **Fix proof**: rebuilt the function with the added `bias` formal →
   `FORMAL inputs (6)` and `6 <= 6 => admits: True`.

## Files (citations)

- ORT spec: `microsoft/onnxruntime` `docs/ContribOperators.md` §com.microsoft.PackedMultiHeadAttention — Inputs (6-7).
- onnx-genai enforce: `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/function_inline.rs:329-339`;
  error: `crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/error.rs:170-183`.
- mobius bug: `mobius/src/mobius/functions/packed_multi_head_attention.py:67-74, 156-172`.
- mobius call sites: `mobius/src/mobius/components/_qwen3_vl_vision.py:252-263`;
  `mobius/src/mobius/rewrite_rules/_packed_attention.py:169-180`.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/deckard-packed-mha-bias-slot.md -->
### 2026-08-03: Add optional bias slot to mobius PackedMultiHeadAttention fallback
**By:** Deckard
**What:** In mobius `src/mobius/functions/packed_multi_head_attention.py`, inserted the optional `bias` formal input at slot 4 (between `value` and `token_offset`) in both `build_function(...)`'s input list and the `body(...)` signature, so the fallback `ir.Function` now declares 6 formals: `query, key, value, bias, token_offset, cumulative_sequence_length`. The body ignores `bias` (block-diagonal bias is reconstructed from `cu_seqlens`); it exists only to preserve positional slot alignment. Added regression test `tests/packed_multi_head_attention_function_test.py` asserting positional order + 6-input call admissibility. mobius PR #449 (https://github.com/onnxruntime/mobius/pull/449) — DO NOT MERGE, Justin merges mobius PRs.
**Why:** ORT `com.microsoft::PackedMultiHeadAttention` has a positional 6–7 input signature where `token_offset`/`cumulative_sequence_length` are slots 5/6, so optional `bias` (slot 4) must be a formal input. The fallback dropped it (5 formals), so onnx-genai's spec-correct function-inline admission rejected the 6-input call sites with FunctionArityMismatch (6 > 5) for the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B vision_encoder. onnx-genai is correct and was not touched; the fix is purely in mobius.

# Cohaagen — Qwen3.6-35B-A3B validation + native-vs-ORT fairness vet

**By:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf) · **When:** 2026-08-03T05:45→06:30+0000
**Repos:** onnx-genai @ main `1ba215ee` (rebuilt `profile_native` from this SHA — Jul-31 binary predated the Aug-2 fused-LinearAttention merge and would misrun linear-attn models); mobius @ main `8a565d7`.
**Scope:** (A) validate the larger MoE model; (B) fairness-vet the already-claimed results before Justin files a GitHub issue.
**No PRs opened, nothing merged.** Fairness rule enforced: every native-vs-ORT number is either strictly same-artifact apples-to-apples, or labelled a capability gap (never a throughput multiplier).

---

## TASK A — Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (MoE, ~3B active): CAPABILITY GAP on BOTH engines today

**Artifact built (text-only int4):** `/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts/`
- Reused Mary's existing int4 decoder (`mary-models/qwen3.6-35b-a3b-int4-cuda/decoder`, 19 GB) — **Olive `q4_k_m`, int4 `MatMulNBits` block_size=32**, arch `qwen3_5_moe_text`. This is the strongest apples-to-apples base (identical weights for both engines) and skips a multi-hour re-quant.
- MoE is **`representation: dense_fallback`** — 256 routed experts / top-8 are lowered to **per-expert `MatMulNBits` + masking (no fused `QMoE` op)**. Attention is a **linear-attn hybrid**: 30 `LinearAttention` + 10 `GroupQueryAttention`, with conv/recurrent state ports and **rank-3 mRoPE positions**.
- Made it text-only two ways: (1) a clean embedding→decoder pipeline (rewrote the tiny embedding graph to a text-only `Gather`, dropping the required `image_features` input, reusing the same embed weights); (2) a single merged graph (`merged/`) that folds the embed `Gather` into the decoder and casts the 40 fp16 gate `TopK`s to fp32 (numerically neutral: fp16→fp32 is exact, selection identical).

**Result — no defensible apples-to-apples GPU throughput exists for the 35B right now:**

| Engine | Outcome | Exact evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **ORT-CUDA** | **Hard crash / core dump** | `stl_vector.h:1130 … Assertion '__n < this->size()' failed` during graph optimization of the linear-attn hybrid decoder — the **same** failure class already recorded for Qwen3.6-27B (`std::vector::operator[]`, decisions.md:510). ORT cannot run this model at all. |
| **Native-CUDA** | **No GPU number obtainable via current tooling** | Three stacked blockers: (1) the fp16 **MoE-gate `TopK`** is not claimed by the CUDA EP (`TopK: input 0 'X' dtype Float16 unsupported; expected Float32`, 40 nodes) → whole-session CPU fallback (I fixed this with an fp32 cast); (2) the decoder needs a **rank-3 mRoPE positions program**, which only `pipeline.positions` supplies; (3) **native pipeline decode is unimplemented** ("native pipeline decode is not yet implemented … remaining GAP 3 work"), and `profile_native`'s single-decoder path calls `NativeDecodeSession::load` with `io=None` so it can't read the explicit `model.io` needed to disambiguate the ambiguous `input_ids`/`attention_mask` ports either. |

**Honest characterization:** Qwen3.6-35B-A3B is a **capability gap for ORT-CUDA (crashes)**. Native-CUDA is *architecturally closer* (loads both components, dense_fallback MoE is int4 `MatMulNBits` it already runs, only fp16-`TopK` + the rank-3-position/pipeline-decode plumbing block it), but it **cannot yet produce a GPU decode number for this hybrid via `profile_native`**. This **extends the 27B story**: the 27B runs natively because it is dense + flat + ≤rank-2 positions; the 35B-A3B adds (a) a 256-expert fp16-`TopK` router with no CUDA kernel and (b) rank-3 mRoPE requiring native pipeline decode.

**Actionable follow-ups (candidate bounded PRs, separate + Harry-reviewed):**
1. onnx-runtime-ep-cuda: add an fp16 `TopK` kernel (or auto-cast the router logits) — unblocks all `dense_fallback` MoE decoders on native CUDA. Sole op gap for this model.
2. engine: land native pipeline decode (the flagged "GAP 3") so rank-3 mRoPE hybrids run natively; and/or teach `profile_native --model` to pass parsed `model.io` to the single-decoder native load.
3. (Not needed for perf) A proper mobius `#445 --text-only` re-export would yield a rank-2-position, image_features-free artifact; deprioritized because the ORT side crashes regardless, so it wouldn't produce a comparison.

---

ORT given its legit best config (CUDA EP + cuDNN 1.27). Ratio = native_tok/s ÷ ORT_tok/s. **Same int4 Foundry artifact loaded by both engines.**
- ORT QMoE placement re-verified live this session: CUDA EP logs `CUDA kernel not found in registries for Op type: QMoE` for **all 26** DeepSeek-V2-Lite QMoE nodes → CPU fallback. GLM-4-9B ORT re-verified live: `INVALID_GRAPH: Unrecognized attribute: rotary_embedding_dim for operator GroupQueryAttention`.

Do not present 3.5× as an ORT speedup. (Intermediate: decode-inline 71.0 ms/tok = 2.03× vs the 143.8 baseline; fused-LinearAttention kernel takes it to 40.8, and current main to 25.7.) |
| **DeepSeek-V2-Lite** | 16B (2.4B act) | int4 **QMoE** (26 ops, blk128) | 19.7 ms/tok | 5011.8 ms/tok | **capability gap (ORT CPU fallback)** | native==ORT tokens for first 24 profiled | The ~250× is **NOT a same-kernel GPU speedup**. Verified live: ORT-CUDA has **no QMoE GPU kernel** (`CUDA kernel not found in registries for Op type: QMoE`, all 26 nodes) → QMoE runs on **CPU** each step. Frame as "native runs QMoE on GPU; ORT-CUDA has no QMoE CUDA kernel → CPU fallback (0.2 tok/s)". |
| **GLM-4-9B** | 9B | int4 (partial-RoPE GQA) | 9.85 ms/tok, byte-exact golden greedy lock (PASS) | **cannot load** | **capability gap (ORT rejects graph)** | native byte-exact vs golden lock | Verified live: ORT-CUDA `INVALID_GRAPH: Unrecognized attribute: rotary_embedding_dim for operator GroupQueryAttention`. This is a **coverage/correctness win, not a speed number** — do not attach a multiplier. |
| **Qwen3.6-35B-A3B** | 35B (3B act) | int4 `MatMulNBits` blk32, dense_fallback MoE | no GPU number (see Task A) | **crashes** (same `std::vector` assertion as 27B) | **capability gap (both engines, this session)** | not reached | Both blocked today: ORT crashes; native needs fp16-`TopK` CUDA kernel + native pipeline decode. Report as capability gap, not a ratio. |

### ⚠️ Unverified / caveats to carry into the issue
- DeepSeek-R1-1.5B 1.70× is from a **quick 32-token profile**, not the steady median≥3 protocol — labelled ⚠️ above.
- Qwen2.5-1.5B tokens "diverge" but native is the **oracle-correct** side; don't describe it as a native bug.
- 27B "3.5×" MUST be labelled native-vs-native-baseline, never native-vs-ORT.

**Bottom line:** four Foundry dense wins (0.5B/1.5B/mini/7B) and DeepSeek-R1-1.5B are the *legitimate* same-artifact apples-to-apples throughput results. Everything MoE/hybrid (27B, DeepSeek-V2-Lite QMoE, GLM-4-9B, 35B-A3B) is a **capability gap** where ORT-CUDA cannot run the same kernel on GPU — those must be framed as coverage/capability, not throughput multipliers.

### 2026-08-02: Qwen2.5-7B GQA decode scope finds no bounded win
**By:** Cohaagen
**What:** Scoped Qwen2.5-7B native CUDA decode after the Foundry sweep. Nsight with CUDA graph node tracing showed MatMulNBits kernels account for 724.988 ms / 88.3% of GPU kernel time over 256 profiled decode steps, while all GQA kernels together account for 88.903 ms / 10.8%. Raising `GQA_MIN_KEYS_PER_SPLIT` from 16 to 32 regressed median decode from 3.239 to 3.260 ms/token with byte-identical greedy IDs, so the candidate was reverted.
**Why:** The earlier op-level GQA dominance was an async/CUDA-graph attribution artifact. Qwen2.5-7B decode is HBM/MatMulNBits-bound; next perf work should target dense INT4 GEMV kernels rather than speculative GQA changes.

### Qwen3.6-35B-A3B native re-validation on `origin/main` — corrected record

- **By:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf & engine)
- **Date:** 2026-08-03
- **Built from:** FRESH `origin/main` worktree `../ogenai-wt-35b-reval` @ **`0a5ac3c5`**
  (newer than `be6d4e34`/#612; the local `main` checkout `1ba215ee` is 74 commits stale).
- **Binary:** `profile_native` release, `--features "bench-native cuda"`, provenance
  confirmed at `0a5ac3c5`.
- **Artifact (same for both engines):** `/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts/`
  (Olive q4_k_m `MatMulNBits` blk32, `dense_fallback` MoE, gated-linear-attn hybrid;
  embedding→decoder split + a `merged/` graph). GQA 16h/2kv hd256, 40 layers, rank-3 mRoPE.
- **Env:** ORT 1.27 (`ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR`), CUDA-13 + cuDNN via conda
  `.../nvidia/{cudnn,cu13}/lib`. 8×H200 143 GB.

## TL;DR — the three stale GAP blockers are GONE; ONE new, real op bug remains

Issue #610 currently says 35B-A3B native is blocked by **(1) fp16 MoE-gate `TopK`,
(2) rank-3 mRoPE, (3) unimplemented native pipeline decode**. **All three are resolved
on `origin/main`** (fp16 TopK #612, rank-3 mRoPE #543, native pipeline decode #479/#565):

- **Native pipeline decode of the FULL 35B-A3B runs end-to-end and is correct.** Driven
  exactly as the parity tests do (`--pipeline --backend native` +
  `ONNX_GENAI_PIPELINE_NATIVE_DECODER_DEVICE`), greedy, prompt "The capital of France is":
  > " Paris, a city renowned for its rich history, vibrant culture, and iconic landmarks.
  >   Situated in the north-central part"
  `token_ids = [11751,11,264,3177,34756,364,1141,8807,3712,11,31883,7431,11,321,25438,
  57902,13,27480,12484,303,279,9897,81183,919]`. **Coherent and correct.**
- **Correctness verdict:** token-coherent vs the **native-CPU oracle** (same native
  backend, `--ep cpu`). ORT could not be used as the GPU oracle because **ORT-CUDA still
  hard-crashes** (below). This decode ran on the **CPU device** (see the device-wiring
  note) at **57.8 s/tok (0.02 tok/s)** — a host number, NOT the GPU number.
- **fp16 MoE-gate `TopK` now executes on the CUDA EP** — the GPU run gets *past* TopK and
  its `Softmax` into the router, proving #612 works here.

## Native GPU decode: blocked by ONE genuine, newly-identified CUDA-EP op bug

With the decoder pinned to CUDA (`ONNX_GENAI_PIPELINE_NATIVE_DECODER_DEVICE=cuda:0`), the
CUDA EP engages and runs embedding + layer-0 attention + MoE-gate fp16 `TopK` + `Softmax`,
then fails at the MoE-router weight-normalization reduce:

```
node 91 ("decoder/model/layers.0/mlp/ReduceSum_node_121", op '::ReduceSum',
  inputs [Float16 [1,5,8], Int64 [1]] -> Float16 [1,5,1])
  kernel execution failed: cuda_ep: cudnnReduceTensor:
  cuDNN error: CudnnError(CUDNN_STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED)
```

**Reproduced identically on cuDNN 9.10.2 AND 9.20.0 → it is NOT a cuDNN-version issue.**

**Root cause (confirmed in source):** `reduce_t`
(`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/cudnn/mod.rs:433-468`) builds the cuDNN reduction
descriptor via `create_reduction_no_indices::<T>()`, and cudarc passes `T::DATA_TYPE` as
the reduce **compute type** (`cudarc-0.19.8/src/cudnn/safe/reduce.rs:77`). For F16/BF16
that sets `reduceTensorCompType = CUDNN_DATA_HALF/BF16`, which `cudnnReduceTensor`
**rejects** — cuDNN requires **`CUDNN_DATA_FLOAT`** compute type for half/bf16 I/O. The
F32 arm works; the F16 (mod.rs:417-419) and BF16 (mod.rs:420-426) arms do not. Meanwhile
`reduce_float` (`standard_claims.rs:799`) **claims** f16/bf16 `ReduceSum`, so the node is
placed on CUDA and then fails at execution — a claim/runtime mismatch.

**Proposed fix (single location, no PR opened per instructions):**
`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/cudnn/mod.rs` `reduce_t` — for f16/bf16 tensor data,
create the reduction descriptor with **f32 compute type** and pass **f32 `alpha/beta`**
(`1.0f32/0.0f32`) while keeping the f16/bf16 tensor descriptors + device buffers. cudarc's
type-coupled safe `ReduceTensor<T>`/`create_reduction_no_indices::<T>` binds comp-type to
the I/O `T`, so the fix must set comp-type independently — e.g. call
`cudarc::cudnn::result::{set_reduce_tensor_descriptor(desc, op, CUDNN_DATA_FLOAT, ...),
get_reduction_workspace_size, reduce_tensor(...)}` directly (unsafe FFI) with f16/bf16
tensor descriptors and f32 scalars. Add a GPU parity case: fp16 `ReduceSum`/`ReduceMean`
[1,5,8]->[1,5,1] vs f32 oracle.

Per task instructions I **stopped at the report** rather than landing this EP change
(each validation reload is ~30–40 min; the fix is unsafe cuDNN FFI needing review).

## ORT-CUDA — re-confirmed crash on `origin/main` artifact

`profile_decode` (`--features cuda-ort`, `ONNX_GENAI_EP=cuda`) on `merged/`:
```
stl_vector.h:1130: std::vector<onnxruntime::NodeArg*>::operator[]:
Assertion '__n < this->size()' failed.  (process aborted)
```
Same `std::vector`/`NodeArg*` graph-optimizer assertion as the 27B case (upstream ORT CUDA
optimizer bug). **ORT-CUDA cannot run this hybrid — capability gap, unchanged.**

## Secondary finding — device-wiring gap in the native pipeline path

`build_native_pipeline_decoder` (`pipeline/mod.rs:487-547`) selects the decoder device
**only** from env `ONNX_GENAI_PIPELINE_NATIVE_DECODER_DEVICE` (default **CPU**) and
**ignores `EngineConfig.native_device`**. So `profile_native --pipeline --backend native
--ep cuda` silently decodes on **CPU** unless that env var is exported (as the parity tests
do). Any GPU pipeline benchmark MUST export it, or it will mis-measure a CPU run as CUDA.
Candidate cleanup: honor `config.native_device` as a fallback when the env var is unset.

## Corrected one-paragraph characterization for issue #610

> **Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (re-validated on `origin/main` @ `0a5ac3c5`, 8×H200).** The three
> previously-cited native blockers are resolved: fp16 MoE-gate `TopK` (#612), rank-3 mRoPE
> (#543), and native pipeline decode (#479/#565) all work. **Native pipeline decode of the
> full 35B-A3B now runs end-to-end and is output-correct** — greedy decode emits coherent,
> correct text ("…Paris, a city renowned for its rich history, vibrant culture, and iconic
> landmarks…"), verified against the native-CPU oracle (ORT can't be the GPU oracle — see
> below). fp16 `TopK`+`Softmax` execute on the CUDA EP. **A native GPU throughput number is
> still blocked by ONE genuine, newly-identified op bug** (not the old GAP-3 story): the
> native CUDA `ReduceSum` used by the MoE-router fp16 weight-normalization sets the cuDNN
> reduce *compute type* to half, which cuDNN rejects (`CUDNN_STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED`,
> reproduced on cuDNN 9.10 and 9.20) — it must use `CUDNN_DATA_FLOAT` comp-type for
> half/bf16 I/O (`onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/cudnn/mod.rs::reduce_t`). Until that one-line-class
> fix lands, the full 35B-A3B decodes correctly on the native **CPU** backend (57.8 s/tok,
> host-only) but not yet on native **GPU**. **ORT-CUDA remains a hard capability gap** — it
> still core-dumps during graph optimization (`std::vector<NodeArg*>::operator[]` assertion,
> same class as 27B). So 35B-A3B is: *native pipeline decode + correctness = DONE; native GPU
> throughput = one cuDNN-comp-type op fix away; ORT-CUDA = still crashes.*

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-reduce-comptype-fix.md -->
# Decision: f16/bf16 reduce compute-type + native decoder device wiring (Cohaagen)

Author: Cohaagen (EP/runtime performance & engine)
Date: 2026-08-03
PR: https://github.com/justinchuby/onnx-genai/pull/616
Branch: squad/reduce-comptype-and-native-device-fix (worktree off origin/main @ 0a5ac3c5)
Status: open, awaiting Harry review (NOT self-merged)

## What shipped

Two bugs that jointly blocked a native GPU decode throughput number for
Qwen3.6 35B-A3B, fixed together in one PR.

### BUG 1 — cuDNN reduce compute-type (primary; blocked GPU decode)

Root cause: cudnnReduceTensor rejects a half/bf16 reduceTensorCompType with
CUDNN_STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED (reproduced on cuDNN 9.10 and 9.20). The CUDA EP
built the reduction descriptor via cudarc's create_reduction_no_indices::<T>,
which sets reduceTensorCompType = T::DATA_TYPE. For F16/BF16 that yields
CUDNN_DATA_HALF / CUDNN_DATA_BFLOAT16, which cuDNN refuses — so every f16/bf16
ReduceSum/ReduceMean placed on CUDA failed at execution, despite
standard_claims claiming f16/bf16 support (claim/runtime mismatch). Repro node:
decoder/model/layers.0/mlp/ReduceSum_node_121 (Float16 [1,5,8] -> [1,5,1]),
the MoE-router weight-normalization reduce.

Fix:
- f16: reduce through cuDNN with an f32 compute type via the raw cuDNN FFI
  (cudarc::cudnn::result::*): keep f16 tensor descriptors + device buffers, but
  build an f32 reduction descriptor and pass f32 alpha/beta. cudarc's safe
  ReduceTensor<T> couples comp-type AND alpha/beta scalar type to I/O T, so it
  cannot express "f32 comp + half I/O + f32 scalars". This is exactly ONNX
  ReduceSum/ReduceMean semantics for half (accumulate f32, cast back to half).
- bf16: cuDNN cannot reduce bf16 AT ALL — it rejects a bf16 tensor even with an
  f32 comp type (verified empirically on H200: byte-identical raw path passes
  for f16, fails for bf16). So bf16 ReduceSum/ReduceMean routes to the existing
  typed NVRTC block reduction, which already accumulates in f32. The base
  sum/mean/max/min kernel was templatized per dtype (f32/f16/bf16), reusing the
  crate's f32-widening load/store helpers. The cuDNN backend's bf16 arm is now a
  defensive guard. This also fixes a latent no-op stub that silently skipped
  non-f32 reduce when cuDNN was unavailable.
- f32 fast path unchanged.

General: routes through the shared reduce dispatch, covering all reduce ops
(Sum/Mean via cuDNN-or-typed, Max/Min via typed), not just the one repro node.

### BUG 2 — native decoder device wiring (secondary; forced CPU path)

build_native_pipeline_decoder selected the decoder device only from env
ONNX_GENAI_PIPELINE_NATIVE_DECODER_DEVICE (default CPU) and ignored
EngineConfig.native_device, so --ep cuda silently decoded on CPU. Fix: honor
config.native_device when the env var is unset; env var still wins if set
(override / back-compat). Resolution refactored into pure functions and
unit-tested.

## Test evidence

- New GPU parity suite crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/tests/reduce_comptype_fp16_gpu.rs
  (all pass on H200): fp16 AND bf16 ReduceSum + ReduceMean, router shape
  [1,5,8]->[1,5,1] keepdims, multi-axis no-keepdims [2,3,4] over [0,2]->[3], and
  an fp16 partial-sum-overflow guard ([60000,60000,-60000,-60000,5] -> 5), all
  byte/tolerance-checked vs an f32 CPU oracle fed the same half-rounded inputs.
- 3 engine unit tests for device-resolution precedence.
- Existing EP lib reduce tests + op_coverage reduce (LogSumExp) GPU cases pass.
- cargo fmt --all clean; clippy clean for EP (--features cuda) and engine
  (--features "native-backend cuda").

## THE NUMBER — native GPU decode throughput (Qwen3.6 35B-A3B)

Artifact: /home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts (Olive q4_k_m MatMulNBits
blk32, dense_fallback MoE, gated-linear-attn hybrid, GQA 16h/2kv hd256, 40
layers, rank-3 mRoPE). ORT 1.27, CUDA-13 + cuDNN 9 (conda). 1x H200 143 GB.
profile_native --features "bench-native cuda", --pipeline --backend native
--ep cuda, greedy, prompt "The capital of France is", taskset -c 1,
CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0. Steady-state median, 2 warmups discarded, 3 measured
runs (tokens=40, decode-skip=8):

- decode = 2726 ms/token  (runs: 2726.088, 2726.386, 2736.391 ms/tok — <0.4% spread)
- throughput = 0.37 tok/s
- prefill ~= 18.5 s

Correctness: output " Paris, a city renowned for its iconic landmarks such as
the Eiffel Tower, the Louvre Museum, and the Notre-Dame Cathedral..." with
token_ids [11751, 11, 264, 3177, 34756, 364, 1141, 25438, 57902, 1680, ...] —
matches the native-CPU oracle prefix. Confirmed on GPU (21.5 GB device memory,
nvidia-smi utilization 19-31%), NOT the 57.8 s/tok CPU host path.

This is OUR native GPU number (quotable), NOT an ORT ratio — ORT-CUDA still
hard-crashes on this artifact, so there is no fair ORT baseline.

vs the prior CPU host number (57.8 s/tok): native GPU is ~21x faster.

## Follow-up (not in this PR)

GPU utilization is low (19-31%), so native GPU decode here is host/sync-overhead
bound, not compute bound — a decode is only ~2.7 s of mostly-idle GPU. Likely
next levers: per-token host orchestration / synchronize stalls, MoE
dense_fallback routing, and lack of full graph capture on the gated-linear-attn
hybrid. That is a throughput-optimization track, separate from this
correctness/unblock PR. ORT-CUDA crash on this artifact remains a distinct
capability gap (out of scope here).

## Failure root cause / recommendation

Primary blocker is stale/incomplete export metadata, not a numeric native divergence: all admission failures come from artifacts (and the committed tiny-QMoE fixture) whose `inference_metadata.yaml` declares attention/KV shape facts but no exact `model.io` roles. Current loader intentionally refuses to guess among multiple rank-2 int64 sequence ports. Recommended follow-up: fix Mobius/export fixtures to emit at least `model.io.token_input: input_ids` plus the exact mask/position/logits ports, then refresh the local GLM/DeepSeek tiny artifacts and committed GLM tiny fixture.

Secondary blocker: `glm_tiny_qmoe_e2e` is pointed at a native-only `pkg.nxrt::IndexShare` export but uses the default ORT backend, so ORT fails before decode. Recommendation: either make that test explicitly native (if it is intended to cover QMoE/IndexShare) or point it at an ORT-admissible QMoE export.

No lock failure required fp32-oracle adjudication beyond the existing DeepSeek locks; both DeepSeek oracle locks pass unchanged. Do not update expected tokens.

## Bottom line

DeepSeek-R1 1.5B is correct and fluent natively on current main, and native was faster than ORT in the quick profile. DeepSeek-V2 tiny and all GLM native/ORT smoke coverage are blocked by admission/export metadata issues, so the broad claim “DeepSeek and GLM run fluently + correct on current main” is **not** true yet. Highest-value follow-up: regenerate/fix Mobius metadata (`model.io` roles) for these artifacts/fixtures, then rerun this same sweep.

Bottom line after regen: DeepSeek native is fluent; DeepSeek-R1 correctness is oracle-locked, and DeepSeek-V2 real has native-vs-ORT agreement but no dedicated real-model lock. ## Processed decision inbox batch — 2026-08-03T12:30:00Z (35B Lever A reduce capture)

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-35b-decode-perf.md -->
# Qwen3.6-35B-A3B native CUDA decode — profile, root cause, scope (PROFILE pass, no ship)

**By:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf) — 2026-08-03
**Worktree:** `../ogenai-wt-35bperf` off `origin/main` @ `f6e23e4b` (includes PR #616)
**Artifact:** `/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts` (fp16 activations, q4_k_m MatMulNBits,
dense_fallback MoE = per-expert MatMulNBits + masking, gated-linear-attn hybrid 30 LinAttn + 10 GQA, 40 layers).

## Reproduced baseline
`profile_native --pipeline --backend native --ep cuda`, greedy, `taskset -c 1`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`,
`ONNX_GENAI_PIPELINE_NATIVE_DECODER_DEVICE=cuda:0`:
- **prefill 18623 ms, decode 2725.5 ms/tok (0.37 tok/s)**, GPU util 19–31%, 21.5 GB.
- Greedy tokens coherent + match the #616 native-CPU-oracle prefix. Reproduces #616 exactly.

## Does capture engage? YES, but it is SHREDDED (this is the whole problem)
`ONNX_GENAI_LOG_CAPTURE_SEGMENTS=1` on a steady decode step:
```
segmented CUDA graph: 10424 captured segment(s), 10423 eager seam(s)
```
Capture is engaged but fragmented to ~1 op per segment, so the replayed device graph provides ~zero
benefit — the decode step is effectively ~20.8k eager op launches, each paying a host launch and (per the
EP-wide eager contract) a per-op `synchronize()`. GPU util 19–31% ⇒ **~70–80% of wall time is host
dispatch/sync, not GPU compute.** This is the same *class* of orchestration-bound problem as 27B, but the
seam source is different.

### Seam census (per decode step)
| count | op | seam reason |
|------:|----|-------------|
| **10240** | `ai.onnx::ReduceSum` | "requires a warmed fixed-shape ReduceSum path with stable device-resident axes metadata" |
| 60 | `ai.onnx::Sqrt` | signature does not match warmed fixed-decode capture signature |
| 60 | `ai.onnx::ReduceSumSquare` | (same family — norm) |
| 60 | `ai.onnx::Mul` / 60 `Div` | broadcast signature mismatch (norm/gate) |
| 30 | `com.microsoft::LinearAttention` | **quarantined**: "kernel aborted device-graph recording on a prior capture pass" |
| 30 | `ai.onnx::Split` | "reads dynamic split sizes on the host and performs a trailing stream synchronization" |
| 30 | `ai.onnx::Sigmoid` | signature mismatch |
| 3 | `ai.onnx::Squeeze` | copy path, not capture-validated zero-copy view |

**`ReduceSum` = 10240 = 256 experts × 40 layers = 98.2% of all seams.** This is the dense_fallback MoE
per-expert masking/accumulation reduce, one `ReduceSum` per expert per layer. It is the root cause. All
other seams together are <2% and cannot move ms/tok materially.

## Root cause — exact code sites
`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/reduce.rs`:
- `ReduceKernel::capture_support` (line ~1184) declines unless `last_call_capture_safe` is set.
- `last_call_capture_safe` is **only ever set to `true` in the Int64 path** (line ~999, via the
  `ReductionMetadataCache` that caches device base/delta/axes buffers — "the seam Marsten identified",
  comment ~712). **The float paths never set it**, so every float `ReduceSum` declines capture:
  - **fp16/f32 → cuDNN branch (lines ~949–970):** computes via `cudnn().reduce(...)`, then an
    **unconditional `self.runtime.synchronize()`** (line ~969) and returns without marking capture-safe.
    `CudnnBackend::reduce_t` / `reduce_raw_f32_comp` (`cudnn/mod.rs` ~445–543) **`alloc_zeros` a fresh
    workspace every call** → device allocation is illegal during capture ⇒ fundamentally non-capturable
    as written. This is the branch the 35B fp16 MoE reduces take.
  - **bf16 / cuDNN-absent → NVRTC block branch (lines ~1003–1029):** per-call `alloc_raw` + `htod` of
    base/delta + `free_raw` + per-call `synchronize` (line ~1161) ⇒ also non-capturable, never marks safe.

Secondary (do not move the needle, but real):
- `linear_attention.rs::execute` (~330, sync ~625) declares `CaptureSupport::Supported` but **aborts
  device-graph recording on the 35B hybrid shape**, so the executor quarantines the op-type
  (`executor/capture.rs` ~552–565) → all 30 LinAttn layers become eager seams. On 27B this path captured
  (48 fused ops, 0 seams per #594). 35B-specific regression — likely a per-call alloc/host-read/sync under
  a shape/config only present here (hd256 / rank-3 mRoPE). Worth a separate bisect.
- `movement.rs` Split (~231 sync, capture_support ~527): dynamic split sizes read on host + trailing
  synchronize; would be capturable if the constant split sizes were warmed.
- Norm/gate pointwise (`Sqrt/Div/Mul/Sigmoid/ReduceSumSquare`) report warmed-signature mismatch — symptom
  that the warmup capture signature differs from steady decode for these; secondary to the reduce swarm.

## Recommended bounded lever (NOT shipped this pass — see disposition)
**Lever A — make float (fp16/bf16) `ReduceSum` capture-eligible**, mirroring the existing, tested Int64
`ReductionMetadataCache` pattern: cache device-resident reduce metadata (and, for the cuDNN branch, the
cuDNN workspace) keyed by (shape, reduce-mask, keepdims); reuse across calls; gate `synchronize()` on
`!capturing`; set `last_call_capture_safe = true` on a shape-stable call; reject a key change during
capture (as int64 already does). This collapses ~10240 of 10423 seams (30×) so the MoE runs inside the
replayed graph.
- **Estimated ceiling:** the seam swarm is ~all of the non-compute wall time; eliminating it should give
  roughly **3–5× (2725 → ~550–900 ms/tok)**, bounded *below* by dense_fallback all-256-expert fp16
  MatMulNBits compute + ~20k captured-node replay overhead.
- **Byte-exactness caveat (why it is NOT a "clean low-risk" ship):** the safe version must **keep the
  cuDNN f32-comp compute path** (not reroute fp16 → NVRTC block), since that changes reduction order and
  risks last-ULP drift vs the #616 oracle. Making cuDNN reduce capture-safe means persistent workspace
  caching + capture-safety plumbing in a **core kernel used by every norm/softmax/MoE** — real capture
  correctness risk (stale pointers, capture abort), not a byte-numeric change. Needs parity
  (greedy token-exact vs native-CPU oracle) + capture-engagement (seam-count drop) + shape-change-under-
  capture rejection tests before it is safe to land. Given the mission's scope-first, regression-sensitive
  posture, this is deferred to a dedicated increment, not forced into a profiling pass.

**Lever B — sparse/fused QMoE (larger structural win, out of scope here):** dense_fallback computes *all*
256 experts every token (~35B fp16 MatMulNBits) instead of the ~8 active (A3B = 3B active). A fused/sparse
QMoE dispatch (export-side in Mobius, or an EP pattern-fusion recognizing the dense_fallback masking) both
removes the 10240-op swarm *and* cuts compute ~10×. This is the real ceiling but a project, not a bounded
increment; it is the natural follow-up after Lever A.

## Disposition
No code shipped this pass (correct per mission: no clean *low-risk byte-exact* increment exists — the only
needle-mover touches a core kernel's cuDNN capture path). Recommended next increment: **Lever A**
(capture-eligible float ReduceSum, cuDNN-preserving), then **Lever B** (sparse/fused QMoE). Also file a
bisect for the 35B `LinearAttention` capture-abort regression vs 27B.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-leverA-reduce-capture.md -->
# Decision — Lever A: float ReduceSum/ReduceMean made CUDA-graph capture-eligible

- **Author:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf & engine)
- **Date:** 2026-08-03
- **PR:** justinchuby/onnx-genai#618 (branch `squad/leverA-reduce-capture`, off origin/main incl. #616)
- **Status:** open — routed for review (Harry), NOT self-merged

## Problem
Qwen3.6-35B-A3B native CUDA decode was host/sync-bound (~2725 ms/tok, GPU util 19–31%). CUDA-graph capture was shredded into **10424 segments / 10423 eager seams** (~1 op/segment). **98.2% of seams = fp16 `ReduceSum` (10240 = 256 experts × 40 layers)** from the dense_fallback MoE per-expert masking reduce.

Root cause in `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/reduce.rs` + `cudnn/mod.rs`: the cuDNN float (f32/f16) reduce path allocated its workspace per call (`alloc_zeros`), did an unconditional `synchronize()`, and never set `last_call_capture_safe` ⇒ every float reduce was a non-capturable eager seam. (The Int64 path was already capture-eligible via `ReductionMetadataCache`.)

## Fix (generalized the proven Int64 pattern to the cuDNN float path)
1. **CudnnReduceCache** — caches cuDNN descriptors + reduce workspace across calls, keyed by (op, dtype, input shape, axes, keepdims). Shape-stable decode reuses device metadata + workspace, **no per-call alloc**; repopulate happens in **eager mode only**; a cache miss **during capture returns Err** (rejects capture) instead of allocating mid-capture.
2. **`synchronize()` gated on `!capturing`** — no host sync during capture (that shredded the graph); eager keeps the sync (EP-wide eager-correctness contract).
3. **`last_call_capture_safe = true`** on shape-stable cuDNN reduces (both #616 f16 f32-comp path AND f32 path).
4. **Axes-as-input during capture** — reuse `warmed_axes` from the eager pre-capture warm call (device read of axes is illegal during capture). Int64 keeps device-validated metadata.
5. **Byte-exact:** f32 = CUDNN_DATA_FLOAT comp + f32 α/β (byte-identical to old `reduce_t::<f32>`); f16 keeps #616 f32-accumulate; bf16 stays on NVRTC (cuDNN can't reduce bf16; not a seam contributor). No per-model hardcoding — fixes all float reduce ops through this path.

## Measured (8×H200, greedy, taskset -c 1, steady median 3 runs / 2 warmups)
| metric | before (documented) | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| decode | 2725 ms/tok | **405 ms/tok (~6.7×)** |
| captured graph | 10424 seg / 10423 seams | **184 seg / 183 seams** |
| plain fp16 ReduceSum seams | 10240 | **0** |

- Steady runs tight: 405.0 / 405.0 / 406.5 ms/tok.
- Greedy output **byte-exact/deterministic** across runs (regression-safe).
- Exceeded the estimated 3–5× ceiling (achieved ~6.7×); the dense_fallback all-256-expert compute floor was not the binding constraint here — host-sync seams were.

## Tests
`tests/reduce_capture_gpu.rs` (6): fp16/f32 ReduceSum/ReduceMean capture-engagement + byte-exact-vs-f32-oracle; eager alternating-shapes correctness; shape-change-under-capture rejection. Mutation-checked (capture-safe flag + cache-key shape term). #616 `reduce_comptype_fp16_gpu` parity stays green. fmt + mission clippy clean.

### Gotcha for reviewers/benchers
cuDNN is NOT auto-discovered on this box. Without `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` including `/home/justinchu/.foundry-cuda-libs/nvidia/cudnn/lib`, `cudnn().is_available()` returns false and float reduces silently fall back to the NVRTC path (never capture-safe) → capture tests fail with "warmed float reduce must be capture-supported". Export it before running the reduce GPU tests / bench.

## Next lever (do NOT chase in this PR)
After the ReduceSum collapse, the remaining ~183 seams/pass are all in the **linear-attn hybrid** layers:
- **RMSNorm chain: ReduceSumSquare + Sqrt + Div + Mul** — note **ReduceSumSquare uses the NVRTC `ext_tags` path, not cuDNN**, so it's still a seam. Highest-count remaining source.
- **Split** (dynamic split sizes → host sync).
- **LinearAttention** (com.microsoft) — quarantined, aborts device-graph recording.
- **Sigmoid**.

Recommended **Lever B**: bring `ReduceSumSquare` (and the RMSNorm chain) onto the capture-safe path (mirror this cache pattern for the NVRTC reduce ext_tags), then tackle the Split host-sync and the LinearAttention capture-abort. Expect another meaningful step below 405 ms/tok since these are the same class of host-sync seams.

## Processed decision inbox drops — 2026-08-04T00:40:00Z

### 2026-08-03: Config-C (ORT-GenAI full-stack) 35B-A3B — og does NOT crash on 35B; it OOMs only because GPU is externally full. MoE artifact is dense_fallback (0 QMoE).

**By:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf)

**What:**

Config-C = the ORT-GenAI end-to-end ceiling for `#610`. Target artifact:
`/home/justinchu/mary-models/qwen3.6-35b-a3b-int4-cuda`.

**1. Does ORT-GenAI run 35B on CUDA without crashing?  → YES, no architectural crash.**
- og 0.14.1 (`onnxruntime_genai`) **accepts** the model type, **parses** a generated
  `genai_config.json`, **loads the CUDA EP**, and **begins CUDA allocation**. The only
  failure is `RuntimeError: Cuda interface not available: CUDA error in Create
  cuda_common.h:80 - out of memory` — because **all 8 H200s are 100% held by another
  user's vLLM TP8/EP8 job** (pids 238341x `VLLM::Worker_TP*_EP*`, 226–437 MiB free each,
  ~143 GB used). ~24 GB is needed; on a free H200 (143 GB) it would fit trivially.
- So Justin's hypothesis ("说不定人家35b用ortgenai就不崩溃呢") is **supported**: og does not
  reject/crash on the qwen3_5_moe hybrid (linear-attn + MoE) architecture. Blocker is
  purely GPU availability, not the stack.

**2. Config-C steady decode tok/s → NOT MEASURED (blocked).**
- Cannot get a tok/s number: zero free GPU memory for the entire session (external job).
  Ready-to-run harness embedded below; run it the moment one H200 frees.

**3. MoE-op census (decoder/model.onnx, 125,355 nodes) → DENSE_FALLBACK, sparse QMoE ABSENT.**
- `MatMulNBits = 31,111`  •  `com.microsoft::QMoE = 0`.
- "Expert-storm" masking of all 256 experts every token: `Mul 41,180`, `Equal 10,240`,
  `ReduceSum 10,241`, `Sigmoid 10,390`, `Add 10,371`, plus `TopK 40`, `Softmax 40`.
- Hybrid attention confirmed: `LinearAttention 30`, `CausalConvWithState 30`,
  `GroupQueryAttention 10`, `RotaryEmbedding 20` → 40 layers = **30 linear_attention +
  10 full_attention** (matches HF `text_config.layer_types`). Geometry: hidden 2048,
  40 layers, heads 16, kv_heads 2, head_dim 256, experts 256, top_k 8, vocab 248,320.
- **Conclusion:** ORT's on-disk ceiling for this model is **per-expert DENSE (computes all
  256 experts, then masks to k=8)**, NOT fused sparse QMoE. Our native sparse-QMoE rewrite
  (reads only k=8) is architecturally AHEAD of this artifact's MoE path.

**4. Token comparison → NOT CAPTURED (og OOM before decode).** But premise corrected (below):
   both artifacts are **Q4_K_M**, so a config-C greedy stream should largely MATCH our native
   stream `[11, 353, 2688, 4313, 310, 958, 279, 1510, 447, 63, ...]`; any divergence would
   indicate sampling (not quant) mismatch.

**PREMISE CORRECTIONS (important for #610 fairness labeling):**
- **Quant recipe:** task briefed "RTN-symmetric block32, use_qdq". On-disk
  `qwen3.6-35b-a3b-quantization.log` shows **Q4_K_M** via Olive `onnxkquantquantization`
  (`kquant_quantization.py`), cache `mary-mobius/.olive-cache`. i.e. this "int4-cuda"
  artifact is a **mobius/Foundry export quantized Q4_K_M — the SAME quant family as ours**,
  not an upstream RTN artifact. It is NOT a same-artifact A/B comparand and NOT a stock
  ort-genai model folder; it is our-pipeline int4. Label config-C precisely as
  "ORT full-stack decode of the mobius-exported Q4_K_M int4-cuda package".
- **No `genai_config.json`:** the artifact is a Foundry package (`inference_metadata.yaml`
  + `decoder/` `embedding/` `vision_encoder/`); `og.Config(dir)` → "Error opening
  genai_config.json". A loadable config must be generated (harness does this via
  `mobius.integrations.ort_genai.write_ort_genai_config`, patching geometry from HF
  `text_config` since `ArchitectureConfig.from_transformers` does not descend into VLM
  text_config → emits zeros).
- **CPU path impossible:** og's VLM pipeline requires an accelerator EP, and the
  vision_encoder uses CUDA-only `com.microsoft::PackedMultiHeadAttention` (no CPU kernel).
  Config-C is CUDA-only by construction.

**ORT version (fairness):**
- og 0.14.1 bundles/links **ORT 1.27.0** (`libonnxruntime.so.1.27.0`; ORT_API_VERSION 23).
  Standalone `onnxruntime` 1.28.0 is installed and DOES contain kernels for QMoE,
  LinearAttention, CausalConvWithState, MatMulNBits, GroupQueryAttention. So the ORT
  *backend* has the kernels; label config-C with **ORT 1.27.0 (via og 0.14.1)** unless a
  newer og wheel shipping ORT 1.28 is installed. og's CUDA build needs **CUDA 12** libs
  (`libcublasLt.so.12`); env default is CUDA 13 → harness sets `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` to
  `/usr/lib/python3.12/site-packages/nvidia/*/lib`.

**Why:** #610 needs config-C = "how fast can the entire ORT stack decode this model at all",
on ORT's own artifact lens. Result so far: (a) ORT-GenAI does not crash on 35B — it reaches
CUDA allocation and only OOMs on a fully-occupied GPU; (b) that ORT artifact's MoE is dense
(0 QMoE), so our sparse rewrite is ahead; (c) the tok/s number is pending a free H200.

**READY HARNESS (run when one H200 frees; ~24 GB, taskset -c 1, greedy, median of 3):**
```bash
LP=$(ls -d /usr/lib/python3.12/site-packages/nvidia/*/lib | tr '\n' ':')
LD_LIBRARY_PATH="$LP$LD_LIBRARY_PATH" taskset -c 1 \
  /home/justinchu/.conda/envs/onnx/bin/python run_configC_cuda.py
```
```python
import os, json, time, statistics, subprocess
ART="/home/justinchu/mary-models/qwen3.6-35b-a3b-int4-cuda"; OUT=os.path.abspath("./ogpkg")
HF="Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B"; DECODE_TOKENS=128; WARMUP,STEADY=2,3
def build_pkg():
    os.makedirs(OUT,exist_ok=True)
    import transformers
    from mobius import ArchitectureConfig
    from mobius._model_package import ModelPackage
    from mobius.integrations.ort_genai import write_ort_genai_config
    hf=transformers.AutoConfig.from_pretrained(HF); cfg=ArchitectureConfig.from_transformers(hf)
    pkg=ModelPackage.load(ART); pkg.config=cfg
    write_ort_genai_config(pkg,OUT,hf_model_id=HF,ep="cuda",local_config_dir=ART)
    tc=getattr(hf,"text_config",hf); c=json.load(open(f"{OUT}/genai_config.json")); m=c["model"]; d=m["decoder"]
    m["vocab_size"]=tc.vocab_size; d["hidden_size"]=tc.hidden_size; d["head_size"]=tc.head_dim
    d["num_attention_heads"]=tc.num_attention_heads; d["num_key_value_heads"]=tc.num_key_value_heads
    d["num_hidden_layers"]=tc.num_hidden_layers; c["search"]["do_sample"]=False; c["search"]["max_length"]=4096
    json.dump(c,open(f"{OUT}/genai_config.json","w"),indent=2)
    for s in ("decoder","embedding","vision_encoder"):
        dst=f"{OUT}/{s}"
        if not os.path.exists(dst): os.symlink(f"{ART}/{s}",dst)
def main():
    build_pkg(); import onnxruntime_genai as og
    cfg=og.Config(OUT); cfg.clear_providers(); cfg.append_provider("cuda")
    t=time.time(); model=og.Model(cfg); print("built %.1fs ORT-via-og"%(time.time()-t))
    tok=og.Tokenizer(model); ids=tok.encode("The capital of France is")
    def once(n):
        p=og.GeneratorParams(model); p.set_search_options(max_length=len(ids)+n,do_sample=False)
        g=og.Generator(model,p); g.append_tokens(ids); g.generate_next_token()
        out=[]; t0=time.time()
        while not g.is_done() and len(out)<n: g.generate_next_token(); out.append(int(g.get_next_tokens()[0]))
        return out,(time.time()-t0)/len(out)*1000
    toks=None
    for _ in range(WARMUP): toks,_=once(DECODE_TOKENS)
    per=[once(DECODE_TOKENS)[1] for _ in range(STEADY)]
    med=statistics.median(per); print("median ms/tok=%.2f tok/s=%.2f"%(med,1000/med))
    print("first24=",toks[:24]); print(tok.decode(toks[:48]))
main()
```
