# Decision — 35B-A3B QMoE sparse-decode measurement (BLOCKED at ORT load) + same-build dense re-baseline

**Author:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime performance & engine)
**Date:** 2026-08-03

### 2026-08-03: 35B-A3B QMoE native decode is BLOCKED at pipeline load; dense re-baselined at 400.8 ms/tok (same build)

**By:** Cohaagen

**What:**

Attempted to measure native CUDA decode on the sparse QMoE artifact
(`/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts`, 40 `com.microsoft::QMoE`
nodes) on a fresh `origin/main` worktree @ `25c2c8e2` (includes #616, #618
Lever A, #621 Lever B). Build: `cargo build --release -p onnx-genai-bench
--features bench-native,cuda --bin profile_native`.

**The QMoE artifact cannot be loaded through the standard native pipeline path.**
It fails *immediately at model load*, before any GPU/cuDNN/kernel runs:

```
taskset -c 1 profile_native --pipeline --backend native --ep cuda \
  --model /home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts \
  --steady --warmups 2 --runs 3 --tokens 128 --decode-skip 8

Error: load pipeline engine .../qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts
Caused by: Failed to load pipeline models: ORT error: Load model from
  .../decoder/model.onnx failed: Type Error: Type parameter (T) of Optype
  (QMoE) bound to different types (tensor(float16) and tensor(float)) in
  node (moe.layer0.qmoe). (code: 1)
```

**Root cause (precise):**

1. The QMoE artifact is authored for the **native onnx-genai QMoE contract**, not
   ORT's. The CUDA kernel `onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/qmoe.rs`
   (`qmoe_linear_f16`: `const __half* input`, `const float* scales`,
   `const float* router_probs`) **requires fp16 activations + fp32 scales +
   fp32 router_probs** (see qmoe.rs; the export note upcasts fp16→fp32 scales
   "which the kernel requires — lossless"). Verified node `moe.layer0.qmoe`:
   `input`/`output` = FLOAT16, `fc1_scales`/`fc2_scales` = FLOAT (fp32),
   `router_probs` = FLOAT (fp32, from a `Cast`).
2. ORT 1.27's `com.microsoft::QMoE` binds **input, router_probs, scales, output
   all to one type parameter `T`**, so fp32 scales/router_probs + fp16
   activations is an invalid graph → ORT rejects it at `Session` load. This is
   an *intentional* divergence: the native kernel's mixed-precision contract is
   not ORT's QMoE contract.
3. The blocker is the **loader layer, not the kernel**:
   `onnx-genai-ort/src/loader.rs::PipelineModels::load_with_options` eagerly
   constructs an ORT `Session::new(...)` for **every** component — including the
   native decoder — via `PipelineEngine::build` (pipeline/mod.rs:715), even for
   `--backend native`. Native decode itself runs off `NativeComponentSession`
   loaded from disk (onnx-runtime-ir), but `DecodeState` IO/KV resolution
   (`decode/resolved_io.rs::resolve_with_positions` → `session.inputs()/
   outputs()`) and `validate_fixed_state_budget` are wired to that ORT
   `Session`. So even though the CUDA QMoE kernel exists and fits the artifact,
   the graph never gets past ORT's load-time type-check.

**Consequence:** No native GPU QMoE decode number could be produced. This is NOT
a silent CPU fallback — it is a hard load-time rejection.

**Fix (well-scoped follow-up, not a measurement hack):** For native-backend
components, resolve decoder IO/state-budget from the native loader
(onnx-runtime-ir / `NativeComponentSession`, already used for the decode forward)
instead of an ORT `Session`, and skip the redundant ORT `Session::new` for
components that `model_requires_native_backend`. That decouples native decode
from ORT's op type-checker and lets ORT-incompatible native ops (this
fp16-act/fp32-scale QMoE, and future native-only ops) load. This is a real
engine change and should ship + be locked by a test — I did not hack it in to
manufacture a number.

**Same-build dense re-baseline (task #3, airtight apples-to-apples anchor):**

Identical protocol/build/GPU on the dense artifact
(`/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts`), which ORT *can* load
(MatMulNBits):

```
steady_run 1: decode=399.731 ms/token  2.50 tok/s
steady_run 2: decode=411.534 ms/token  2.43 tok/s
steady_run 3: decode=400.771 ms/token  2.50 tok/s
steady_median: decode=400.771 ms/token  throughput=2.50 tok/s
              (prefill=14649 ms, runs=3 warmups=2 decode_skip=8)
```

- **GPU-confirmed:** GPU0 66% util, 19.9 GB resident during decode (nvidia-smi),
  compute-app PID present — genuinely on GPU, not CPU fallback.
- Confirms the ~405 ms prior baseline is current on `origin/main` @ 25c2c8e2
  (400.8 ms/tok). The dense native GPU path (embedding + native pipeline decoder)
  works end-to-end here; the cuDNN 9 (cu13) + CUDA 13.3 runtime must be on
  `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`.
- Dense-GPU greedy token stream (prompt "Hello", 128 tokens) captured as the
  reference the QMoE-GPU stream MUST match once the loader is fixed:
  `[11, 353, 2688, 4313, 310, 958, 279, 1510, 447, 63, 1654, 314, ...]`
  ("... I'm trying to use the `get` method of the `requests` library ...").

**Byte-exactness (task #2):** Cannot be executed while load is blocked. The
rewrite is provably byte-identical to the dense subgraph (Deckard's export note:
bit-identical int4 weights/zero-points, lossless fp16→fp32 scale upcast), so once
the loader fix lands, QMoE-GPU tokens should equal the dense-GPU stream above
token-for-token. That equality remains the required proof and is not yet
demonstrated.

**Repro env:**
`ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR=/home/justinchu/onnx-genai/.ort-cuda-1.27/root/lib`,
`LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/local/cuda-13.3/targets/x86_64-linux/lib:/home/tlwu/cudnn9.12_cu13/lib:$ORT_LIB_DIR`.

**Why:** The QMoE sparse-decode win (~32× less MoE weight HBM traffic at rows==1)
is real and the CUDA kernel supports the artifact's exact dtype layout, but it is
currently unreachable through the product's native pipeline because the loader
forces an ORT graph load that rejects the native-contract QMoE graph. The number
that would go into #610 is gated on the loader decoupling above; publishing a
number from a hand-hacked divergent path would not be the airtight same-build,
same-product comparison #610 requires.

---

### 2026-08-03 (update) — native-only QMoE load unblocked; GPU measurement blocked

**By:** Cohaagen

**What:** Landed the loader fix (PR #625, branch `squad/native-loader-skip-ort`)
that lets native-backend pipeline components skip the redundant ORT `Session`
that ORT 1.27's `com.microsoft::QMoE` type-checker was rejecting at load (fp16
I/O + fp32 scales/router_probs bound to one type param `T`). The QMoE decoder's
decode I/O / KV layout / state budget now resolve from a backend-neutral
`GraphIo` seam (`GraphIoMetadata` parsed from the ONNX graph) instead of the
absent ORT session. Loader-side regression test proves a native-only-op
component loads with no ORT session.

**Why:** Native decode already runs off `NativeComponentSession`; the ORT session
for that component was pure redundancy but still aborted the whole pipeline load.
The CUDA kernel `qmoe_linear_f16` matches the artifact exactly — the blocker was
the loader, not the math.

**MEASUREMENT STATUS — BLOCKED (external):** The QMoE decode speedup vs the
same-build dense baseline (400.8 ms/tok reference) and the byte-exact greedy
parity check (QMoE-GPU must equal dense-GPU stream `[11, 353, 2688, 4313, 310,
958, 279, 1510, 447, 63, ...]` for >=32 tokens) could NOT be run: all 8 H200s
are fully occupied (~142.9/143.8 GB each, ~437 MiB free) by another user's
(adnagara@) vLLM tensor-parallel job (PIDs 2383412-2383419, `VLLM::Worker_TP*`,
running 2h+). The 21 GB QMoE decoder cannot be resident with <1 GB free, and I
must not kill another user's processes. Polled for ~20 min with no change.

**Reproducible measurement command (run once a GPU has >= ~30 GB free), from the
worktree with the release build already compiled:**

env:
- ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR=/home/justinchu/onnx-genai/.ort-cuda-1.27/root/lib
- LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/local/cuda-13.3/targets/x86_64-linux/lib:/home/tlwu/cudnn9.12_cu13/lib:$ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR
- CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free gpu index>

QMoE decode:
  taskset -c 1 target/release/profile_native --pipeline --backend native --ep cuda
    --model /home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts
    --steady --warmups 2 --runs 3 --tokens 128 --decode-skip 8

Same-build dense baseline (apples-to-apples ratio):
  taskset -c 1 target/release/profile_native --pipeline --backend native --ep cuda
    --model /home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts
    --steady --warmups 2 --runs 3 --tokens 128 --decode-skip 8

Byte-exact gate: compare each run's printed `generated_token_ids`. QMoE-GPU must
equal dense-GPU token-for-token for >=32 tokens (and the dense stream should
reproduce `[11, 353, 2688, 4313, 310, 958, 279, 1510, 447, 63, ...]`). Report
median ms/tok + tok/s and the QMoE/dense speedup. If they diverge, STOP — that is
a real bug in the rewrite or the sparse kernel path, not something to paper over.

Build (if the worktree is rebuilt):
  cargo build --release -p onnx-genai-bench --features bench-native,cuda --bin profile_native

---

### 2026-08-03 — ORT 1.28 verdict + load-unblock confirmed (measurement still GPU-blocked)
**By:** Cohaagen

**ORT 1.28 QMoE type-check verdict — STILL REJECTED.**
Rebuilt/ran under the now-required ONNX Runtime 1.28.0 (API 27, confirmed at
runtime: `selected ONNX Runtime 1.28.0 ... libonnxruntime.so.1.28.0`). A minimal
synthetic QMoE model (fp16 input/output, fp32 router_probs/scales, uint8
weights/zero_points, swiglu_fusion=2) loaded via a plain CPU `Session::new`
reproduces the EXACT original error under 1.28:

    Type Error: Type parameter (T) of Optype (QMoE) bound to different types
    (tensor(float16) and tensor(float)) in node ().  (code: 1)

=> ORT 1.28 did NOT relax the single-`T` binding on com.microsoft::QMoE. The
direct-load path is NOT available. The loader fix (skip the redundant ORT
Session::new for native-backend components) remains REQUIRED. **Path taken:
loader-fix (PR #625), validated under ORT 1.28.**

**Load-unblock PROVEN end-to-end under ORT 1.28 (GPU-independent evidence).**
Ran the real 35B-A3B QMoE artifact through the patched loader:

    ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR=.ort-cuda-1.28/root/lib (+ cuda-13.3/cudnn9.12 LD prefix)
    target/release/profile_native --pipeline --backend native --ep cpu \
      --model /home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts --tokens 4 --warmups 0 --runs 1

Result: the pipeline LOADS (previously aborted at ORT `Session::new`'s QMoE
type-check), admission passes, the native decoder builds WITHOUT an ORT session,
and native decode runs all the way into the QMoE op:

    node 79 ("moe.layer0.qmoe", op 'com.microsoft::QMoE', inputs [Float16, Float32,
    Uint8, Float32, ..., Uint8, Uint8]) ... => reached native executor.

The only remaining CPU error is a CPU-kernel dtype contract
(`QMoE: input requires Float32, got Float16`): the CPU QMoE kernel
(crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/qmoe.rs:125) is fp32-input-only, whereas
this artifact is authored fp16-input for the CUDA kernel
(`qmoe_linear_f16`, `const __half* input`). That is a CPU-kernel limitation, NOT
the loader blocker — the loader fix has done its job (load + reach native QMoE).
GPU (CUDA kernel) is the target path and does accept fp16 input.

**GPU measurement + GPU byte-exact: STILL BLOCKED** on the same adnagara@ vLLM
TP8 job (PIDs 2383412-2383419, 142 GB/GPU x8, util 0 but memory pinned; ~437 MiB
free per GPU). Must not kill another user's job. The reproducible command block
above stands; only change: use `.ort-cuda-1.28/root/lib` for ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR
(1.28 is now the required version for all numbers).

---

### 2026-08-04 — #610 configs A/B (ORT 1.28, greedy/temp=0)
**By:** Cohaagen

#610 fairness expansion asks for up to three same-artifact configs with identical
greedy sampling. For this 35B QMoE task, configs A and B on ORT 1.28 (config C /
standalone ORT-GenAI is a separate harness, skipped here).

**Config B = our engine + ORT backend (`--backend ort`) on the SAME QMoE artifact
— CAPABILITY GAP (not a speed number).** Rebuilt profile_native with
`--features bench-native,bench-ort,cuda` and ran (greedy, `--temperature 0`):

    target/release/profile_native --pipeline --backend ort --ep cpu \
      --model /home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts --tokens 1 --temperature 0

Result (ORT 1.28.0, API 27; ORT EPs available: Tensorrt, CUDA, CPU):

    Failed to load pipeline models: ORT error: Load model from
    .../qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts/decoder/model.onnx failed:
    Type Error: Type parameter (T) of Optype (QMoE) bound to different types
    (tensor(float16) and tensor(float)) in node (moe.layer0.qmoe). (code: 1)

=> Under the ORT backend, ORT 1.28 CANNOT LOAD the native fp16-act+fp32-scales
QMoE graph at all — there is no ORT ms/tok for this artifact. This is the honest
#610 capability gap; NOT dressed up as a speed ratio. The rejection is
`VerifyTypeConstraint` at graph-resolve (EP-independent, before any GPU/kernel
assignment), so `--backend ort --ep cuda` fails identically at load; demonstrated
on CPU because the GPU is blocked and the error is pre-EP, hence fully
representative. (This is the same blocker the loader fix exists to route around
for config A / native.)

**Config A = our engine + native CUDA EP** — the primary number + byte-exact vs
the dense-GPU stream. Still pending a free GPU; the background watcher now runs
A (QMoE native CUDA), a faithful GPU-side B attempt (QMoE `--backend ort --ep
cuda`), and the same-build DENSE native baseline — all greedy (`--temperature
0`), writing `generated_token_ids` to gpu-measure-results.txt for token-for-token
comparison. Sampling is pinned identically across configs; A and B are required
to produce the SAME token stream (B currently produces none — it can't load).

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-leverB-nvrtc-reduce-capture.md -->

# Decision — Lever B: NVRTC reduce capture-eligibility (35B decode seam collapse)

- **Author:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime performance & engine)
- **Date:** 2026-08-03
- **PR:** squad/leverB-nvrtc-reduce-capture (onnx-genai) — see PR link in the branch
- **Status:** shipped (candidate A); candidate B investigated + rejected (unsafe)
- **Follows:** #616 (cuDNN reduce comp-type), #618 (Lever A: float ReduceSum cuDNN capture-eligibility)

## TL;DR

Re-profiled Qwen3.6-35B-A3B native CUDA decode on a fresh worktree off `origin/main`
(#618 present). The top remaining capture seam is `ReduceSumSquare` on the **NVRTC**
reduce path (Lever A only fixed the **cuDNN** path). Fixed it byte-exact by routing the
NVRTC path through the existing shape-keyed metadata cache (mirror of #618). **Throughput
is FLAT** — the honest, measured finding is that post-Lever-A **35B decode is compute-bound
on the captured MoE `MatMulNBits` segments, not seam/sync-bound**. Shipping A as a correct,
tested capture-hygiene generalization (no throughput claim). Candidate B (LinearAttention
un-quarantine) is **unsafe** (byte divergence) and was reverted.

## Exact seam breakdown (this is what the profile said — I did not assume)

Config: `--pipeline --backend native --ep cuda`, greedy, 8×H200, `taskset -c 1`, steady
median of 3 / 2 warmups, `ONNX_GENAI_LOG_CAPTURE_SEGMENTS=1`. Post-#618 capture =
**184 captured segments / 183 eager seams**.

Per decode pass, over the 30 linear-attn hybrid layers, the eager seam **nodes** are:

| op (seam node) | nodes/pass | eager wall (per-op profile) | why it seams |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ReduceSumSquare (NVRTC)** | **60** | **6.78 ms / 60 calls** (TOP, ~3× next) | per-call device `alloc`+`htod`+`sync`+`free` of i64 offset tables |
| Sigmoid | 30 | 2.30 ms / 110 | fixed-decode elementwise signature miss (per-head shape) |
| Split (dynamic) | 30 | 2.26 ms / 40 | reads split sizes on host + trailing stream sync |
| LinearAttention | 30 | 1.92 ms / 30 | quarantined recurrent kernel, unconditional `synchronize()` |
| Div | 60 | 1.44 ms / 60 | elementwise signature miss |
| Sqrt | 60 | 1.43 ms / 60 | elementwise signature miss |
| Squeeze | 3 | — | copy-path seam |

Node total per pass ≈ 2×(RSS+Sqrt+Div) + Split + LinearAttention + Sigmoid + 2×Mul gate
per layer × 30 + 3 Squeeze = 333 seam nodes, which merge (adjacent seams coalesce) into the
**183 eager segments** in the header. **The header "183 seams" is the eager-SEGMENT count,
not the node count** — an important distinction for reading the before/after.

**Wall-time ranking (not just count):** `ReduceSumSquare` dominates and is the *only* seam op
carrying per-call device alloc/htod overhead. This **disproved the mission hypothesis** that
the LinearAttention abort would dominate wall-time — it does not.

## What I fixed (candidate A)

`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/reduce.rs`: unified the NVRTC block-reduction path
(Int64 DATA reduce + every float/bf16/extended reduce) through the one shape-keyed
`ReductionMetadataCache` (renamed field `int64_metadata` → `reduce_metadata`; the base/delta
tables are dtype-agnostic i64 offset tables):

- shape-stable decode reduce reuses cached device tables — **no per-call alloc/htod/free**;
- trailing `synchronize()` **gated on `!capturing`**;
- `last_call_capture_safe.store(true)` so the segmenter folds it in;
- mid-capture signature change **rejected by `prepare()`** (no realloc inside capture);
- only the Int64 DATA reduce still `validate_captured_axes` (it reads the axes device buffer);
  the float/bf16 reduce bakes the mask into base/delta and never reads axes.

Byte-exact: NVRTC reduce already f32-accumulates; only allocation lifetime + capture gate change.

## Before / after (35B, steady median of 3)

| | ms/tok | tok/s | seg / eager seams | RSS seam **nodes**/pass | greedy tokens |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline (#618) | 417.7 | 2.39 | 184 / 183 | 60 | reference |
| after (A) | 418.0 | 2.39 | 184 / 183 | **0** | **40/40 identical** |

- **Throughput FLAT** (within ~1–2% run noise). RSS seam **nodes** 60 → 0, but eager-**segment**
  count unchanged: each RSS node lived inside an RMSNorm eager region still flanked by
  `Sqrt`/`Div` seams, so removing RSS shrinks the region without eliminating the segment.
## Correctness verdict

- **Change-level byte-exactness (mandatory, and satisfied): GPU before == GPU after = 40/40
  identical tokens.** A is numerically inert (NVRTC reduce already f32-accumulates; only
  allocation lifetime + capture gate change).
- **Native-CPU oracle (matched flags, greedy):** first 7 tokens identical, then GPU vs CPU
  **diverge at token index 7** (CPU `8807` vs GPU `25438`).
  CPU: `[11751, 11, 264, 3177, 34756, 364, 1141, 8807, 3712, 11, 31883, 7431, 11, 321]`
  GPU: `[11751, 11, 264, 3177, 34756, 364, 1141, 25438, 57902, 1680, 430, 279, 242476, …]`
- ⚠️ **This GPU-vs-CPU divergence is PRE-EXISTING, not caused by this PR.** The GPU *baseline*
  (#618) emits the same GPU token `25438` at index 7, and GPU before==after is 40/40. It is a
  known native-CUDA-vs-native-CPU **MoE/fp16** numerical difference (close expert-routing /
  accumulation flips a greedy token, then sequences diverge). **Flagged to Justin — not
  silently accepted.**
  - **Follow-up (separate from this PR):** justify the CUDA MoE path vs an **fp32/CPU oracle**
    (is the GPU token the fp32-correct greedy choice, or the CPU one?) and lock the verdict with
    a regression test. This is orthogonal to seam hygiene — A does not touch numerics — so it
    should not block this capture-eligibility change, but it is real and should be picked up.

## Candidate B — LinearAttention 35B capture-abort — REJECTED (unsafe)

The mission flagged the quarantined `LinearAttention` (30 layers eager, "captured fine on 27B
#594") as a suspected regression. Root: `linear_attention.rs:625` issues an unconditional
`synchronize()` that aborts capture recording. Gating it on `!capturing`:

- **does** un-quarantine all 30 layers (segments 184 → 154), **but**
- **changes greedy output** — diverges at ~token 37 (`1880` vs byte-exact `1814`).

The sync is **load-bearing**: it masks a recurrent-state read/write hazard in the captured
path (the 35B rank-3 state shape differs from 27B). Byte-exactness is mandatory → **reverted**.
The quarantine is correctly protecting correctness; this is not a "free" win. A real fix must
make the recurrent state update capture-safe (double-buffer / hazard-free ordering), not just
drop the sync — that is a larger, separate change.

## Next lever (for the following increment)

**Not further seam collapse.** The remaining seams (Sqrt/Div/Sigmoid elementwise signature
misses; Split host-sync; LinearAttention quarantine) are each either low-ceiling or unsafe, and
decode wall time did not move when the top seam's sync was removed. The dominant decode cost is
now the **captured MoE `MatMulNBits` / dense-fallback compute** itself. The next increment
should profile and attack MoE matmul compute (e.g. dequant/gemm kernel efficiency, expert
routing, tile/occupancy) — that is where the ms/tok now lives. If LinearAttention un-quarantine
is revisited, it must come with a hazard-free capture-safe recurrent-state update + a byte-exact
regression lock, not a sync removal.

## Tests

`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/tests/nvrtc_reduce_capture_gpu.rs` (6 GPU tests, mirror of #618):
numeric parity (f16/f32/bf16 RSS vs f32 oracle, multi-axis/keepdims/overflow), capture-engagement,
alternating-shape cache correctness, shape-change-under-capture rejection. Mutation-checked
(flip `store(true)`→`store(false)` → 5 tests fail; reverted). fmt + clippy clean.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-native-loader-fix.md -->

### 2026-08-03

**By:** Cohaagen

**What:** Made the pipeline loader skip building a redundant ORT `Session` for
components the engine executes on the native backend, and resolve those
components' decode I/O / KV layout / state budget from a new backend-neutral
graph-I/O seam instead of the (now-absent) ORT session. This unblocks loading
any native-only-op pipeline component — the 35B-A3B QMoE decoder today (ORT
1.27's `com.microsoft::QMoE` type-checker rejects its fp16 I/O + fp32
scales/router_probs contract at `Session` build), and future custom ops
tomorrow. General, no per-model or per-op hardcoding.

The seam:
- `crates/onnx-genai-ort/src/session/mod.rs` — new `GraphIo` trait
  (`inputs`/`outputs`/`input_names`/`output_names`) implemented for both the ORT
  `Session` (returns its own resolved I/O) and a new session-free
  `GraphIoMetadata` (declared graph I/O parsed straight from the ONNX
  `ModelProto` graph.input/graph.output — no ORT, no weight materialization).
- `crates/onnx-genai-ort/src/loader.rs` — `PipelineModels::load_with_ort_session_filter(root, opts, build_ort_session: impl Fn(&str)->bool)`.
  A component the predicate rejects is captured as `GraphIoMetadata` instead of
  an ORT `Session`. `graph_io(component)` returns the ORT session's I/O when
  present, else the metadata — one accessor both backends read through.
  `load_with_options` delegates with `|_| true` (ORT path byte-identical).
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/pipeline/mod.rs` — `PipelineEngine::build`
  calls `load_with_ort_session_filter(dir, opts, |_| false)` when the resolved
  `PipelineBackend` is `Native` (skip every ORT session), else the unchanged
  `load_with_options`. Decoder KV/DecodeState metadata now resolves through
  `models.graph_io(&ar.decoder)`.
- Load-path resolvers converted from `&Session` to `&dyn GraphIo`:
  `decode/resolved_io.rs`, `decode/values.rs`, `decode/state.rs`,
  `kv_bridge.rs` (`infer_kv_model_info` / `resolve_kv_layers` /
  `require_present_kv_output` / `require_kv_input`), and the routing/flat-AR
  metadata reads (`routing.rs` `build_step_bindings` /
  `resolve_optional_decoder_extras` / `prepare_request_tensors`;
  `flat_autoregressive.rs` `new_decoder_state`). `&Session` auto-coerces to
  `&dyn GraphIo`, so ORT callers are untouched. The ORT decode *run* path
  (`OrtPipelineDecoder`, paged ORT KV mirroring, prompt-phase `.run()`) still
  takes a real `&Session`, fetched only on the ORT branch so native decode never
  requires the skipped session.

**Why:** Native decode already runs off a disk-loaded `NativeComponentSession`
(onnx-runtime-ir) — the ORT `Session` for that component was pure redundancy, yet
its construction still tripped ORT's op type-checker and aborted the whole
pipeline load. The kernel (`qmoe_linear_f16`: `const __half* input, const float*
scales, const float* router_probs`) matches the artifact exactly; the failure is
in the loader, not the math. Gating ORT-session construction on the resolved
backend (not on a hardcoded op list) keeps the fix general: any component the
engine runs natively skips ORT, and its decode contract is sourced from the same
ONNX graph the native backend loads. Package admission (which runs onnx-std
shape inference during directory load, before any session build) is unchanged and
still validates every component's graph, so the skip only drops the redundant
ORT kernel-resolution step.

Regression test: `crates/onnx-genai-ort/tests/pipeline_loader.rs::native_only_component_loads_without_ort_session`
— a pipeline whose decoder carries a native-only `pkg.nxrt::BlockQuantizedMatMul`
op (valid graph, shape-inferable, but no ORT kernel) fails `load_with_options`
(ORT rejects at session build) yet loads cleanly through
`load_with_ort_session_filter(..., |c| c != "decoder")`: `session("decoder")` is
None, `graph_io("decoder")` exposes the decoder's I/O, and the ORT-backed encoder
keeps its session. Locks the fix against future regressions.

Validation: `cargo fmt --all` clean; `cargo clippy -p onnx-genai-ort --features
cuda` and `-p onnx-genai-engine --features "native-backend cuda"` both `-D
warnings` clean; full `onnx-genai-ort` suite passes (the one unrelated failure,
`flat_directory_with_unrelated_manifest_remains_backward_compatible`, is
pre-existing on origin/main — verified in a clean origin/main worktree — and is
untouched by this change). GPU load/run + QMoE decode measurement + byte-exact
greedy parity vs the dense-GPU stream are pending an available GPU (all 8 H200s
are currently held by another user's vLLM job); results appended to
`cohaagen-35b-qmoe-measure.md` once measured.

---

### 2026-08-03 — ORT 1.28 validation
**By:** Cohaagen

ORT 1.28.0 (now the required version) still binds com.microsoft::QMoE's
input/router_probs/scales/output to one type param `T`; a plain CPU
`Session::new` on a synthetic fp16-act+fp32-scales QMoE model reproduces the
exact original "Type parameter (T) ... (tensor(float16) and tensor(float))"
rejection under 1.28. So the loader fix is still required (direct-load is not
available). Confirmed end-to-end under 1.28: the real 35B-A3B QMoE artifact now
LOADS through the patched loader (`--backend native`, ORT session skipped for the
native decoder) and native decode reaches the QMoE op (node 79) — previously the
load aborted at ORT `Session::new`. The binary is ORT-version-agnostic (ORT is
dlopen'd at runtime via ONNX_GENAI_ORT_LIB_DIR), so switching 1.27->1.28 needs
only the env var; no rebuild. GPU perf + byte-exact remain pending a free GPU.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/cohaagen-qmoe-sparse-decode-design.md -->

# QMoE Sparse Decode — Qwen3.6-35B-A3B design & plan (Cohaagen)

**Scope:** read-only scoping pass. No product code, no PR. Author: Cohaagen (EP/runtime perf).
**Date:** 2026-08-03. **Artifact:** `/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts/` (dense_fallback MoE, 405 ms/tok after #616/#618).

---

## 0. TL;DR — the answer that unblocks dispatch

- **The CUDA `QMoEKernel` decode path is ALREADY sparse.** For a single decode token (`rows == 1`) it reads **k=8** expert weight matrices per FC layer from HBM, **NOT E=256**. The per-token expert weight traffic is `k/E = 8/256 = 1/32` of dense.
- **Therefore work item (b) — an onnx-genai decode sparse-dispatch kernel — is NOT required.** The kernel already does top-k gather + per-route GEMV at `rows==1`. The only thing standing between us and A3B speed is the **export**: the artifact emits `dense_fallback` (per-expert `MatMulNBits` + `Equal`/`ReduceSum` masking), which computes **all 256 experts every token**.
- **Only work item (a) — the mobius export change — is on the critical path.** mobius *already has* a full `com.microsoft::QMoE` emission path and an expert-weight repacker; it is simply gated off for this model (Olive quant + unconditional per-expert unpack). Deckard's change is small and localized.

---

## 1. CRITICAL QUESTION — decode: sparse or dense today? (k vs E HBM reads)

**Sparse. k, not E.** Traced end-to-end in `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/qmoe.rs`:

`impl Kernel for QMoEKernel::execute` (qmoe.rs:1010):
- Expert **grouping is built only when `rows > 1`**: `let grouping_sizes = (rows > 1).then(|| …)` (qmoe.rs:1209). For decode (`rows == 1`) `grouping` is `None`.
- With `grouping == None`, execute takes the **`else` branch** (qmoe.rs:1335) which calls `launch_linear(… expert_counts = None …)` for fc1, (fc3), fc2 — the "Phase-1 per-route GEMV path" named in the file header (qmoe.rs:3-5).
- `launch_linear` → kernel `qmoe_linear_impl` (qmoe.rs:264). Its work domain is `tasks = routes * out_features` where **`routes = rows * k`** (qmoe.rs:1197). For `rows==1`, `routes = k = 8`.
- Inside the loop each task does `expert = selected_experts[route]` (qmoe.rs:291) and dequant-GEMVs **only that routed expert's weight row** `expert_row = expert*out_features + output_feature` (qmoe.rs:301, 308-320). There is **no loop over all experts**. Experts that aren't routed are never touched.
- The `expert_counts[expert] >= gemm_min_tokens` guard (qmoe.rs:292-295) only *hands large-count experts off to the grouped GEMM* in the prefill path; at decode `expert_counts` is null so the per-route GEMV owns every route.

**Quantified HBM reads at `rows==1`, E=256, k=8:**

| Stage | Expert matrices read (dense_fallback today) | Expert matrices read (CUDA QMoE) |
|---|---|---|
| fc1 (gate+up) | 256 | **8** |
| fc2 (down) | 256 | **8** |
| per layer | 512 | **16** |
| whole model (×40 MoE layers) | 20,480 | **640** |

`qmoe_grouping.rs`'s "prefill" header is accurate — the *grouping/gather* helpers (count/prefix/assign/gather + tiled `qmoe_grouped_linear_f32` in `qmoe_gemm.rs`) are the `rows>1` batched path. Decode bypasses them entirely and is already the sparse per-route GEMV. The decode path is also **capture-eligible** once warmed (`capture_support`, qmoe.rs:1393): it is a single templated kernel (`qmoe_linear_{f32,f16,bf16}`) parameterized by bits/block_size/zero-points, not per-expert-compiled — fits the #618 CUDA-graph pattern.

> **Conclusion:** switching the export to QMoE is *sufficient* to realize A3B decode. No kernel change needed. (b) is optional future optimization only.

---

## 2. QMoE op I/O contract our CUDA kernel expects

Source: `QMoEFactory`/`QMoEKernel` (qmoe.rs:777-1403), `MoeAttributes` (qmoe.rs:658-727), `QuantizedExperts::validate` (qmoe.rs:908). Domain `com.microsoft`, 7–21 inputs, 1 output.

**Inputs (index → name, by position):**
| idx | name | dtype | shape | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | input | f32/f16/bf16 | `[rows,H]` or `[B,S,H]` | output dtype must match |
| 1 | router_probs | **float32** | `[rows, E]` | raw router logits (see routing) |
| 2 | fc1_experts_weights | **uint8** | `[E, fc1_out, H*bits/8]` | packed int, expert-major |
| 3 | fc1_scales | **float32** | `[E, fc1_out, H/block_size]` | |
| 4 | fc1_experts_bias | f32 | `[E, fc1_out]` | optional |
| 5 | fc2_experts_weights | uint8 | `[E, H, inter*bits/8]` | required |
| 6 | fc2_scales | float32 | `[E, H, inter/block_size]` | required |
| 7 | fc2_experts_bias | f32 | `[E, H]` | optional |
| 8/9/10 | fc3_experts_weights/scales/bias | | `[E, inter, H]`… | **only** unfused SwiGLU or SiLU-gated (`uses_separate_gate`) |
| 11 | fc1_experts_zero_points | uint8 | `[E, fc1_out, ceil((H/block)*bits/8)]` | optional (asymmetric) |
| 12 | fc2_experts_zero_points | uint8 | `[E, H, ceil((inter/block)*bits/8)]` | optional |
| 13 | fc3_experts_zero_points | uint8 | | optional |
| 14 | router_weights | float32 | `[rows, E]` | optional; **CUDA ignores it** (softmax-top-k on input 1); CPU gathers it |
| ≥15 | | | | reserved for FP4/FP8 modes → **hard error if present** (qmoe.rs:1032) |

**Attributes:**
- `k` (int > 0) — top-k experts per token.
- `expert_weight_bits` ∈ **{1,2,4,8}** (qmoe.rs:784). We use **4**.
- `block_size` — **power-of-two, ≥16** (qmoe.rs:790). We use **32** ✓.
- `quant_type` — string, **must be `"int"`** (integer-affine). FP4/MXFP4/IQ rejected (qmoe.rs:802-809).
- `activation_type` ∈ {relu, gelu, silu, swiglu, identity} (qmoe.rs:635) → we use **`swiglu`**.
- `swiglu_fusion` ∈ {0,1,2}, only valid with swiglu (qmoe.rs:689-699):
  - `0` = separate gate tensor via **fc3** (`uses_separate_gate`), fc1 width = inter.
  - `1` = **interleaved** gate/linear in fc1 (`[…2*f, 2*f+1]`), fc1 width = 2*inter.
  - `2` = **concatenated halves** in fc1 (`gate = fc1[..inter]`, `linear = fc1[inter..]`), fc1 width = 2*inter. **← what mobius emits.**
- `normalize_routing_weights` (bool) — renormalize selected top-k weights.
- `activation_alpha` (def 1.0), `activation_beta` (def 0.0), `swiglu_limit` (def +inf) — SwiGLU clamp/shape (qmoe.rs:418-430).
- `use_sparse_mixer=1` → **unsupported** (qmoe.rs:684).

**Dequant math (must match exactly):** `w = ((q:uint - zero_point) * scale)` per int4 nibble; default `zero_point = 1<<(bits-1) = 8` when zero_points absent (symmetric); block index `= depth/block_size` (qmoe.rs:169-239, `decode_affine_weight` / `qmoe_int4_chunk`). This is the **same affine convention as `MatMulNBits`**, so dense_fallback→QMoE is byte-equivalent given a faithful repack.

**Routing plumbing (contract mobius must honor):** pass **raw float32 logits** as `router_probs` (input 1); CUDA QMoE internally does softmax→top-k→(optional normalize) and ignores input 14. This reproduces a softmax-topk router (`Softmax(TopK(logits))`) exactly. Selection is by raw logit (monotonic). This is precisely what `TopKGate.qmoe_routing`/`SoftmaxTopKGate.qmoe_routing` already emit in `mobius/src/mobius/components/_moe.py:39-110`.

---

## 3. What the current dense_fallback 35B graph looks like (measured)

Op census of `decoder/model.onnx` (structure only): `MatMulNBits ×31,111`, `Mul ×41,180`, `Sigmoid ×10,390`, `Equal ×10,240`, `ReduceSum ×10,241`, `TopK ×40`, `Softmax ×40`, plus `LinearAttention ×30`, `CausalConvWithState ×30`, `RMSNormalization`, etc.

Derived MoE geometry (from initializer/attr inspection):
- **E = 256** routed experts, **k = 8** (`TopK` k-input == `[8]`), **40 MoE layers** (40 TopK/Softmax; hybrid stack: linear-attn + full-attn).
- **hidden H = 2048**, **moe_intermediate = 512**. Experts store **separate gate & up** (`MatMulNBits` K=2048,N=512) + **down** (K=512,N=2048).
- **block_size = 32, bits = 4** on every expert `MatMulNBits` ✓ (blk32 int4).
- **Per-expert weight footprint** (int4 blk32): fc1(gate+up) = 1024×2048 → ~1.05 MB packed + 0.26 MB scales; fc2(down) = 2048×512 → ~0.52 MB + 0.13 MB scales; ~2.0 MB/expert incl. zero-points.
- 256 × 40 × ~2.0 MB ≈ **19.2 GB** of routed-expert weights — i.e. **~97% of the 19.78 GB `model.onnx.data`.** The rest = shared experts, attention/linear-attn projections, embeddings, lm head, norms.

**dense_fallback per-MoE-layer structure** (from `MoELayer.forward`, `_moe.py:375-395`):
`router = Softmax(TopK(MatMul(h, gateᵀ)))`; then **for each of 256 experts**: `expert_out = MLP_i(h)` (3× `MatMulNBits` + SwiGLU), `mask = Equal(selected, i)`, `w = ReduceSum(routing*mask)`, `acc += expert_out * w`. Shared expert is a **separate always-on MLP** scaled by `sigmoid(shared_expert_gate(h))` (`Qwen2MoELayer.forward`, `moe.py:292-299`) — it stays dense and is **outside** the QMoE node.

**dense_fallback → QMoE mapping (byte-equivalent):**
- 256 per-expert `{gate,up,down}` `MatMulNBits` → one `com.microsoft::QMoE` node with expert-major `fc1_experts_weights=[256,1024,1024]`, `fc2_experts_weights=[256,2048,256]` (+scales, +optional zero_points), `k=8`, `expert_weight_bits=4`, `block_size=32`, `activation_type="swiglu"`, `swiglu_fusion=2` (gate=first 512, up=second 512). `fc1_out = 2*inter = 1024`.
- Router `Softmax(TopK(logits))` → feed **raw logits** as `router_probs`, `normalize_routing_weights` per gate (`TopKGate` uses softmax-of-topk; verify vs HF Qwen3.6 router — softmax gate, low risk).
- `Equal`/`Mul`/`ReduceSum` masking + weighted sum → **absorbed inside QMoE** (combine kernel, qmoe.rs:515-541).
- Shared expert + sigmoid gate → **unchanged** (kept as dense MLP + `Sigmoid`/`Mul`/`Add`).

---

## 4. Weight repack plan (Olive q4_k_m MatMulNBits blk32 → QMoE ABI)

**Is blk32 already QMoE-compatible?** Yes for the *quant scheme*: block_size 32 is power-of-two ≥16 ✓, bits 4 ✓, quant_type integer-affine ✓, same `(q - zp)*scale` dequant as MatMulNBits ✓. **No requantization; no accuracy loss** — this is a pure *layout* transform.

**What changes is layout, expert-majorness, and the un-split:**
- MatMulNBits stores each projection **per-expert, separately**, as `weight=[N, n_blocks, blob_size]` uint8, `scales=[N, n_blocks]`, `zero_points=[N, ceil(n_blocks*bits/8)]` (see `preprocess_olive_weights`, `_weight_utils.py:709-804`, `blob_size = group_size*bits/8 = 16`).
- QMoE wants **expert-major fused 3-D** tensors: `fc1_experts_weights=[E, fc1_out, H*bits/8]`, flattening the `(n_blocks, blob_size)` inner dims into one packed axis, with **gate and up concatenated** along `fc1_out` (fusion=2), and down as `fc2`.

**Mechanics (already implemented in mobius):**
- `pack_qmoe_expert_weights` (`_weight_utils.py:592-633`) maps `experts.gate_up_proj.{weight,scales,zero_points}`→`fc1_*` and `experts.down_proj.*`→`fc2_*`, and `flatten(-2)` collapses `[…, n_blocks, blob_size]`→`[…, n_blocks*blob_size]` = the QMoE packed inner axis. `MoELayer._init_qmoe_parameters` (`_moe.py:293-339`) declares exactly these shapes incl. the `ceil(...*bits/8)` zero-point width.
- **Nibble/pack order:** ORT MatMulNBits and QMoE share the same little-endian nibble packing (low nibble = element `2i`), verified by the CUDA decode (`qmoe_int4_chunk`, qmoe.rs:220-238) and `decode_affine_weight` — no nibble swap needed. Transpose: none beyond the existing MatMulNBits `[N, …]` output-major layout, which QMoE also expects (rows = output features).
- **Zero-point convention:** q4_k_m is asymmetric → carry `fc1/fc2_experts_zero_points` (uint8, packed 2/byte). If a projection is symmetric, omit zero_points and the kernel defaults zp=8 (must match how Olive dequantized — verify per-tensor; symmetric-vs-asymmetric mismatch is the one real accuracy trap).

**Accuracy gate:** because scheme is identical, the QMoE node must match the dense_fallback graph **to fp32 rounding**. Validate with a byte/near-exact oracle: same packed weights fed to (dense per-expert MatMulNBits path) vs (QMoE node) must agree within int4-dequant fp32 tolerance on identical inputs. Lock a golden logits fixture. No "justified-more-accurate" case expected — this is a faithful re-layout, so hold **byte-exact vs the dense_fallback oracle**.

**The actual gap for Qwen3.6 (why it exported dense):** `Qwen35MoECausalLMModel.preprocess_weights` (`qwen35.py:369-386`) **unconditionally** un-fuses `experts.gate_up_proj`/`down_proj` into per-expert `experts.{i}.*` tensors → forces the `MLP`-list dense path. And `_supported_qmoe_quantization` (`_moe.py:398-409`) only accepts `quant_method ∈ {gptq, awq}` — **Olive (`quant_method="olive"`) is excluded**, so even fused weights wouldn't hit QMoE. DeepSeek already shows the correct pattern (`deepseek.py:448-484`): a `use_qmoe` guard that *keeps* the fused expert-major tensors and calls `pack_qmoe_expert_weights`.

---

## 5. Realistic decode ceiling (HBM-weight-bound estimate + honest caveats)

**Weight-traffic model (MoE is ~97% of bytes):**
- Dense_fallback reads **all** experts every token: ~19.2 GB expert weights + ~0.6 GB non-MoE ≈ **19.78 GB/token**.
- QMoE sparse reads k=8/256: `8×~2.0 MB×40 ≈ 0.64 GB` expert + ~0.6 GB non-MoE ≈ **~1.2 GB/token** → **~16.5× less total** weight traffic (32× on the MoE fraction alone).

**But decode is not bandwidth-saturated today.** 19.78 GB / 405 ms ⇒ ~49 GB/s effective — an order of magnitude under any datacenter GPU roofline (A100 ~2 TB/s, H100 ~3.3 TB/s). So the 405 ms is dominated by **dense_fallback overhead**, not HBM: per token it launches 256 per-expert `MatMulNBits` GEMVs **plus** ~256×(`Equal`+`Mul`+`ReduceSum`) elementwise masking **per layer ×40** — tens of thousands of ops and full-expert compute that QMoE collapses into **one fused node doing only k=8 GEMVs**.

**So the QMoE switch wins on both axes simultaneously:** 32× less expert weight traffic **and** ~256→8 GEMVs + elimination of the masking op storm.

**Ceiling estimate (be honest, must be measured):**
- Pure-roofline for 1.2 GB/tok: ~0.4 ms (H100) / ~0.6 ms (A100) — an unreachable lower bound.
- Realistic batch=1 caveat: the decode GEMV assigns one CUDA block per `(route, out_feature)` = `k*fc1_out + k*H = 8*1024 + 8*2048 ≈ 24.6k` blocks/layer, each a memory-bound `in_features`-length reduction. Good parallelism and capture-eligible, but **rows=1 GEMVs do not saturate HBM** (latency/occupancy-bound, poor arithmetic intensity), so expect to land well above the roofline.
- **Defensible target: ~25–60 ms/tok (≈7–16× e2e)** after (a), gated by attention + linear-attention state updates + KV + the always-on shared expert + small-GEMV efficiency. Best case toward the low end if non-MoE is cheap and the per-route GEMV occupies well.
- **Caveat/HARD RULE:** these are estimates from static byte counts + code reading. The **405 ms baseline and the post-switch number must be measured on an origin/main GPU worktree** (local main is stale; do not trust local perf). Treat §5 numbers as sizing, not commitments.

---

## 6. Concrete work split + dependencies + validation

### (a) mobius export change — **critical path, Deckard implements, Justin merges (never self-merge)**
Files: `mobius/src/mobius/models/qwen35.py`, `mobius/src/mobius/components/_moe.py`, `mobius/src/mobius/_weight_utils.py` (already has the repacker).
Changes (mirror `deepseek.py:448-484`):
1. **Extend `_supported_qmoe_quantization`** (`_moe.py:398-409`) to accept the Olive q4_k_m config (add `"olive"` to the allowed `quant_method` set, keep `bits==4`, `not float_zero_point`, block_size power-of-two ≥16). Confirm Olive symmetric/asymmetric → zero_points presence.
2. **Guard the per-expert un-fuse in `Qwen35MoECausalLMModel.preprocess_weights`** (`qwen35.py:369-386`) with a `use_qmoe` check: when QMoE is active, **keep `experts.gate_up_proj`/`down_proj` fused/expert-major** and route through `pack_qmoe_expert_weights` (after the Olive MatMulNBits reshape) instead of splitting into `experts.{i}.*`.
3. Ensure the routed gate exposes `qmoe_routing` (TopKGate/SoftmaxTopKGate already do) and that `normalize_routing_weights`/softmax semantics match HF Qwen3.6 (correctness checkpoint). Shared expert + `shared_expert_gate` stay dense — unchanged.
4. **Regenerate** the 35B-A3B artifact (same Olive weights, re-export) → expect the MoE `MatMulNBits` storm to collapse to **40 fused `QMoE` nodes** (verify: `grep`/scan `model.onnx` for `QMoE`, expect ~256×40 fewer `MatMulNBits`, zero per-expert `Equal`).

### (b) onnx-genai QMoE decode sparse-dispatch — **NOT NEEDED (deferred/optional)**
Per §1 the CUDA decode path is already sparse (k, not E) and capture-eligible. **No blocker work.** Only-if a future profile shows the batch=1 per-route GEMV under-occupies: consider a decode-specialized grouped GEMV / warp-per-route layout (Cohaagen implements, Harry reviews) — but this is a *later* optimization, not required to realize A3B speed. Do not schedule it against (a).

### Ordering / dependencies
- (a) is **independent and sufficient**; it does **not** need (b). They are not even both required.
- **Validate the kernel path is production-ready BEFORE the 35B regen** using the existing tiny fixtures — no dependency on the 19 GB re-export:
  - `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/glm_tiny_qmoe_e2e.rs` (CPU ORT-contrib QMoE, structural e2e; built by `mobius/export_glm_tiny_qmoe.py`).
  - `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/glm_tiny_qmoe_native_cuda_e2e.rs` (**native CUDA** eager-decode regression against committed fixture `tests/fixtures/tiny-glm52-qmoe-indexshare/`, reproducible via its `generate.py`). This directly exercises the `rows==1` sparse decode kernel — run:
    `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1 cargo test -p onnx-genai-engine --features cuda,native-backend glm_tiny_qmoe`.
  - CPU parity: `grouped_moe_measures_benefit_over_dense_fallback_decode_and_prefill` (`crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/moe.rs:919`) already asserts grouped/sparse beats dense_fallback.
- **Validation plan (glm_tiny_qmoe first, then 35B):**
  1. Land/verify (a) on a **tiny** Qwen3.6-MoE-shaped (or reuse glm_tiny) Olive-quant export → confirm fused `QMoE` node emitted + byte-exact vs dense_fallback oracle on CPU.
  2. Run the native-CUDA glm_tiny_qmoe decode regression → confirm sparse decode correctness + capture.
  3. Only then regenerate the 35B-A3B artifact; smoke via the engine (prefill + N decode) and **measure ms/tok on an origin/main GPU worktree** vs the 405 ms baseline.

---

## 7. Sources (citations)
- Decode-sparse proof: `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/qmoe.rs:1209,1271-1369` (grouping `rows>1`, decode `else`→`launch_linear`), `:264-330` (`qmoe_linear_impl` per-route expert read), `:1197` (`routes=rows*k`).
- I/O contract: qmoe.rs:777-1007 (`QMoEFactory`/`QuantizedExperts::validate`), `:658-727` (`MoeAttributes`), `:418-486` (swiglu/activation), `:1032-1041` (FP4/FP8 reject).
- dense_fallback + QMoE emit + repack: `mobius/src/mobius/components/_moe.py:247-409`, `mobius/src/mobius/models/moe.py:268-299`, `mobius/src/mobius/models/qwen35.py:220-390`, `mobius/src/mobius/models/deepseek.py:448-484`, `mobius/src/mobius/_weight_utils.py:592-633,709-804`.
- 35B geometry: op census + initializer/attr inspection of `/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts/decoder/model.onnx` (E=256, k=8, 40 MoE layers, H=2048, moe_inter=512, blk32/int4).
- Validation assets: `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/glm_tiny_qmoe_e2e.rs`, `glm_tiny_qmoe_native_cuda_e2e.rs`, `tests/fixtures/tiny-glm52-qmoe-indexshare/`, `crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cpu/src/kernels/moe.rs:919`.

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** Handle ModelOpt `MIXED_PRECISION` detection in PR #446's `is_modelopt_quant_config`.
**Why:** ModelOpt writes `quant_algo="MIXED_PRECISION"` (plus a `quantized_layers` map and a `producer.name="modelopt"` stamp) when a checkpoint mixes formats — exactly the NVFP4-MoE + FP8-attention Qwen3.6 target. That string contains neither `NVFP4` nor `FP8`, so the old substring test returned `None` and silently mis-routed the checkpoint. The predicate now also keys on `producer.name` and `quantized_layers` (both ModelOpt-only) so detection no longer rests solely on `quant_method=="modelopt"`.

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** Move `is_modelopt_quant_config` into a dependency-free `integrations/modelopt/_detect.py`; make the package `__init__` load the numeric `_dequant` symbols lazily (PEP 562 `__getattr__`).
**Why:** The predicate is a pure dict check but lived in `_dequant.py`, so importing it dragged `numpy` + `ml_dtypes` onto the core config path (`QuantizationConfig.from_transformers`, which runs for every model). Splitting the predicate out and lazy-loading the numeric core lets `_configs/_quantization.py` import the predicate at module scope while keeping numpy/ml_dtypes off that path. Verified: importing the package + calling the predicate does not import `_dequant`.

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #451 dense-fallback for a *quantized* MoE that misses the QMoE ABI raises `NotImplementedError` rather than attempting to split fused quantized experts.
**Why:** `_supported_qmoe_quantization()` returns `None` for non-int4 / float-zero-point / unsupported-method / non-pow2-block configs. In that case the fused quantized expert tensors (`...experts.{gate_up_proj,down_proj}.{qweight,scales,qzeros}`) can neither be repacked into QMoE nor un-fused into per-expert dense params, and would otherwise pass through to a confusing weight-load mismatch. Note: a non-pow2/<16 block size can't even construct the model (attention `QuantizedLinear` enforces the same pow2>=16 rule), so the reachable triggers are bits!=4 or float zero-points; the guard covers all of them and fails loudly. Splitting fused quantized experts is deferred (no test checkpoint; out of scope for a docstring PR).

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #444 gemma4 audio `audio_features` docstring — describe conditional rank instead of "fix forward".
**Why:** `forward()` is correct: rank-2 `[num_valid_frames, H]` on the export path (mask supplied) and unmodified rank-3 `[B, T//4, H]` when `input_features_mask` is None (bare eager call). Only the docstring overclaimed rank-2 unconditionally, so the docstring was reconciled to the real behaviour; no code change.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/deckard-447-450-review.md -->

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #447 — merged `origin/main` into `feat/fp8-kv-cache` (was CONFLICTING). Sole conflict was a `_builder.py` `build()` docstring: kept both main's `qwen3_5_moe_vl (Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B)` `text_only` sibling and the branch's `fp8_kv_cache` / `kv_cache_scales` param docs.
**Why:** main and the FP8 branch both extended the same contiguous docstring block (`text_only` siblings vs the two new FP8 params), so git couldn't auto-merge. Both additions are independent and correct, so the resolution is a union — no code semantics changed. PR is now MERGEABLE.

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #447 — the only outstanding review fix was tianleiwu's: `Fp8KvCachePass.__init__` now runs `_validate_scales()` on the supplied `kv_cache_scales` map (finite + strictly positive; rejects 0/neg/NaN/inf/malformed with `ValueError`). Added `TestDirectScaleMapValidation` (direct-map + build-path regression).
**Why:** `build()`/`build_from_module()`/`optimize_model()` accept a direct `kv_cache_scales` dict that bypassed the positivity/finiteness checks in `load_kv_cache_scale_file()`, so a programmatic caller could emit a corrupt FP8 scale. `__init__` is the shared boundary for BOTH entry paths (file-loaded scales also flow through it), so validating there enforces one contract. The other four unresolved Copilot threads (scale-name collision L75, docstring-vs-impl graph-input contract L124, SM89 test gate L43, `_optimizations.py` FP8-capability gate L586) were already fixed by earlier commits on the branch (49cd272 / 3efd9c6 / 15382d1); I verified each against current code + tests and replied+resolved rather than re-fixing.

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #450 — in `TextModel.forward`, moved the `output_layer_indices` intermediate-hidden-state capture to AFTER the DeepStack injection `Add` (was before). Added `TestDeepStackCaptureOrdering`.
**Why:** HuggingFace records `all_hidden_states` at the top of the decoder loop, so `hidden_states[k+1]` is layer k's output WITH the DeepStack contribution already added. Capturing pre-injection made requested intermediate layers within the DeepStack range miss that contribution and diverge from HF `output_hidden_states`. Only `TextModel` has the capture logic; the `qwen35.py` decoder loops inject DeepStack but never capture, so no change there. Non-DeepStack models are unaffected (the `Add` is skipped).

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #450 — corrected the `qwen_vl.py` `Qwen3VL3ModelVisionModel.forward` comment: `Qwen3VLVisionModel` ALWAYS returns a tuple (`return merged, *deepstack_features`) — a 1-tuple `(merged,)` when `deepstack_visual_indexes` is empty, never a bare tensor.
**Why:** The old comment claimed a bare-tensor return without DeepStack layers, which is wrong and would mislead future tuple-handling changes. The `isinstance(outputs, tuple)` guard is now documented as a defensive fallback; the `if not deepstack_features: return merged` branch is what actually handles the empty-DeepStack case. Comment-only fix; return contract unchanged.

### 2026-08-03
**By:** Deckard
**What:** PR #448 (Cosmos3-Edge) — no action needed. 0 review threads, MERGEABLE; lint clean on all 12 changed files and `-k cosmos` build/coverage tests pass (12 passed, 2 skipped). Left untouched; not pushed.
**Why:** Task was to confirm it is clean/mergeable and needs nothing. Verified, nothing to change.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/deckard-glm404-review.md -->

### 2026-08-03: mobius PR #404 review closeout — merge main + resolve remaining threads
**By:** Deckard
**What:**
- Merged `origin/main` into `glm5.2-moe-export` (branch was CONFLICTING). Two conflicts, both docstring/comment-only:
  - `src/mobius/_builder.py`: kept main's `qwen3_5_moe_vl` text-only note AND the GLM branch's `config_overrides` docstring.
  - `src/mobius/integrations/ort_genai/auto_export.py`: kept the GLM `"glm_moe_dsa": "decoder"` mapping and took main's more-accurate "Qwen VL model families have separate ORT GenAI model types" comment (the old GLM comment was stale after main changed qwen3_vl mappings).
- Thread "`_sparse_bias` Add(attention_bias, sparse) can underflow → use elementwise min": resolved as **no longer applicable**. The dense ScatterElements additive-mask path was replaced by the `pkg.nxrt::IndexShare` lowering; causality is carried entirely by the (already-causal) `topk_indices`, so no second dtype.min mask is added. The only remaining `op.Add(scores, causal)` is a single add of the causal bias onto FLOAT indexer scores before TopK — not a two-mask intersection — so the underflow concern does not apply.
- Thread "`--glm-full-attention` has no CLI test / side effects not explicit": a focused test (`tests/cli_test.py::test_glm_full_attention_passes_config_overrides`) already asserts `config_overrides == {use_dsa: False, num_nextn_predict_layers: 0}`. Made the MTP side-effect explicit in the CLI `--help` text (docs already covered it in `glm52-export.md`).
**Why:** The branch head already contained Justin's substantive fixes (symmetric zero-point contract, asymmetric QMoE wiring, g_idx forwarding, portable export paths, onnx-genai `{model,mtp}` dispatch). Remaining work was the main-merge, verification, and closing the two un-answered threads. Prefer main's version for stale comments; keep GLM feature lines. The IndexShare rewrite structurally eliminates the additive-mask underflow class, so an elementwise-min change would be dead code.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/deckard-qwen35-qmoe-export.md -->

# Qwen3.5-MoE fused QMoE export (Deckard) — decision note

**Scope:** mobius export change only (systems/export). No onnx-genai product code
touched. Author: Deckard. Date: 2026-08-03.

## Decision

Qwen3.5/3.6-MoE now emits a fused `com.microsoft::QMoE` node per MoE layer when
the int4 block-quant scheme matches the QMoE ABI (GPTQ / AWQ / **Olive** blk32
int4), instead of the `dense_fallback` per-expert `MatMulNBits` + `Equal`/
`ReduceSum` masking. This unblocks the CUDA QMoE sparse top-k=8 decode (already
implemented in the kernel) — no onnx-genai change required (per Cohaagen's
design, work item (b) is not needed).

## mobius PR

**onnxruntime/mobius#451** — branch `squad/qwen35-qmoe-export`. NOT merged
(Justin merges mobius). Files:
- `src/mobius/components/_moe.py` — `_supported_qmoe_quantization` accepts
  `quant_method="olive"`; requires pow2 `block_size>=16` (CUDA constraint).
- `src/mobius/_weight_utils.py` — `preprocess_olive_weights` preserves leading
  dims so fused expert-major `.qweight` `[E,N,packed_K]` repacks for QMoE
  (2-D linears unchanged, byte-identical).
- `src/mobius/models/qwen35.py` — `Qwen35MoECausalLMModel.preprocess_weights`
  guards the per-expert un-fuse behind `use_qmoe`; keeps fused weights and calls
  `pack_qmoe_expert_weights(..., target_moe_path=".mlp")`. Mirrors DeepSeek-V3.
- `src/mobius/models/deepseek.py` — `use_qmoe` now uses the shared
  `_supported_qmoe_quantization` predicate (DRY; no preprocess/graph drift).
- Tests: `_moe_test.py` (+3), `models/qwen35_test.py` (new, 3).

## Correctness

Byte-equivalent to dense_fallback: same `(q-zp)*scale` int4 affine, same
softmax-top-k routing, same SwiGLU (`swiglu_fusion=2`). Pure layout repack.
`test_olive_fused_expert_packing_is_byte_exact_vs_dense` asserts dequant `atol=0`
+ SwiGLU forward parity. 132 targeted tests pass; ruff clean.

## Structural proof (tiny 4-layer, E=16/k=4, blk32 int4 Olive)

`com.microsoft::QMoE`: 4 (was 0). `MatMul`: 37 (was 229). `Equal`: 0 (was 64).
`ReduceSum`: 0 (was 64). `TopK`: 0 (was 4). => 40 MoE layers in real 35B => 40
QMoE nodes, ~20,480 per-expert MatMulNBits eliminated. Census +
`node-census.txt` at `/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts/proof-tiny/`.

## Dense fallback unchanged for other models

`_supported_qmoe_quantization` still returns `None` for every non-{gptq,awq,olive}
method and for float/non-pow2-block configs → identical dense fallback. The
olive reshape change is a no-op for 2-D linears (existing olive models). deepseek
and other MoE families keep their exact QMoE behavior (now via the shared
predicate). `_weight_utils_test.py`, `deepseek_test.py`, `_qwen35_mtp_test.py`
all green.

## BLOCKER for full 35B regen (needs a compute step, not a code change)

The existing dense artifact (`/home/justinchu/qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts/`) was
built via mobius **float** export -> Olive ONNX-graph `onnxkquantquantization`
(q4_k_m, blk32) — pipeline (B). Recipe: `.olive-cache/default_workflow/
olive_config.json` in `/home/justinchu/mary-mobius` (input =
`qwen3.6-35b-a3b-f16-cuda/decoder/model.onnx`).

This PR implements the mobius **olive-import** QMoE path — pipeline (A),
`quant_method="olive"` with pre-packed HF `.qweight`. To regenerate the 35B with
QMoE you need ONE of:
1. **(recommended)** An Olive-RTN-quantized Qwen3.6-35B **HF checkpoint** (fused
   expert `experts.gate_up_proj.qweight/.scales/.qzeros`, `quantization_config.
   quant_method="olive"`, blk32 int4), then `mobius` export -> emits 40 QMoE.
   Only the float bf16 source is on disk
   (`~/.cache/huggingface/hub/models--Qwen--Qwen3.6-35B-A3B`), so this needs an
   offline RTN weight-quant pass on the 35B (heavy, ~70GB float; out of this
   worker's budget).
2. A graph-level fusion pass that rewrites the pipeline-(B) per-expert
   `MatMulNBits` storm into a QMoE node post-Olive (separate, larger change; not
   in this PR's scope).

Tiny proof + byte-exact tests validate the path end-to-end. Full 35B regen is a
compute/recipe step a measurement pass can pick up once an olive checkpoint
exists.

## Handoff to measurement/review

- Measure native decode ms/tok on a real QMoE 35B artifact vs the 405 ms dense
  baseline **on an origin/main GPU worktree** (local main is stale — do not trust
  local perf), and byte-exact vs the dense artifact's CPU oracle.
- Route mobius#451 review.

---

## Follow-up (same PR #451): dense_fallback → QMoE graph rewrite on the EXISTING artifact

Item 2 above ("graph-level fusion pass") is now **done** and is the way we get a
measured 35B number **without** the 70GB Olive re-quant.

### `mobius.rewrite_rules.fuse_dense_moe_to_qmoe` (`rewrite_rules/_qmoe_fusion.py`)

Topology-driven ONNX-to-ONNX pass, anchored on each MoE `TopK`. Per layer it:
- resolves the router gate `MatMulNBits` (through an optional `Cast`), the hidden
  input, the 256×3 expert `MatMulNBits`, the `Equal/Cast/Mul/ReduceSum` mask, the
  left-folded routed-sum `Add`, and the shared expert;
- builds one `com.microsoft::QMoE` node reusing the int4 weights **byte-for-byte**
  (`fc1 = concat(flatten(gate), flatten(up))`, `fc2 = flatten(down)`, stacked
  expert-major; zero-points copied unchanged; `float16` scales upcast to the
  `float32` the kernel requires — lossless). Router logits `Cast`→float32 →
  `router_probs`, `normalize_routing_weights=1`; attrs
  `swiglu / swiglu_fusion=2 / k=8 / bits=4 / block_size=32`;
- keeps the shared expert dense and rewires `Add(routed, shared)` onto the QMoE
  output; DCEs the dead dense-routing nodes + unused initializers.

Byte mapping verified as **identity** against the CUDA kernel
(`onnx-genai/crates/onnx-runtime-ep-cuda/src/kernels/qmoe.rs`): same
low-nibble-first, K-contiguous int4 packing; kernel requires `float32`
`scales`+`router_probs` (qmoe.rs:927, :1050).

### Real 35B artifact (A/B, no requant)

Applied to `qwen36-35b-a3b-artifacts/{decoder,merged}/model.onnx` →
`qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts/` (dense artifact untouched; sidecars +
`inference_metadata.yaml` copied, `embedding/` hardlinked). The pipeline uses
`decoder/` + `embedding/`; `merged/` rewritten too for completeness (it inserts a
`Cast` between the gate `MatMulNBits`↔`TopK` and `TopK`↔`Softmax`, which the pass
handles).

Node census:

| graph   | nodes            | MatMulNBits   | TopK   | QMoE      |
|---------|------------------|---------------|--------|-----------|
| decoder | 125,355 → 2,259  | 31,111 → 391  | 40 → 0 | 0 → **40** |
| merged  | 125,436 → 2,300  | 31,111 → 391  | 40 → 0 | 0 → **40** |

`MatMulNBits` drop = `40×256×3 = 30,720` (exactly the expert storm); the 391
remaining = router gate + shared-expert projections. Graph integrity re-checked
(0 dangling inputs, 0 missing outputs, 0 topo violations). Data grew 19.78→21.79
GB from the lossless fp16→fp32 scale upcast (~2 GB). Byte-exactness re-verified on
real experts (layer 0, experts 0/1/127/255): fc1/fc2 weights + zero-points
bit-identical, scales fp16→fp32 lossless.

Reproducer (env-specific onnx_ir hardlink/mmap workaround, not committed):
`onnx-genai/.scratch/apply_qmoe_rewrite.py`.

### Correctness status

- Rewrite unit tests (6) green; ruff clean; full `rewrite_rules/` suite green.
- Provably a pure layout transform (bit-identical weights/zp, lossless scales) —
  mathematically byte-equivalent to dense_fallback.

### Handoff to measurement/review

- Measure native decode ms/tok on `qwen36-35b-a3b-qmoe-artifacts/decoder` vs the
  405 ms dense baseline **on an origin/main GPU worktree**, and byte-exact vs the
  dense artifact's CPU oracle.
- Route mobius#451 review (now includes both the export guard and the graph
  rewrite as one reviewable unit).

### 2026-08-04: PR #625 re-review — APPROVE (MAJOR resolved)

**By:** Harry

**What:** Re-reviewed the delta 70abc750...3b615953 on `squad/native-loader-skip-ort` (fix by Quaid; cohaagen locked out). The only production change is in `graph_io_from_model_path` (loader.rs): it now collects `graph.initializer` names into a `HashSet` and filters them out of `graph.input` before building `GraphIoMetadata.inputs`. This mirrors `onnx-runtime-loader::graph_builder` §2 (graph_builder.rs:114-126) and ORT `Session` GetInputCount semantics exactly — order preserved, only-initializer / only-input / both cases all handled. New test `graph_io_metadata_excludes_initializers_and_matches_session_geometry` authors one ORT-loadable model with a weight in both graph.input and graph.initializer plus rank-4 fp16 KV, and cross-checks `session.inputs()==metadata.inputs()` / outputs byte-for-byte (TensorInfo: PartialEq) plus explicit fp16 dtype and `[-1,2,-1,4]` geometry asserts — a strong, non-stub proof. No regression: for models without input/initializer overlap the filter is a no-op, so all previously-approved paths are unchanged.

**Why:** My prior [MAJOR] rejection is fully and correctly resolved with a general, no-hardcoding fix and rigorous parity coverage. All earlier "Verified correct" items (ORT |_| true byte-preservation, native |_| false generality keyed on backend==Native, trait soundness, KV dtype-from-caller not graph) remain untouched by this delta. Verdict flips to APPROVE.

<!-- source: .squad/decisions/inbox/harry-625-loader-review.md -->

### 2026-08-04: PR #625 native-loader-skip-ort review — REJECT (1 MAJOR)

**By:** Harry

**What:** Independent review of PR #625 (`squad/native-loader-skip-ort`, HEAD 70abc750). The loader refactor (backend-neutral `GraphIo` trait + session-free `GraphIoMetadata`, `load_with_ort_session_filter`, `graph_io(component)`, native branch skipping every ORT session via `|_| false`) is sound and well-documented, and the ORT `|_| true` path is behaviour-preserving. Verdict is REJECT on one correctness/parity gap: `graph_io_from_model_path` (loader.rs:451-460) maps **all** of `graph.input` to `TensorInfo` without excluding initializer-backed inputs. Both ORT and this repo's own native loader (`crates/onnx-runtime-loader/src/graph_builder.rs:114-126`) treat "names that are also initializers" as constants, NOT real graph inputs. For any model that lists initializers in `graph.input` (all pre-IR-4 exports; legal in IR>=4), `GraphIoMetadata.inputs()` leaks weight tensors, which can falsely trip `resolved_io::from_structure` (resolved_io.rs:373 — bails on any float rank>=3 input) and mis-route in `build_step_bindings`. Modern genai/QMoE exports don't list initializers, so the specific target works, but the general parity guarantee (claim #1) is broken.

**Why:** Fix is one-line and clearly correct: collect `graph.initializer` names into a set and skip matching `graph.input` entries before mapping, mirroring graph_builder.rs. Justin explicitly wants a general, no-hardcoding seam on fairness/perf-critical mainline, so a legal ONNX construct the codebase already handles elsewhere must not silently corrupt the decode contract. Non-blocking follow-ups: (a) no test asserts GraphIoMetadata yields identical KV geometry/dtype vs a real Session; (b) `value_info_to_tensor_info` hard-errors on a graph output with no declared type (legal-but-rare) — acceptable as fail-loud but worth a note.

### 2026-08-02: Qwen2.5-1.5B benchmark-prompt divergence adjudicated native-correct
**By:** Cohaagen
**What:** Adjudicated the Foundry sweep divergence for `Microsoft/qwen2.5-1.5b-instruct-cuda-gpu-4/v4` with prompt `Hello` (token `[9707]`). Release `profile_native` reproduced the default greedy split deterministically: native and ORT agree for generated indices `0..25`, then first diverge at 0-based generated index `26`; native emits token `1909` (`" top"`) and ORT-CUDA emits token `821` (`" data"`).

**Evidence:** Built an accuracy-level-1 oracle directory with `python3 scripts/qwen_q4_f32_oracle.py --case qwen2.5-1.5b --rewrite-acc1-dir target/qwen15b-bench-acc1` (141 `MatMulNBits` nodes rewritten to fp32 activation accumulation). At index 26, with `top_logprobs` enabled for logit/logprob inspection:

| path | selected | logprob(1909) | logprob(821) | margin 1909-821 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| ORT CPU acc-level-1 fp32 oracle | 1909 | -2.7209473 | -2.7365723 | +0.015625 |
| ORT CUDA acc-4 logits/host argmax | 1909 | -2.7209473 | -2.7365723 | +0.015625 |
| native CUDA logits | 1909 | -2.70755 | -2.754425 | +0.046875 |

The default release ORT-CUDA greedy path (without `top_logprobs`) is the path that emitted `821`; requesting logprobs bypasses that device greedy fast path and exposes logits/host argmax that agree with the fp32 oracle. Native default greedy and native logits both stay on token `1909`.

**Verdict:** Case (a): native matches the fp32 oracle; the benchmark divergence is an ORT-CUDA acc-4/default-greedy near-tie on the lower-precision side, not a native bug. No product-code fix is needed. Added a real-model ignored regression lock in `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/qwen2_5_1_5b_divergence.rs` for the benchmark prompt, alongside the existing France-prompt lock.

**Validation:**
- `cargo fmt --all`
- `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1 ONNX_GENAI_QWEN15B_CUDA_DIR=$HOME/.foundry/cache/models/Microsoft/qwen2.5-1.5b-instruct-cuda-gpu-4/v4 ONNX_GENAI_QWEN15B_ACC1_DIR=/home/justinchu/wt-cohaagen-1p5b/target/qwen15b-bench-acc1 cargo test -p onnx-genai-engine --features native-backend,cuda --test qwen2_5_1_5b_divergence -- --ignored --nocapture` (2 passed)

### 2026-08-06: cohaagen-async-pagein-opt-in
**By:** Unknown
**What:** # Decision: weight-offload async page-in → opt-IN (default sync)

- Date: 2026-08-01
- By: Cohaagen (EP/runtime)
- Lineage: #544 (weight-offload) follow-up; supersedes the async-default-ON introduced with the async fence-ordered page-in (issue #87 infra).
- Branch/PR: `fix/weight-offload-async-pagein-opt-in`

## Decision

Invert the default of `ONNX_GENAI_WEIGHT_OFFLOAD_ASYNC_PAGEIN`:

- **Before:** async page-in default-ON; only `=0` opted out.
- **After:** async page-in **opt-IN**. Unset / falsey → synchronous page-in (new default). A truthy value (`1`/`true`/`yes`/`on`, case/whitespace-insensitive) opts into async.

Only the DEFAULT changes. The async fence-ordered page-in path is fully preserved behind the flag (it is the A/B "after" arm and the anti-regression fence test still guards it).

## Why — measured A/B (the data)

qwen3-0.6b-int4, native CUDA, weight-offload engaged, eviction/thrash regime (small device budget so every admit evicts):

| Config (device budget 96 MiB) | tok/s |
|---|---|
| async page-in ON  | 12.16 |
| async page-in OFF (sync) | **15.84** |

Sync is **~1.30× faster** in the eviction regime. Async net-regresses and is never a clear win on any on-hand model.

Per-page-in tax breakdown (96 MiB, async), from the inc-2 instrumented probe:

| term | ms |
|---|---|
| materialize (int4 canonical-bytes staging/dequant prep) | 791 |
| pinned host-buffer alloc + copy | 792 |
| raw H2D copy | 46 |
| eviction compute-stream drain | 15 |
| fence wait | 7 |

The transfer (H2D) the async path tries to overlap is ~3% of the cost. Materialize + pinned-staging co-dominate (~48% each) and sit on the critical path; when every admit evicts, the eviction drain re-serializes, so async cannot overlap anything and only adds the non-overlappable pinned-staging alloc. Async becomes a net win only once a warm-host materialize cache keeps pinned canonical bytes warm (removes both dominant terms from the per-page-in critical path).

## Correctness / blast radius

- **Byte-exact:** offloaded == resident token stream is unchanged (weight_paging §9 invariant). Verified on qwen3-0.6b-int4 native CUDA e2e with the NEW sync default: tokens byte-identical to resident baseline; page_ins=12544, evictions=12541 (non-vacuous).
- WAR / eviction-drain safety and fence-ordering primitives are UNTOUCHED. The async fence anti-regression GPU test (`async_pagein_fence_orders_weight_page_in_consumer`) still passes — it drives the async primitives directly, independent of the default.
- Capture interaction: none. Dynamic page-in is outside any captured region (already established); this flag doesn't touch #571 capture.
- Blast radius = flag default + tests + docs only. Pager internals, capture, and GAP-3 untouched.

## Tests / evidence

- Unit (`weight_paging::tests::async_pagein_env_is_opt_in`): `None→false`, `1/true/YES/ On →true`, `0/false/""/maybe→false` (non-vacuous both directions).
- `device_policy_defaults_to_disabled` extended to assert `!policy.async_pagein` (default sync).
- e2e `weight_offload_native_cuda_e2e`: asserts the resolved `from_env()` policy is sync by default AND offloaded tokens == resident (byte-identical) on real fp16/int4 GPU.
- Async fence GPU test unchanged and passing (guards the async path when opted in).

## Escape hatch

Async stays available via `ONNX_GENAI_WEIGHT_OFFLOAD_ASYNC_PAGEIN=1`. Revisit the default once a warm-host materialize cache lands — at that point async should overlap and may flip back to a net win.
**Why:** Preserved from decision inbox drop `cohaagen-async-pagein-opt-in.md`.

### 2026-08-06: cohaagen-autoderive-io
**By:** Unknown
**What:** # Auto-derive `model.io` (kv + recurrent state_pairs) from graph ports

Date: 2026-08-01
By: Cohaagen
Lane: #384 recurrent-state / native decode loader
Status: PR opened (S increment)

## What this unlocks

Stock qwen3.x **linear-attention hybrid** exports (16 dense GQA layers + 48
recurrent `conv_state`/`recurrent_state` layers with a `Scan` body), including
Justin's named **qwen3.6-27b-int4**, now load + decode **natively** with **no
hand-written `io:` overlay** — the loader derives the decoder I/O topology from
the ONNX graph ports.

Before this, the stock export (whose `inference_metadata.yaml` carries no `io`
block) failed native load at token-input resolution:

```
cannot resolve model.io.token_input from tensor shape because 3 ports match:
[input_ids, attention_mask, position_ids]
```

The recurrent hybrids also declare NO dense KV for the linear layers, so shape
inference cannot classify their `conv_state`/`recurrent_state` running-state
ports. During the #384 re-probe I proved a hand overlay (states in
`io.state_pairs`) makes it load + decode byte-identically to the CPU fp32
oracle. This PR removes the overlay requirement.

## The change (blast radius: loader fallback + genai-config helper + tests + docs)

1. **genai-config** (`compatibility.rs`): extracted the pure, config-free core of
   `strict_decoder_state` into `decoder_state_from_patterns(graph, past_key,
   past_value, present_key, present_value, shape_match)`. Added a new public
   `GenAiConfig::derive_decoder_io_from_graph(graph) -> Option<DerivedDecoderIo>`
   that calls it with the conventional onnxruntime-genai patterns
   (`past_key_values.%d.key`/`.value` → `present.%d.key`/`.value`). This is TRUE
   reuse of the guarded derivation — recurrent ports are found by
   `suffix_tensor_map` (never `strict_indexed_kv`), so cross-attention/Whisper KV
   is never misclassified as running state.

2. **One relaxation, isolated to the fallback:** a `StateShapeMatch` param.
   `strict_decoder_state` passes `Exact` (byte-for-byte unchanged). The new
   fallback passes `AllowSymbolic`: a `present.*` state port whose exported shape
   is fully symbolic (`[?,?,?]`) is accepted against its concrete `past_*` input
   (`[?,10240,3]`). This was REQUIRED — the real 27b export leaves present
   recurrent-state shapes fully symbolic, and `Exact` rejected them. A symbolic
   axis means "unknown", not "different"; concrete-vs-concrete mismatch still
   fails.

3. **Engine loader** (`native_decode/load.rs`): in
   `from_session_with_cuda_options_and_io`, when `io.is_none()`, build a
   `ModelGraphInfo` from the session ports and call the helper. **Safety gate:**
   engage the derived spec ONLY when it yields ≥1 recurrent state pair (the case
   shape inference can't handle). Pure-dense decoders derive 0 state pairs → the
   fallback declines → their existing `io=None` path is untouched. Declared `io`
   always wins (fallback runs only when `io` is absent).

## Correctness bar

- **GPU e2e** (`native_autoderive_io_cuda_e2e.rs`, `#[ignore]`): STOCK 27b
  metadata (no overlay) → native CUDA load + decode == native CPU fp32 oracle,
  **byte-identical** token IDs. Proven parity token IDs:
  `[11751, 13, 271, 248068, 271, 248069, 271, 4639, 369, 4252, 13, 11751, ...]`.
- **Unit tests** (genai-config): 27b port layout → 32 kv entries + 96 state
  pairs; qwen3-0.6b dense layout → dense kv + **0** state pairs (gate doesn't
  over-derive). Non-vacuous both directions. The 27b fixture uses fully-symbolic
  present-state shapes to lock the `AllowSymbolic` path.
- **No-regression** (engine): a hybrid decoder with `io=None` now auto-derives
  and loads; a dense ambiguous decoder with `io=None` still fails with the same
  `model.io.token_input` error (fallback declines). Existing declared-io tests
  unchanged and green.

## Honest caveats

- **Eager only, ~5.91 tok/s.** The `Scan` control-flow body declines CUDA-graph
  capture, so these hybrids run eager. Capturing the Scan body is a separate,
  parked perf lane; this PR is a **correctness** unblock, not a perf win.
- **CPU is the oracle, not ORT.** ORT-CUDA crashes on this model class (internal
  `stl_vector` assertion), so there is no ORT reference to compare against; the
  native CPU fp32 path (which already threads recurrent state) is the oracle.
- Follow-up (separate): is the ORT-CUDA crash a mobius export bug or an ORT
  limitation? Affects whether we ever get an ORT baseline for this class.
**Why:** Preserved from decision inbox drop `cohaagen-autoderive-io.md`.

### 2026-08-06: cohaagen-capture-default-on
**By:** Unknown
**What:** # Decision — native decoder step-inputs CUDA-graph capture is now DEFAULT-ON

**Author:** Cohaagen (EP/runtime)  ·  **Branch:** `feat/native-decode-capture-default-on`
**Reviewer:** Harry (independent; author locked out)  ·  **Authority:** Justin — "确保高性能"
**Lineage:** Inc3c / issue #384 (the captured step-inputs path); scope note
`cohaagen-perfcap-scope.md` (WIRING-GAP verdict).

## What changed

The captured per-step-input decode path for **multi-component / routed** native
CUDA decoders (`inputs_embeds` + routed ports, e.g. gemma-3n / gemma4-e2b and the
GQA layers of the 35B-A3B class) is now **on by default**. Previously it was
gated OFF behind an opt-in env flag, so every routed decode step ran the eager
owned-input path and forfeited CUDA-graph capture (the root cause of the ~6.5×
gemma4-e2b native-vs-ORT decode gap).

The env var `ONNX_GENAI_NATIVE_DECODER_CAPTURE_STEP_INPUTS` is inverted from
opt-in to **opt-out**:
- unset / truthy / unrecognized → capture-on (default);
- `0` / `false` / `no` / `off` → force the eager owned path (escape hatch).

This only affects the **step-inputs (multi-component/routed)** path. Monolithic
single-component decoders (qwen2.5/qwen3) already captured by default via the
token-id `run_one_token` path — unchanged. No capture-core / `plan_capture_region`
/ `standard_attention` / GAP-3 KV edits.

## Files

- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/native_decode/cuda.rs`
  - `capture_step_inputs_enabled()` inverted to default-on; parsing split into a
    pure, unit-testable `capture_step_inputs_from_env_value(Option<&str>)`.
  - Comments at the `decode_cuda` gate (~:401) and the `capture_step_inputs`
    construction (~:1405) updated to describe default-on + the structural gates.
  - New `capture_step_inputs_gate_tests` unit module (default-on, opt-out falsy
    set, truthy/unknown stay on).
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/native_cuda_captured_step_inputs_parity.rs`
  - Asserts the **default** (no env) engages capture (`captured_decodes>0`) and is
    byte-identical to the `=0` opt-out eager baseline; explicit `=1` still on.
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/gemma3n_native_cuda_capture_realmodel.rs`
  - `run(dir, capture)`: `true` = default (env unset), `false` = `=0` opt-out.
    Asserts token parity + default engages + opt-out declines.
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/tests/qwen3_0_6b_capture_step_inputs_decline.rs`
  - Now proves the single-component decoder declines under the **default** (no env),
    i.e. default-on never mis-engages an ineligible decoder.
- `docs/CUDA_GRAPH_CAPTURE.md` — new "Multi-component / routed decoders" section
  documenting default-on + the opt-out env semantics + structural decline.

## Correctness evidence (GPU 2, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=2)

- `capture_step_inputs_gate_tests` (3 unit tests) — PASS.
- `native_cuda_captured_step_inputs_parity` (`--test-threads=1`) — PASS:
  `tokens=[0,5,6,7] default_captured_decodes=3 opt_in_captured_decodes=3
  opt_out_captured_decodes=0`. Byte-identical tokens across default / opt-in /
  opt-out; the counter proves default-on genuinely engages (non-vacuous) and the
  `=0` opt-out genuinely falls back to eager.
- `cargo fmt --all --check` — clean. Real-model `--ignored` tests compile.

**Regression safety (the whole point):** default-on output is byte-identical to
the eager path on a real capture-eligible GQA decoder fixture; ineligible
decoders auto-decline via the unchanged structural gates (`graph_enabled`,
non-empty `captured_step_inputs`) — no silent-wrong. The `=0` opt-out is a
